    Still, it's the only reason she slips out of the house before he's even awake on Monday morning and arrives at L-Corp just moments before her team does. Winn is on the ball, of course, directing their interns to construct the scaffolding and hang the tarps.

    In the mean time, Lucy helps Kara mix paint, setting up her paintbrushes and helping her measure out and block the entire white wall.

    It's laborious, but the planning is the most important part. The last thing she wants is to get the proportions wrong and end up reaching Autumn before time. The thought, itself, brings a smile to her face, and Lucy smiles in return.

    Kara feels guilty, of course, because these interns came to her gallery to learn from her and she knows she's neglected them. Never mind the fact Lucy is actually her friend.

    She's also lucky that James, a photographer who regularly displays pieces in the gallery and goes by Guardian, has stepped up to fill a mentor role while she figures out how to navigate life as a widow.

    It's already past noon by the time the scaffolding is up and the entire space has been closed off. Her supplies are tucked away inside, ladders set up for her and paints just waiting to be used.

    Instead of getting started, she treats her team to coffee and donuts, and then delivers some to Alex at work, calling ahead to make sure she has a few minutes to step outside.

    Alex says, "I think I could get used to this," and Kara would have to agree.

    It's when she's getting back to L-Corp that her phone rings and she answers to Eliza telling her that Carter managed to get off to camp just fine. Apparently, he was a little mopey, but Kara did promise him a trip into the city on Friday after his last day of camp lets out at eleven o'clock, and both he and Alex are looking forward to it.

    Kara is just stepping into the foyer when Lena is making her way out, trailed by a woman and a man who are listening to every word she says. Lena almost plows right into her, and she easily dodges before someone ends up breaking bones.

    "Oh, Kara," Lena says. "Sorry, I didn't see you there, we're in a bit of a crisis."

    "I'm sorry to hear that," Kara says, tipping an invisible hat. "I'd offer my help, but I'm but a lowly artist."

    Lena, at least, looks amused by Kara's slowly making a fool of herself. "I was going to stop by to say hello when I got in this morning, but you all looked so busy that I didn't want to be a bother."

    Kara waves away that notion. "You'd never be a bother," she says. "You should stop by whenever you want."

    "Right, I - thank you."

    Kara uses the silence that follows to take her in, all pristine and perfect. She's literal art. There's really no other way to describe her, and Kara's fingers twitch with the desire to sketch and paint and emulate what's before her eyes.

    Lena stares right back until the woman with her clears her throat and quietly says, "Miss Luthor, our crisis."

    "Oh, right, of course, sorry," Lena says, shaking her head at herself. "Um, Kara, this is Jess, my assistant, and this is Brainy, my head of R&D."

    Kara smiles at them both. "Nice to meet you."

    Jess offers a flash of a smile in response, already keen to get her boss moving, and Brainy tips his head in greeting. Wow. Kara does not miss this part of the assistant's job, so she takes a purposeful step back, allowing Lena the opportunity to leave.

    Only, Lena doesn't move. Instead, she asks, "How's it coming along?" about the piece.

    "I'm just about to start," Kara tells her. "I had to make sure the proportions were correct before we introduce the colour."

    "Is it just you?"

    "Just me," Kara says, and she's actually rather excited about getting to work.

    "Then, we should leave you to it," Jess interjects. "Miss Luthor, please."

    Lena nods, absently, and then allows Jess to direct her away. "Perhaps I'll see you later," Lena manages to say.

    "I'll be here," Kara calls out, watching in amusement as Jess almost has to manhandle Lena to get her moving in the direction she needs her to.

    It is a crazy, wild thing that Kara doesn't stop smiling.

    Not once.

    She's smiling as she ducks into her workspace and throws on her smock. She's smiling when she collects her brushes and summer colours. She's smiling when she paints her first strokes.

    And she's smiling when she realises the smile on her face is actually a new one.

    Lena can feel Jess' eyes on her as the afternoon progresses. Crisis is easily averted just by showing her face and using her brain, but it's really something that Lena would rather have stood and talked to Kara than deal with whatever mess her researchers have made.

    So, Jess is looking at her in a particular way, and Lena isn't going to be the one to bring it up.

    It's just that, when she gets back to her office, there's a box of donuts sitting on Jess' desk with a little note that says, Jess, Lena and Brainy, I hope you managed to handle your crisis - Kara, and Lena stands perfectly still in front of Jess' desk and stares.

    Jess walks around her own desk and sets her tablet on her desk. She doesn't sit; just rests her hands on the back of her chair and waits for some kind of instruction. Maybe an explanation.

    Lena offers neither; just spins on her heel and disappears onto an elevator without another word. It's such an odd thing, this feeling in her chest.

    Jack has sent her almost fifty bouquets of flowers just in the last few months, and none of them have affected her the same way a stupid box of donuts has. Lena has so much work to do, some of it damage control, but the only thing on her mind is seeing Kara and thanking her. Just seeing her, really.

    Except, the woman is hidden behind her scaffolding, and Lena has to talk herself into poking her head inside past the 'Do Not Enter' sign, hoping she doesn't interrupt anything important.

    Kara immediately notices her, turning her head away from what she's doing. She's midway up a tall ladder, paintbrush in hand, and Lena sees nothing other than just her.

    Especially when Kara beams - absolutely beams - at her. "Hey," she says, quickly abandoning her work and descending the ladder. "I find I'm caught between knowing you have to see the progress but also wanting the entire thing to remain a surprise."

    "I'm not even looking," Lena assures her, and can barely handle it when Kara is standing right in front of her. She's practically bouncing on her feet, hair in a messy ponytail. There's paint on her chin and her left cheek, all over her hands and on the odd apron thing she's wearing.

    "You can, if you want to," Kara offers.

    "No, I just - we wanted to thank you for the donuts," Lena says. "We definitely appreciate it."

    "Of course," Kara says. "Did you manage to sort out your crisis?"

    "We did, yes," she says. "Just a bit of human error, unfortunately, but easily fixable."

    "Because you were on the case, I bet."

    Lena doesn't blush. She absolutely does not. "Anyway, I just thought I'd come down to thank you," she says. "And make sure you're not too lonely in your little bat cave here."

    Kara laughs, running a distracted hand over her hair and leaving a few specks of paint in its wake. "I - can I tell you a secret?"

    "Anything." The word comes out too breathy; with too much meaning, but Kara thankfully doesn't seem to notice.

    "I was super nervous," Kara says, looking away. "Terrified, really, that I wouldn't remember - that it would start out bad, because it's been so long since - it's just been a while since I've done this kind of work." She's blushing as she finishes off, and Lena feels the danger for what it is. Especially when Kara's brow creases with some realisation. "I feel good," she whispers. "It feels good."

    To Lena, it appears to be some profound realisation, because Kara suddenly looks so, so sad. Eyes filled with the kind of grief Lena would wish on nobody, but she forces it away after a moment, eyes looking to the ceiling for a short moment.

    "It feels good," Kara says again. "To be painting again; to be doing what I love."

    "I'm glad to hear that, Kara," Lena says, because she doesn't know what else to say.

    "Thank you, Lena," Kara says, and then groans loudly. "Ugh, now I'm going to have to buy Alex so much pizza." She looks positively miserable about it, and Lena can be only amused.

    Though, when she finally leaves Kara to get back to work, she's anything but amused. The smile remains, but her heart is thundering in her chest in a way nobody could quite tell unless they had super hearing.

    Lena holds it together well as she arrives on her floor and walks straight past Jess' desk. The box of donuts is still there, untouched, and Lena directs her feet towards Sam's office. It's an odd time in the day, and she expects Sam to be busy.

    Lena walks into her office anyway, closes the door and says, "Sam, what have you done?"

    Sam looks panicked immediately, but Lena doesn't quite register it for what it could be. "Lena, I'm sorry," she says, abandoning her work and getting to her feet. "I just - I was trying to help, and you said you wouldn't write them yourself, so I just - "

    Lena's eyes snap towards her. "What are you talking about?"

    Sam freezes. "Um. What are you talking about?"

    "Kara." Lena's head tilts. "I'm talking about Kara. What are you talking about?"

    Sam blinks, as if coming back online. "Nothing," she squeaks. "I mean, some random documents."

    Lena waves off her weirdness because she's experiencing her second crisis of the day. She feels so young; off-kilter just because a pretty woman has been smiling at her. She's even tingling in places. Something is happening.

    Sam clears her throat, settling into her desk chair once more. "What about Kara?" she asks. "Did you see the art piece? Is it terrible?"

    Lena moves to sit in one of the chairs opposite her, fingers trembling slightly. "There is a problem."

    "With Kara?" There's a frown in her voice. "Did she say something mean to you about being a Luthor, because I had to give Alex a stern word when I told her I work here?"

    "What? No, nothing like that," Lena says, shaking her head. "If anything, she's been extremely kind."

    Maybe there's a certain inflection in her voice, because Sam catches onto it rather quickly, her expression shifting to something like delight. "Oh, my God," she says. "You like her."

    There is no point in denying it, so she says, "She's married," and Sam's face falls into confusion.

    "Um, no she's not."

    "She definitely is," Lena says. "I've seen her rings, Sam."

    Sam frowns. "No, Lena," she says. "Alex definitely would have mentioned that. She hasn't said a word about a spouse."

    "Sam."

    "Lena, I'm telling you - "

    "She also has a child," Lena adds, and Sam shuts up. "I don't even know what's happening to me, to be honest. It's so new. Different. I'm not certain I like it. Actually, I'm pretty sure I hate it. I mean, I made a complete fool of myself earlier, because I didn't want to stop talking to her, and I - what is that?"

    "You like her," Sam says, as if it explains everything.

    "I don't even know her," Lena says, which is true. She knows next to nothing about this woman except the way her eyes light up when she talks about her art, or the way she looks as if she's constantly amused about something; some secret only she knows.

    "Lena."

    "What?"

    "Are you sure you know what you know?" Sam asks. "Because you might be wrong. Alex hasn't told me about any child in their lives, which is odd when I've mentioned Ruby, you know. So, I think - "

    "No," Lena says. "It's better this way."

    "Lena."

    It's simpler to say it than act it, but Lena knows the best thing to do is leave things as they are. Perhaps even avoid Kara. Yes. That sounds like the perfect plan. At least until she can figure out just what's going on with her.

    "Lena," Sam says again, but Lena has already decided.

    It's really for the best.

    Carter catches Kara in the middle of a yawn when he comes scrambling into the living room and climbs right into her lap. It isn't common for him to seek touch this way, so she allows him to shift on her until he's comfortable, noticing the piece of paper in his hand only when he shows it to her.

    She's borderline exhausted after an early start and almost six hours of nearly nonstop painting. Her spine aches a little, but she'll happily take cuddles from Carter.

    "Mommy," he says, and there's a certain wonder in his voice Kara has rarely heard. "She wrote again."

    "Excuse me?"

    "L," he says, and very gently places the letter into her hands. "She wrote to us again."

    Kara knows Carter has latched onto this L woman for reasons she can understand. If she's being honest, she didn't think they would ever hear from her again, but she's immensely relieved they have. She keeps the first letter in her nightstand, finding comfort in being able to reach for it and read the words over and over again, as if that'll help her believe them.

    Maybe L has even left a way for them to contact her and thank her in this one.

    Carter shifts again until he's comfortably in position for the two of them to read together. He's obviously already read it, but there's something about L that lets Kara know this letter isn't solely for her.

    Would you look at that.

    Dear Carter's Mom,

    Not to overstep or anything, but I hope it's all right that I've sent Carter a radio. It's one of the older ones I keep in my laboratory - yes, I have an actual laboratory, I'm that cool - and I thought he would appreciate it.

    I remember being his age and being interested in how things work. It wasn't anything my own mother encouraged, but my older brother allowed me to watch him work and tinker with a few things. Our relationships are even more strained now, but they remain good memories for me, and I think that's what we all deserve. Especially the two of you.

    It's what your story has taught me, actually. I was just talking to my one friend about life, in general, and we've learned that life is short and unexpected, and we have to make the good memories so we can hold onto them for forever.

    Besides sending the radio, I also wanted to tell you that it's okay to move on. I think you need to hear it, even from a stranger. It is okay to keep going and grow and love life and try to be happy. It isn't an insult to your wife's memory and you have nothing to feel guilty about.

    There is no timeline on grief, of course, but it's okay to get back to things you love; the people you love. It's okay to get back to the life you were always meant to live, with and without her. Something tells me you'll need someone to keep telling you that, so I hereby apply for the job. I'll tell you as many times as you need to hear it.

    We're getting close to Christmas, so I thought I'd take the opportunity to wish you, Carter and the rest of your family a merry one. I hope the holiday isn't too hard for you, but know that I'm thinking of you both.

    Please take care,
     L

    Now.

    Kara is generally an emotional person. She wears her heart on her sleeve, feeling things to a level that she generally can't hide them.

    Carter twists in her lap and says, "You're supposed to be smiling," with confusion. "Why are you crying?"

    She didn't even realise she was, and she uses her free hand to wipe her cheeks. "She sent you a radio?" she deflects.

    Carter's brow is furrowed, but he nods slowly. "It's upstairs," he tells her. "Do you want to see it? It's so cool."

    Kara nods, because she was always going to, and then allows him to drag her up to his bedroom as if she wouldn't follow him anywhere and everywhere.

    Jess tells her that Jack is expected back in National City at the end of the week, and he would like to see her, when Lena comes in on Tuesday morning, and she decides to focus on only that and not on the fact Kara is currently at work downstairs.

    It's both easier and more difficult than she thought. Active thinking is on only work and Jack, but her passive mind continually drifts to a crinkled brow and twinkling blue eyes.

    Lena's avoidance of Kara is successful, and Sam tells her during a short lunch break that Alex was very shady about confirming that Kara had a family. "She seemed reluctant to offer any information," Sam says, frowning. "But, yes, she definitely has a nephew."

    Wednesday is another feat in itself, her feet carrying her past Kara's little world and right to the elevators. She spends the day in meetings and avoiding Jess' curious looks. She eats lunch in her office, and even stays long enough to eat dinner in the same place.

    And listen to Carter's mother's segment, live for once.

    Lena almost expects Sam to join her, but she also knows Sam knows to give her some space to deal with this very tricky position she's now put herself in. So Lena is already tuned in when Nia places the call and, instead of Carter's mother's voice coming on the line, it's Carter's.

    "She's working," he explains, and he sounds so happy about it. "She's been working all week. And smiling. So much smiling."

    "Oh, Carter," Nia says, and she sounds emotional, herself. "That's great news."

    "But that's why she's not home right now," Carter explains. "I think she just forgot she's supposed to talk to you, Nia. Sometimes, she can get so lost in her own world. Mama used to say she would go 'red creative,' and we wouldn't see her for hours. Mama and I would play this game about how she would act when she came out. If she'd be sleepy or want coffee or cuddles." His voice goes soft. "I usually won, because she always wants cuddles."

    Lena leans her head back, eyes closing as she listens.

    "Your Mom likes cuddles, does she?"

    "Sometimes," Carter says. "Sometimes, not. Grandma says we're the same that way, because we have, um, sensory sen - sensi - "

    "Sensitivity?" Nia offers.

    "Uh huh," he says. "It's not always fun."

    "I imagine not," Nia sympathises, just as Lena hears a knock on her office door. Her heart jolts a little, because she's pretty sure everyone's already gone home. Well, they're supposed to have left for the day, and she doesn't really have the time to call out in response when the door opens and Kara - Kara - pops her head inside.

    "Hey," Kara says, and slips her body through the small gap she's made. "I'm glad you're still here."

    Lena is still in a bit of shock as she watches Kara get closer, because it feels like a breath of fresh air to see her in the flesh after just a few days. It's also the reason she doesn't remember to mute her computer in time.

    Because Lena hears Carter say, "It helps to think about what Mama would do if things get confusing," and something very particular happens to Kara's face. Her eyes widen in something like realisation, before they get impossibly wider than that in something that looks like frustration.

    Before Lena can question her, Kara says, "If you'll excuse me a moment," and then stalks out of the office.

    Lena just sits, confused and a little thrown by the current events. She can't even focus on Carter, wondering where Kara's gone and how long she's going to take to come back. If she even will.

    Then Carter says, "Oops," and Lena turns her head. "Grandma's not too happy with me." There are muffled voices. "I have to go to bed now, Nia."

    "Oh, okay, Carter," Nia says. "Goodnight."

    "Goodnight," he squeaks out, and then hangs up.

    Nia lets out a soft laugh. "Well, it seems Carter's mother is unavailable tonight, which is disappointing, but also not," she says. "We would have liked to hear the progress from her, but I'm still glad to hear she's finding joy in her work again."

    There's another knock, and then Kara's back, her face a little flushed. This time, Lena makes sure to mute her computer before she embarrasses herself further.

    "Sorry about that," Kara says, fidgeting nervously. "You - I didn't think you'd listen to something like that."

    Lena didn't, either. "The kid is cute," is how she explains it, and Kara's smile softens.

    "Yeah," she says; "he is."

    There's a certainty in her voice that intrigues Lena, but she ignores it. "Is everything okay?" she asks. "This is a surprise."

    "Oh, right, yes," Kara says, waving a hand. "I guess sorry for just dropping in like this, but I just wanted to check that everything is still okay."

    Lena frowns. "Of course, it is," she says. "Why wouldn't it be?"

    "I - no actual reason," Kara says, awkwardly rubbing a hand against the back of her neck. "It's nothing. I just - I feel as if you've been quiet - that I maybe made things too unprofessional and you're just - yeah."

    Oh.

    Lena curses herself. "No, Kara, no, it's nothing like that," she assures her, though it's not entirely accurate. "I've just been really busy these past few days."

    Kara looks far too relieved for that to be her only worry. "Oh, okay, that's good to know," she says. "Thought you were avoiding me or something."

    "No, of course not." The lie tastes like acid, and she should have known attempting to avoid Kara would backfire. She's just as intrigued by this woman as she was the first day they met, if not more.

    Definitely more.

    Especially when Kara does a slow twirl to get a proper look at the office. "So, this is the scene of the crime, hmm?" she says, grinning. "It's worse than the picture."

    "Ouch."

    Kara spins around, looking horrified. "No, I didn't mean it like that."

    Lena arches an eyebrow. "You didn't?"

    "I mean, I definitely did, but it came out sounding terrible, didn't it?"

    Lena chuckles. "I'm a little insulted, I'll admit."

    Kara looks miserable at the sound of that, and Lena should take pity on her, but it's a little fun to see her flap and stutter. "I just mean that it looks a lot whiter than I thought."

    "Because I'm in here, and I'm the palest person on Earth?"

    "You think you're funny, but you're really not," Kara deadpans, and Lena remains charmed. So very charmed.

    "Sam would probably agree with you," Lena tells her. "Though, I just think she's the one who's not funny."

    Kara turns around the room again, taking it in. "I'm meeting her tomorrow," she says, sighing. "Officially. Alex is taking us both to lunch at Noonan's and she's been texting every half hour reminding me of the time, place, not to wear anything too covered in paint and also never to mention her high school girlfriend who wasn't actually her girlfriend."

    "Sounds to me you could bargain quite a few things out of your sister there," Lena points out.

    Kara grins. "Oh, totally," she says. "She's promised I could borrow her motorbike for years, but my - " she stops quite suddenly and clears her throat. "I just wasn't allowed to."

    Lena tilts her head a little. "And now?"

    "Now, I guess I can do whatever I want," she says with a little shrug. "Though, definitely don't tell Eliza I said that, because I'll just get another lecture about my recent choices."

    "Eliza?"

    "My adoptive mother," Kara says, walking towards the floor-to-ceiling windows and looking out at the world.

    "You're adopted," she says, and positively hates how this other thing somehow connects them together in some way. It's not helping. None of this is helping.

    She needs Kara to leave.

    She needs her to walk out right now.

    "When I was fifteen, yeah," Kara says, focus remaining outward. "I went to live with the Danvers when I was twelve, though. After I lost my parents."

    Lena watches her stand there, turned slightly away. The moonlight makes her look ethereal, not-of-this-world, and it's almost as if she could float right out into the stars. "I was four," she says, quiet like a secret. Which it is, of course, because Lillian buried the truth of her birth right alongside her biological mother.

    Kara turns only her head to look at her.

    "When I was adopted," Lena clarifies. "Only four."

    Kara blinks, but she does not apologise. Lena appreciates that, because there is a loss but also something found in being through what they've been through. One cannot outweigh the other, but they are both substantial.

    Lena gets to her feet, a moth drawn to a flame. She steps around her desk, getting closer but telling herself it's a terrible idea. "You're not a Danvers, though," she says, because it's something that's intrigued her since her initial Google search of her name.

    "I - what?" Kara says. "Oh, no, I'm not a Danvers," she says, and Lena doesn't miss the way her eyes drop to her own left hand where her wedding ring sits. A haunting reminder.

    Lena stops moving closer.

    "I never was," Kara says. "I didn't want - I wanted to keep the name of my parents, you know? Sometimes, it felt as if I was the only proof they were ever even here in this world, and I couldn't just leave their name behind." She smiles this tiny grin, and Lena imagines she's thinking about her spouse in this moment; about the fact she probably did end up changing her surname eventually.

    Maybe this is what Lena needs to see. She needs to witness Kara as someone else's, and then this nasty little crush will fade away. She really needs it to, because it already hurts.

    She really knows how to pick them, doesn't she?

    Kara leans forward slightly. "Is that why I detect a bit of an accent?" she says. "Something from the Continent?"

    Lena just manages a smile, trying to settle her thoughts. It should be simple. She's already lived an entire quarter of a century already. She should be able to handle emotions.

    But Kara is unexpected in a way Lena can't quite figure out, and it's such a shame that she's dedicated her life to Science. It's in her very blood to learn all she can about things she doesn't understand; pick them apart and figure out how they work.

    Apparently, it also applies to people named Kara.

    "Ireland," she says, allowing her full accent to curl around the word, and Kara's eyes snap up, a little wide.

    Well, well, well.

    "Have a thing for accents, do you?" she asks, and this is trouble.

    "I - " Kara sputters, cheeks flushing in embarrassment. "I mean, yes, who wouldn't, right?"

    Lena wrinkles her nose. "It depends on the accent, I think," she says. "It could be an English one." She exaggerates a shudder, and Kara laughs loud and gloriously.

    "You're just biased," Kara accuses, which is accurate, and she smiles in response. "Still have it all these years later, hmm?"

    "I'm an Irish girl at heart, what can I say?" Lena says, slightly coy.

    Kara hums. "I've been there, once," she says. "I think, um, a few years ago. It was really beautiful." Her tone is soft in a way that's related to a memory of that time, and Lena almost asks if it was her honeymoon. She needs the confirmation, maybe, to nip this in the butt.

    It's definitely not helping that Kara is existing in this moment with her.

    "Have you had dinner?" Kara asks her, and it sounds as if she's asking out of curiosity and not invitation. It's almost a relief.

    "I have," she says, and she sounds proud to be able to tell Kara that truth.

    And Kara looks equally proud, which is just ridiculous. It's just dinner. Seriously. "That's good, because I had a lecture all planned for you," Kara tells her. "Now I won't even need my index cards."

    "Well, now I want to hear this lecture," she says, laughing softly.

    "Too bad," Kara says, stepping back as if she's just realising how close to each other they're standing. "You're being all good and eating on time and taking care of yourself, so it's your own fault."

    Lena watches as she turns to the window again, silent as she takes in the view. It's almost as if Kara feels it, too, but Lena isn't brave enough to ask.

    So she turns to face the view as well, hands clasped in front of her.

    She wouldn't be able to say for how long they stand there, but she knows it's long enough to mean something rather significant.

    Kara gets very little sleep.

    It's not anything unusual for her, but the reason this time is not from grief but from guilt. It's in her bones and in her veins, making her think horrible, horrific things.

    When exhaustion does claim her, she dreams. It's the only time Cat becomes real. Kara can get through her days without really thinking about her wife in a literal sense, but she has no control over her dreams.

    Cat is there.

    She's always there, but always just out of reach. She never says anything, and Kara never minds. She just likes to be able to look at her the way she remembers, from Before.

    During those months when they were happy, settled, allowed to love each other and willing to show it.

    Before everything fell apart at the seams and Cat left her alone with a seven-year-old, a life-changing life insurance settlement and complete control over the dissolved empire she was still building. Just thinking about it still makes her anxious, because she knows she's not living to Cat's standards.

    Whatever those were.

    And now. God, now there's this other woman. A woman who is gorgeous and incredible and smart and interested. It's what has played on her mind from the moment she left Lena's office.

    Lena.

    In her dreams, Cat is usually silent, just listening as Kara talks. But tonight is different. Tonight, Cat says a single word, and it's Lena's name, and Kara feels the chill in her bones right in her dream... and it's still there when her alarm goes off.

    The only thing that settles the demons she wakes up with is L's letters she keeps in her nightstand drawer. The words slow her racing heart, and she sits a little while in bed and tries to recall the tone of her wife's voice.

    Everything about her was to do with tone. She could be saying certain words with certain meanings, but her tone would be saying something else entirely. It was a difficult thing for Kara to figure out, and she's didn't always manage to catch her meaning.

    Now is no different.

    Ugh.

    It would be easy to remain in a mood, but she knows she can't. She has a day full of expectations, including getting Carter ready for camp and dropping him off, going in to National City, working on the piece, and fitting in a lunch with Alex and Sam in the process.

    Kara will, under absolutely no circumstances, see Lena.

    Which is, of course, exactly what happens the second she arrives at L-Corp, trying and failing to sneak into the building without encountering the boss. She doesn't mean to be awkward, but it helps that Lena's in a rush for a meeting, looking devastating in a dark pantsuit and equally dark lip.

    It's just that Kara feels inexplicably sad when Lena is gone, and she doesn't know what to do with it beyond channeling it into her art. Her emotions are responsible for so much of her work, reflecting her feelings in a way that very obviously gives her away.

    Which is why Kara skips ahead to the darker colours, slipping on her headphones and spending a full four hours working, but not working at all. It comes out messy and disjoint, telling a tale of betrayal and confusion, and her hands are covered by the time her alarm rings to tell her she has half an hour to meet Alex.

    Kara sighs.

    Maybe this is something she can talk to Alex about. She knows about moving on from a relationship that's left her broken. Not in the same way, though, and Kara has thought endlessly about whether it's better or worse to lose someone who didn't want to leave, or someone who did.

    Does it matter, anyway? It's still a loss.

    Kara descends from her position on the ladder and spends a few minutes cleaning her hands and removing her smock. She steps back as far as she can in the enclosed space and takes in her progress.

    Well.

    That's one way to get work done, isn't it? Dream about your dead wife and exist in emotional turmoil. It's really some of her best work, and she's convinced it's exactly what Lena wants.

    Lena.

    Kara is not thinking about her right now.

    One last check of her hands and clothes, she fluffs her hair to look somewhat presentable, and then slips out of her enclosure and checks the time. Fourteen minutes to go.

    It's silly of Alex to give her and Sam different meeting times, because she doesn't want them leaving the building at the time. Kara knows Alex really likes this woman, merely based on the way she acts whenever it comes to her. The fact Kara is meeting her at all, before the holidays, speaks volumes of Alex's intentions, and Kara isn't going to do anything to mess this up for her.

    Like potentially develop a disastrous crush on Sam's best friend. Wow. Kara is just asking for trouble right there. Forget her own feelings on the mere idea of cheating on her wife; what would Alex think?

    What would Alex think?

    She's almost tempted to ask, but she remembers that she's supposed to be the last to arrive because Alex didn't want the two of them sitting there waiting on Sam. Outnumbering her, as it were. Overwhelming her.

    Kara opens the door to the restaurant and spots them immediately. Alex has shown her pictures of Sam before, but an image doesn't do her justice, and she's immensely proud of her sister for finding and actually managing to land her.

    Also, is it written somewhere in the contracts that people working for L-Corp have to be good-looking?

    Alex spots her a beat later and waves her over, smiling a smile Kara hasn't ever seen before. Hah. It seems she has a Sam-Smile. Definitely going to tease her about that later.

    Kara crosses the room quickly, her own smile a reflection of her sister's. It's impossible not to return it, regardless of the hurricane going on within her. Alex and Sam rise in sync, and Kara holds in a snort as Alex does the introductions and Kara greets Sam with a firm hug.

    They're huggers in this family.

    Sam doesn't seem to mind, and they all take their seats once more, a server bringing a menu for Kara. She glances over it only once, already familiar with it, and then they place their orders.

    Kara knows there's a critical balance between showing disinterest and essentially interrogating Sam, so Kara starts her questions safely in the realm of work.

    "I've been in National City for almost eighteen months now," she says, her hands fiddling with the edge of the tablecloth. "When Lena expanded from Metropolis, she asked me to come along. I think she needed a friend more than a CFO, if I'm being honest. The transition wasn't easy and she needed the support."

    Of course asking about work would lead to Lena, and Kara needs to be talking about anything but Lena to the woman's best friend.

    "Plus, it's always great having the extra help with Ruby," Sam adds, shrugging sheepishly. "She's great with her."

    Kara gets the feeling Sam says those words for a very specific reason, but the last thing she's going to do is bring up her seven year old son when Sam knows she's twenty-seven, herself.

    Instead, Kara asks, "So, how does NC compare?"

    Sam glances at Alex. "It's definitely got its perks."

    Kara groans. "Sam, no, that's my sister," she jokes, and Alex winks at her. She shakes her head. "Well, I'm glad NC is looking up."

    "Alex mentioned you don't live here anymore," Sam says, and Kara feels a tingle run down her spine.

    "I - yeah, I'm kind of on a sabbatical," she says, which isn't entirely untrue. She's taking a break from National City. That's exactly what she's doing. "Staying with Eliza in Midvale."

    Sam nods, thankfully not requiring an explanation on who Eliza is. "I forget you two are surfer chicks."

    Alex scoffs. "Uh, I'm the surfer chick here," she says, pointing at herself. "Kara can't even get onto a board."

    Kara shrugs, unashamed. "They say it's because I'm clumsy, but I reckon I'm just not one for the water."

    "She claims she's a 'cloud girl,'" Alex tells Sam, using her fingers to indicate invisible quotation marks. "That's where her head always is."

    Kara rolls her eyes. "I just prefer the air," she says. "I like to fly." It's not untrue. She loves the sky. It's just that her feet have been firmly planted on the ground since a cancer diagnosis altered the very fabric of their lives.

    Sam seems amused by them, which is the general consensus whenever she and Alex get into a groove. It almost feels as if they haven't had the roughest year of their lives - since they were teenagers, at least.

    The conversation is easiest when they talk about themselves when they were younger. Things get tricky whenever stories about Kara's time in National City pop up, but she and Alex carefully avoid any talk about that.

    There is no mention of any late wives or adoptive sons. No talk about Alex's job or what Kara did before she left National City. They don't even really talk about the work she's currently doing for Lena.

    Kara learns that the two of them actually met in the coffee shop across the street, Alex treating herself to non-hospital coffee and Sam needing a break from the white walls of her office. Kara learns that Alex was very smooth during their first meeting, scoring a number and a promise to meet again for coffee in the same place the next day. Kara also learns that Sam is allergic to sesame seeds and she doesn't talk to her parents.

    "They kicked me out when they found out I was pregnant," she explains, and her voice is steady, as if it's something she's come to accept about her life. "Lena, Jack and I really bonded over shitty parents in college."

    Kara blinks, because Jack isn't a name she's heard before. It's another piece of information she's sure Sam offers for a reason, and Kara glances at Alex, whose own eyes are obviously on Sam already.

    She's totally gone on her; it's both cute and hilarious.

    "I guess Alex and I are pretty lucky then," Kara says, because she's had two sets of wonderful parents. Flawed, sure, but they've both been loved unconditionally, and that's the part a child remembers.

    "I suppose we can only be better parents to our own children than ours were to us," Sam says, focus on her plate.

    Kara exchanges a look with Alex, and she just smiles back. Alex mentioned that Sam asked if Kara had a child, based on what Kara told Lena, so she knows Sam knows. It's just that, as much as she would love to gush about her son, she's not entirely comfortable with the questions that come after.

    There is a lore that surrounds the death of Cat Grant, and Kara is not going to be the person to enlighten the masses. Some people don't even believe it's true, and, frankly, Kara joins that group of people when she's feeling particularly masochistic.

    Sometimes, she imagines her wife just took off to some island to live out the rest of her days, and the days just kept coming. The idea brings her comfort whenever she feels cold under her fingertips, a beating heart gone still in a forty-six year old body.

    "Kara?"

    She snaps to attention, noticing Sam and Alex looking at her, both with concern.

    "You okay?" Alex asks, and she looks nervous.

    "I - yeah, sorry, I must have zoned out there," she says, and absently rubs at the skin around her wedding ring. Both Alex and Sam zero in on the action, and Kara immediately stops, tucking her hands under the table.

    "Are you sure you're okay?" Alex asks, and she must know how complicated a question that is.

    "I'm fine," she says. "Just didn't get much sleep last night," she admits, and then winces, because that'll only make Alex worry even more. "I'm fine," she repeats, words firm enough for Alex to know to drop it. She glances at her watch, and sighs. "I should probably get back, anyway. I need to put in as many hours as I can today."

    Alex nods, conceding. "We're still on for tomorrow, right?"

    "Kirby's Deli at noon," she confirms, and then gets to her feet. "You're paying for me, dear sister," she tells Alex.

    "Freeloader," Alex comments.

    Kara ignores her and looks at Sam, who is watching her very closely. "It really was nice to meet you, Sam," she says, and she means it. Sam truly is lovely. "If ever you want to hang out without the lame surfer chick, you know where to find me."

    Alex throws her napkin at her. "Get out of here, loser."

    Kara just grins at them both, and then ducks out of the restaurant as quickly as she can without looking as if she's rushing.

    She needs space.

    She just needs some air.

    She needs to go back to a month ago when the most she had to think about is how to get Carter to eat his green beans, and not how she's managed to develop an attraction for yet another untouchable woman.

    "Christmas is next week," Sam says, ducking into Lena's office and tossing a Peanut Cup at her. "What are we doing?"

    Lena blinks away from her screen, headache blooming behind her eyes. "Um."

    "You're coming over to spend it with Ruby and me," Sam declares, dropping onto a chair in front of Lena's desk. "It's decided."

    Lena stretches her spine. "No Alex?"

    Sam's expression pinches slightly. "No Alex," she confirms. "She's spending it in Midvale with her family. It's too soon in the relationship to spend the holiday together, anyway."

    Lena opens the Peanut Cup in her hands. "Okay."

    Sam lifts her head. "That's it? You're not even going to try to get out of it? Try to convince me you're better off spending the day at work?"

    "Why would I bother wasting my breath?" Lena says, carefully biting into the edge of her little chocolate candy.

    Sam laughs. "You are a genius."

    "Always a tone of surprise," Lena notes. "But, yeah, I suppose I'm stuck with you for the holidays."

    "You're not allowed to get Ruby a better present than me," Sam warns.

    "That sentence is intentionally ambiguous, isn't it?"

    "Both meanings stand," Sam says, grinning. "You have to get me a better gift than you get Ruby, but yours for her has to suck more than the one I got for her."

    "You're worse than a child."

    "I won't even try to deny it."

    Lena just shakes her head, indulging in the sweet peanut butter on her tongue. "So, how was your lunch?" she asks. "How was Kara?"

    Sam doesn't immediately respond, which is particularly worrying for Lena. "I - she's really nice," she says. "Kind and sweet and engaging, and I can see why you like her."

    "Sam."

    "But there's just something... odd," Sam says. "To do with her marriage or her son, I don't know. The way she and Alex are just so cagey about it raises red flags for me."

    Lena suddenly feels cold. "Do you think they're hiding something?"

    "Yes."

    Lena looks down, brow creased in thought.

    "Maybe Kara is hiding," Sam suggests. "She's anonymous in her work, and she's been on a 'sabbatical' for a year, so maybe she's hiding from someone."

    "Her husband?" Lena asks, horrified at just the thought.

    "Maybe," Sam offers. "I don't know, and I doubt Alex would actually tell me if I flat-out asked. She's very protective of Kara; almost defensive in a way that tells me the two of them have definitely been through some intense things."

    Lena sighs. "I'm sure Alex will tell you when she's able to," she assures Sam. "It seems as if the secrets aren't actually hers to share."

    "I know," Sam says, sighing. "I can't help that I'm curious, but I definitely respect their privacy. Doesn't mean I won't continually speculate. I like knowing things. Especially when it involves someone I'm seeing."

    Lena fidgets with the wrapper of her Peanut Cup. "But, other than that, what did you think of her?" she asks, forcing herself not to blush. Sam wouldn't let her live it down.

    Sam grins, anyway. "Lena."

    "Oh, my God, can you stop being so weird and tell me what you thought of her?"

    "She's very pretty," Sam finally says. "And a bit of a beefcake, wow. When she took off her coat and revealed the tight sweater beneath; I almost dumped Alex right there."

    "Can you please be serious?"

    Sam softens considerably. "She's great, Lena," she says. "She's purposeful and interesting and she seems to care a whole lot about the people around her. If you were going to develop a disastrous crush on anyone; you could have picked much worse."

    "Oh, well, thank you."

    "Anytime," Sam says, getting to her feet. "I better get back to work; my boss is kind of a hardass."

    "I'll fire your ass," Lena automatically responds, and Sam holds up her middle finger.

    "You'd miss my fine ass."

    Lena doesn't dispute it. "Thank you for my afternoon treat," she says instead, which earns her a blown kiss this time around.

    Alex calls twice and sends three texts while Kara works through the afternoon, and she only sees the notifications when she takes her first break. She's managed to get into something of a groove, distracting herself from her spiralling thoughts by working.

    Huh.

    Why hasn't she been doing this all this time? She was convinced creating was so linked to her wife, but now it's providing her with an escape. She feels untouchable when she has a paintbrush in her hand and an empty canvas before her.

    The progress she's made just in this day alone puts her into a good position going into the weekend, and she doesn't feel too guilty spending a few extra hours in bed on Friday morning.

    Reading L's letters has turned into a bit of a ritual, and she does so before getting out of bed to face the day. She feels a little more settled than she did the day before and she's convinced it's because she didn't get a chance actually to interact with Lena.

    It also helps that her wife didn't haunt her dreams.

    She knows she's going to have to talk to Alex about it. Alex would know what to do. She'd be sensitive to the situation in which Kara now finds herself, tease her a little, but ultimately help her figure out -

    Well.

    Kara can't realistically claim she knows what to do about all of this. If she had it her way, she would have avoided any confusion that meeting Lena has created, because Kara was quite sure she would go through the rest of her life alone and -

    It hasn't even been a year.

    The thought alone makes her feel green. Surely that's too soon to start feeling something for someone else. What would her wife say?

    Well. She'd probably shove Kara forward and tell her to go for it. She gets the feeling Lena wouldn't be completely opposed to something happening and, if Kara were anyone else and Lena weren't so connected to Alex, it could be considered something purely physical.

    But Kara is Kara and she's gone so many years of avoiding her feelings already. Imagine how much more time she could have spent with her wife if she stopped doing exactly that.

    So, Kara has a lot on her mind when she picks up Carter from his last day of day camp at his school, almost forgetting to expect him to be excited about their trip into the city. He practically runs to the car, throwing himself into her arms as if she's rescuing him from captivity.

    Well.

    School can be prison-like at times.

    When Carter starts chanting, "Aunt Alex, Aunt Alex, Aunt Alex," Kara swallows her guilt and tries to muster matching enthusiasm. She loves getting to watch her sister and son interact. It just fills her with warmth; especially after how shy Carter once was around her.

    They actually met before Kara and his mother were anywhere near dating, and that first meeting devastated Kara in the sense that this important person in her life wasn't immediately clicking with this other important person. Her two favourite people.

    But then, the third time they met up, Alex had some brain scans with her, and Carter's curiosity peaked to astronomical levels, so now he's probably going to be a neurosurgeon.

    Alex is immensely smug about it.

    The drive into National City is a lot more enjoyable with Carter in the backseat. He's unusually talkative today, telling her about his class' pet rabbit, Charles. Charles is an adorable, fluffy, complete monster of a rabbit, and they had to look after him one weekend in October, which was -

    Eliza had to take Carter to school on the Monday, because Kara couldn't bear to part with the little beast. Alex still teases her about the exasperated call Eliza paid to her.

    That little interaction was probably one of the more normal ones she and Alex have had in a while. Kara can count them on one hand, and she hates the way she's handled herself when it comes to Alex.

    Instead of holding onto her tightly, she rather pushed her away. But she's trying now. This whole thing with Nia and L's letters and the mural - they've all given her the kind of incentive she needed to start living a life she and her wife could be proud of.

    All these little things putting her scattered pieces back together.

    It's really been that kind of day, and she has to force herself not to burst into tears when she and Carter arrive at Kirby's Deli to find Alex already sitting at a tiny table, folder open in front of her. It's the way her entire face lights up when she sees Carter, though. That's what stops Kara in her tracks because, dear God, how lucky are they?

    Alex almost gets barrelled over by the force of Carter's hug, and she looks so unexpectedly pleased that Kara actually calls her silly.

    "Stop being mean to me," Alex pouts, looking at Carter. "Tell your mom to be nice to me."

    Dutifully, Carter says, "Mommy, be nice," from his spot on Alex's lap.

    "Yeah yeah," Kara says, already moving to order their lunch. She doesn't even bother to contain her smile when she hears Alex says, "Wanna see the scans I brought for you?"

    If Lena had it her way, she would probably spend the evening catching up on her work, but Jess reminds her she's meeting Jack for dinner, and she's suddenly irritated for a reason other than that her evening plans aren't really her own.

    With a sigh, she packs up and leaves the office for the evening. She doesn't have much time to change, and she's not sure she would, either way. It just seems like too much work at the end of a long work week, and Jack has definitely seen her looking worse. There were many a sleepless night in the labs.

    It's just that, when Lena gets to the foyer, she's made increasingly aware that Kara is still here. She knows she should just keep walking. She knows she needs to leave things be and maintain this distance between them, but -

    Lena is weak, you see, and there's already a part of her that misses the woman.

    She moves to the enclosed section, takes a breath, and then pokes her head inside. She keeps her eyes downcast, not willing to ruin the surprise of the unveiling, and she says Kara's name.

    Kara, who sees her rather than hears her, and removes her headphones. She abandons her work immediately and slides out of the space so Lena doesn't have to keep her gaze averted.

    "You can look, you know," Kara says, sounding amused. "It still doesn't look like much."

    "I'd rather wait," Lena tells her. "I can be a very patient woman."

    Kara's eyebrows rise, because it sounds as if Lena is telling her something specific. Maybe she is, who knows? Lena certainly doesn't.

    Kara clears her throat. "Are you heading home?" she asks.

    "Dinner first, don't worry," Lena assures her. "Though, I'm still waiting on that carefully-worded lecture you've promised."

    "It really is a good one," Kara brags. "There's even a Benjamin Franklin quotation."

    "Wow."

    "I'm pretty proud of it."

    "As you should be."

    Kara grins at her, visibly pleased, and Lena is reminded why interacting with this woman is such a bad idea. She's some kind of dichotomy, both light and heavy, happy and sad in a profound way, and Lena itches to pick her apart and learn all she can.

    Instead, they stand and smile at each other like a pair of teenagers, and Lena knows she's acting silly. Everything is just silly at this moment, childish and exciting, and Lena wishes she knew what Kara was thinking.

    This moment is also silent confirmation that she's not the only one experiencing all these new, terrifying feelings. It's so much worse in many ways, because there are obviously so many things Kara is purposefully not telling her, and Lena wonders what'll change about her opinion if she were to learn them.

    But, still, Kara smiles until she doesn't, anymore. It fades and shifts into confusion, and Lena doesn't realise what's caused the change in expression until she feels a warm arm slide around her waist and a stubbly kiss against her cheek.

    Kara actually takes a step back, shaking her head as if she could clear it of whatever she was initially thinking.

    Lena forces her head to turn, Jack suddenly at her side, and her heart stutters for reasons other than excitement at the sight of him. In fact, she feels a flash of irritation that is hot and intense. "Jack," she says, voice overly sweet. "What are you doing here?"

    There is another bouquet in his free hand, and he presents it to her with a charming smile, her hands automatically moving to take them. "I thought I'd surprise you," he says. "We can head to the restaurant together."

    Lena blinks. "Oh," she says, because that is a surprise. "I - that's sweet."

    "I generally am," he quips, and his eyes sparkle.

    Lena looks away from him, eyes on Kara, who is quietly watching them. Her eyes are blue and cloudy, conflicted in a way Lena wishes she could understand. Lena clears her throat. "Jack, this is Kara," she introduces. "She's painting our mural for L-Corp's first birthday." She's a lot more than that, of course, but Lena doesn't know how to give that away. "Kara, this is Jack Spheer, we - "

    See.

    Here's the thing.

    There are a number of ways Lena could introduce him. Jack has been many things to her in the years she's known him. She's even allowed herself to think about what it would be like to spend the rest of her life with him; have a family with him.

    But, in this moment, all she wants to tell Kara is he's the Founder of Biomax, and that they went to college together.

    So, it is both a blessing and a curse when Jack holds out his free hand and says, " - are hoping Lena finally agrees to marry me."

    Lena, well, she's what one would call mortified, because that's - they haven't -

    Kara shakes his hand, and Lena notices the way her grip is tight around Jack's. "Nice to meet you," she says, voice equally tight, and Lena has never heard it sound that way before.

    Jack's smile is a little uneasy when he takes his hand back, opening and closing it as if he's lost feeling in it. "I - you too."

    Kara's smile is incredibly polite, and there's a small chunk of time that's intensely awkward, before Kara's attention drifts past them to a point behind them.

    It is the most breathtaking thing, what happens next. If Lena thought her smile was radiant when lost in her art, it is a thief of the highest proportions when she smiles in this moment, because it steals Lena's breath right out of her lungs.

    Lena turns, not sure what to expect, and she catches sight of a woman who must be Alex standing with a young boy just outside the building. They're both waving at Kara, beaming smiles on their faces, and Lena feels her heart continually thump in her chest.

    "We should get going," Jack says, with no tact.

    Kara just nods, eyes not even on them. "Of course," she says. "You two have a pleasant evening." She barely meets Lena's gaze, before she's ducking away first and heading out of the building.

    Lena turns to watch her go, unable to stop herself. Her eyes track Kara's steps as she walks, purposeful and with an obvious destination, and it is something to behold when the little boy with Alex bounces on his toes, and then throws himself at Kara when she's near enough, seemingly uncaring of all the paint.

    Lena feels inexplicably warm when Kara drops to a knee to be at his level and listens with her complete attention. She would spend hours watching them, if allowed, but Jack gently adjusts his grip on her waist and she's reminded he's actually there.

    Lena wonders if he thinks about it, too. About what could have been if their lives played out differently. About how their futures would look if life had decided to give rather than take the first time they were together.

    Because Lena thinks about it a lot. More than she would care to admit. She thinks about living in a small suburban home in Metropolis. She thinks of having an actual backyard; of a home that feels lived in. A home with love given and taken so freely.

    They haven't talked about it, not really. It's been nearly six years since they ended the first time, and they're so much older now. So much more settled and accomplished. Jack has managed to become the successful businessman he wanted to be, and Lena has stepped into a role she wasn't always sure she wanted.

    She doesn't have to be a genius to know things would be vastly different if a certain night nearly six years ago hadn't occurred. And the morning after. The days that followed.

    Their inevitable end.

    Jack leads her out of the building with a hand at the small of her back, turning her away from Kara's direction and that is a generosity. She doesn't think she would be able to keep walking if presented with the interaction right in front of her.

    It doesn't stop her from hearing a tiny voice say, "Mommy, you should have seen Aunt Alex's hair when she touched the Van Der Graaf Generator," with all the excitement in the world, and Lena has to force herself not to turn around and demand something she knows she has no right to.

    Instead, she lets Jack take her to dinner, and they decidedly don't mention Kara or children or marriage.

    She would hate it, if she weren't so relieved.

    Chapter 5

    V

    "So, do you want to tell me what's up with you?"

    Kara should expect the question, but it still surprises her. She's just relieved Alex waited until Carter lost his fight with sleep and dropped his head into her lap, tucking himself into a small ball on the plush seat of the booth they're sharing.

    Still, Kara says, "What do you mean?"

    Alex gives her a look. "You've been acting weirder than usual."

    "Ouch."

    "Kara," she sighs. "Just tell me what's going on. I can't help if I don't know what's bothering you."

    Kara clenches her jaw, biting her tongue not to make an unfair remark about how now she wants to help.

    Alex must sense it, though, because her shoulders drop in defeat. "Kara," she sighs again. "I - we've had a good day, right? Lunch together, Carter and I going to the Science Museum, all of us having dinner? It's been good, right?"

    "It's been great, Alex," Kara says, and she means it. If she allowed herself, it almost feels as if it's from Before.

    If she allowed herself, she could imagine she and Carter would leave this restaurant and return home just a few blocks from here to find a particular blonde woman waiting for them with a smile, sarcastic remark about Dr Badass and kisses for them both.

    But Kara doesn't allow it.

    Alex clears her throat. "Carter told me about this potential new wife he's found for you," she says, managing a smile.

    Kara rolls her eyes. "He's convinced she's the answer to everything," she explains, which might be true, because the letters have helped Kara in ways she wouldn't even be able to explain. "Did he tell you about the radio she sent him?"

    "Oh, I heard everything about it," Alex says, lifting herself up enough to sneak a look at a slumbering Carter. "He said her first letter made you smile, but her second made you cry."

    "I - yeah." She runs a hand over her hair. "She wrote some things I needed to hear, particularly from a stranger."

    "What did you need to hear?"

    "That I'm doing okay, despite everything," Kara says. "That I should be proud of the way I'm raising Carter; that it's okay that it's taking as long as it's taking to find my feet again. But especially that it's not some insult to her that I'm figuring out how to keep living without her here."

    What's worse, though, is that Kara already knows all of these things. She's always known them, without needing anyone to tell her the words. And yet, she still needed to hear them.

    People talk of sticks and stones, but words truly are powerful.

    "She's helping," Alex says.

    "She's helping," Kara confirms.

    "Reckon she's the one, then?" Alex asks, grinning.

    "Hah." Kara drinks the leftover of Carter's milkshake. "I think - "

    "What?"

    Kara clears her throat. "Let's say, hypothetically, there was a woman," she starts, and Alex's eyebrows actually rise. "Would that - does that - I keep having these dreams."

    "Dreams?"

    "About Cat."

    Alex's expression would be humorous if both of them weren't deeply aware this is one of the first times Kara has said the name in Alex's presence since the day Alex said the words, I'm sorry, there's nothing I can do.

    "It was always a future problem," Kara says; "what would happen if ever - "

    "You met someone."

    "I told you I was safer in Midvale," Kara jokes halfheartedly.

    "Whoa."

    Kara doesn't confirm it, but she doesn't need to. Instead, she glances down at her left hand. "A week before she died, she told me she trusted me." She shakes her head. "You'd think something like that would be implicit in an actual marriage, but I think she needed to say the words as much as I needed to hear them. She trusted me, with her son and her company and her legacy. She trusted me to keep loving her even after she was gone."

    "Kara, you do know that finding someone else doesn't mean you can't still love her, right?" Alex says. "I know she made a joke of the qualities she wanted for you in a new wife, but you and I both know she meant it. All she wanted was happiness for you when she was gone. If it's coming now, months earlier, or even years from now; she would want it for you."

    Kara thinks of Lena, who has given her something very particular to think about. Not in any realistic way, of course, because she's now deeply aware of who Jack is, but enough for Kara to know she can move on from her loss.

    It can happen, and it will, and it is a beautiful, heartbreaking thing.

    "Kara?"

    Her eyes meet Alex's. "So."

    "So?"

    "You're still coming with us to see the llamas tomorrow, right?"

    Alex pauses, as if contemplating whether to let Kara to change the subject. Eventually, she allows it, because at least they're talking to each other now. They're figuring things out, and Kara doesn't think, for a second, that this is the end of this conversation.

    "Of course," Alex says, and they just keep moving in the right direction.

    Lena doesn't sleep.

    Not really.

    When she arrives home after dinner with Jack, she steps out of her armour of clothing, takes a long shower, puts on some comfortable pyjamas and makes herself some tea. It's the perfect recipe for falling into a peaceful sleep, but it just never comes.

    Instead, she thinks about Kara and Kara's son and Jack and what could have been and what could still be.

    Jack is offering her what she wanted six years ago, right on a platter. He's said it offhandedly a few times, how he would move out here if she asked; how they could really try again, and how they could build that family they failed at the first time around.

    Lena doesn't know how to tell him it's unlikely they'll ever be able to reclaim that.

    To distract herself, she intends to spend her Saturday working from home, maybe do some leisure reading. She makes Eggs Benedict for breakfast, enjoying the task until she actually has to eat. Alone.

    With a sigh, she steps out onto the balcony with her plate, book and espresso. Some fresh air will do her good. Maybe it'll make her brave enough to make the decisions she knows she needs to.

    Or, maybe it'll make her feel lazy enough actually to spend the day relaxing. She doesn't normally take breaks from work but, with the Christmas holiday coming up, she's almost expected to.

    So she reads her book and drinks her coffee and takes a nap at noon, before she spreads out on her couch and watches the shows Sam is always telling her to watch. She orders in some lunch, spoiling herself with a selection of sushi, and then re-immersing herself in Sugar Rush.

    It's much, much later that she receives a burst of five pictures from Kara. Her heart skips a literal beat in her chest at merely the sight of her name, and she forces herself to take a few deep breaths before she opens the messages.

    To reveal llamas.

    Lena blinks. Yip. They're actual llamas.

    There are two pictures of just llamas in a small green field, and another picture of Kara actually hugging one. The fourth picture is of Alex looking severely unimpressed as a llama picks at her hair. But it's the last one, of Kara and the little boy from the day before, both with their backs turned to the camera and leaning against a metal railing and watching the llamas.

    It's Lena's favourite.

    Llamas?, she sends back.

    Today has been the best day! is Kara's response, and Lena laughs. They were so cute! And FLUFFY! Alex had to drag us out of there when we had to leave. I wanted to take them with us.

    Lena definitely isn't surprised by that. Kara seems like the type of person who would adopt a plethora of animals just for being cute. It takes the people around her to make sure she doesn't. She must have potent puppy dog eyes.

    Please tell me you don't now have llamas in your backyard.

    Kara's next message is a snapped selfie of her pout, captioned with the words, no, but I wish

    Lena probably stares at the picture for a little too long, but she can't help it. Kara just looks so soft, hair loose around her shoulders and -

    She's also not wearing her glasses, and Lena can't help thinking she looks so remarkably different. Like an entirely new person, and, yeah, okay, maybe this is her new favourite picture.

    She definitely does stare at the picture for too long, because Kara sends a tentative, Lena?, and Lena snaps back to the present.

    The first thing Lena goes to type is, You're cute, but she stops herself, because that's just asking for trouble. Instead, she types, You're not going to get any llamas out of me with that expression.

    It was worth a try.

    Does that expression usually work for you?

    Sometimes, Kara texts back. Eliza and Alex are nearly immune, but I can get my son to eat his vegetables quite quickly with the baby blues.

    Something niggles at the back of Lena's mind. Something familiar in a way that she can't quite grasp. It's right there, just waiting to be figured out, but she hasn't quite made the connection.

    Lena wants to ask about Kara's son. She's almost desperate to know everything about him: his name and his age and his favourite colour and the things he likes to do. She wants to know him, and she wants to know Kara - especially the parts she's determined to keep hidden.

    Well, you should know that expression won't work with me, either, Lena sends, though she's not entirely sure she means it, because she suspects she would cave if ever it were actually in front of her.

    Believe it or not, that actually works in your favour, is Kara's reply, and Lena doesn't get the chance to ask what that means, because Kara bids her goodnight in the next message she sends, almost immediately.

    Lena also doesn't mention that it's barely past eight o'clock.

    Well, okay, then.

    Christmas Eve is probably Kara's favourite day of the year - besides Carter's birthday, of course - and she goes all out. This year, especially, because it's Carter's first holiday season without his mother, and Kara has to make it special. She would have made it special, regardless, but now it's extra special.

    She has the means to spoil him, but even she knows not to do something like that. In another life, she actually might have considered taking him to the moon, but in this life, she's just keeping him in Midvale and surrounding him with all the love and attention she can.

    Maybe, just maybe he won't miss his mother as much.

    As much as she tries, though, she's increasingly aware there's something missing. Someone. In the greater scheme of things, they had only two actual Christmases together. It wasn't nearly enough, but Kara is choosing to look at it that actually did have two Christmases as a couple.

    Four knowing each other.

    It's still a bit of an adjustment, though, and Kara knows Carter is struggling with it when he crawls into her lap after they've had dinner and sits quietly. She looks at Alex over his head, and she smiles gently in response.

    She knows.

    They all know.

    Kara gently rubs his back, a Christmas movie playing on the screen in front of them. Eliza snaps a picture of them, smiling in that way she does when she has all her family in one place.

    Kara knows Eliza understands her loss far better than others would, having lost her own spouse. Kara has talked to her about it only once, but she can't stop thinking about how unfair it is that Kara got such a short time.

    It felt too minimal, just a blink of an eye between 'okay, yeah, okay, of course I love you, you big idiot' to 'you're going to have to teach Carter how to drive when I'm gone, and that's enough to terrify me enough to stick around until he's sixteen.'

    Kara aches with how much thinking about it can hurt.

    "Mommy," Carter whispers, getting her attention.

    "Hmm?"

    "What do you think Mama is doing in Heaven?" he asks, and Kara suppresses a whimper. It's how she explained Cat's death, because she wasn't sure how else to do it. Carter understands the concept of Heaven, and Kara wanted to give him some comfort.

    Even if Cat was always convinced she would burn in Hell. Seriously. She was truly very dramatic.

    "She's probably beating everyone at Scrabble," Kara tells him.

    "And being really smug about it," Alex adds after a moment. "She was a terrible winner."

    Carter giggles. "She was the best."

    "I almost beat her once," Kara makes sure to mention, as she always does.

    "Almost isn't actually winning, Kara," Alex points out, which was always what Cat would do. It's - it's just really hard, but - they're okay. They're going to be okay.

    "She's probably hogging the eggnog, as well," Eliza says into the silence. "She always did say she needed alcohol to get through the holidays."

    "Because Mommy is always too cheerful," Carter says, twisting around to look at Kara. "You always made up for Mama."

    "I did," Kara says, hugging him close. Only for a moment, though, because he trembles slightly.

    "Mommy," he says, sounding very serious. "What do you think L is doing right now?"

    Kara glances at the clock, thinking it over. L sounded lonely in her own way, suffering from loss of her own, but determined to make sure Kara didn't suffer the same hangups in getting back to what her life is meant to be.

    "I don't know," Kara finally says; "but I hope she's with people who love and appreciate her."

    Carter nods slowly. "And buy her lots of presents," he declares.

    "I hope she has all the food she could want," Alex adds. "And alcohol."

    Carter giggles, burrowing further into Kara. "Do you think she's thinking about us?"

    "Maybe," Kara says, though she suspects they have to have crossed the woman's mind at least once.

    "I wish there was a way to wish her Merry Christmas," he mumbles, and Kara weirdly wishes for the same thing. Just for the sake of letting L know that they're thinking of her, too. Wherever she is.

    Carter eventually falls asleep where he is, after demanding promises from them that they'll all wake up super early to open their presents first thing in the morning.

    Eliza snaps another picture of them, and Kara asks her to send the pictures she's taken throughout the day to her. Alex also takes out her phone and says, "There's a picture you should see," and Alex doesn't quite know what she's given Kara when she forwards a picture Sam sent her of herself, Ruby and Lena posing in front of a wonderfully-decorated tree.

    Kara makes the decision without thinking about it too much, and she hides herself away once she's carried Carter to bed and tucked him in snugly, and sketches the picture.

    She chooses pencil as her medium, and her hands are smudged once she's finished, satisfied she's managed to catch the light behind Sam's eyes, the mischief in Ruby's, and the longing in Lena's.

    Kara knows this is the way she expresses affection. It started young; her tendency to draw the people who are important to her. It's one of the ways she even figured out she liked girls, her desire to emulate her crushes in art too overwhelming to ignore.

    So, she knows this picture means something.

    Something she's just now coming to accept. Lena is important to her. It's really as simple as that. And as complicated.

    When she's done, she creates a digital second print of her own drawing with the intention of gifting it to Sam. Kara knows she'd want a copy in Sam's shoes, but -

    The original is meant for Lena, and that says a hell of a lot.

    It is both a surprise and a little horrifying that Sam attempts to cook something that isn't pasta on Christmas Day. Lena and Ruby sit at the raised island still in their pyjamas and watch her cook with trepidation, takeout menus ready if it doesn't go to plan.

    Which is extremely likely.

    Sam has had years of preparing food for herself and Ruby, but it's been established that her skills remain with numbers and pasta.

    "You're still a great mom," Lena assures her when the potato hash she attempts to make gets a little too charred for pleasant consumption.

    "Oh, thanks," Sam mutters. "Totally not what I was worried about, but thank you for bringing it to my attention."

    "Maybe Alex is a better cook," Ruby pipes up, and Lena laughs at the expression on Sam's face.

    "Yeah, Sam," Lena joins in. "Maybe Alex could make next year's Christmas meal."

    Sam glares at her. "From your lips into the Universe, Lena Luthor," she finally says.

    "You'll have me to thank," Lena agrees.

    "You're being awfully bold for someone who won't speak about her own love life."

    Lena laughs through her discomfort, because that's definitely not something she wants to be talking about tonight.

    Or ever.

    Ruby looks at Lena. "Are you seeing someone too?" she asked, bouncing in her seat a little.

    For a terrifying moment, the first person to come to mind is actually Kara. Her smile slips, because that's -

    She's not seeing Kara.

    If anything, she's seeing Jack, but even that doesn't feel like the correct answer to the question Ruby is asking. Everything about the situation feels out of control in a way she hates. She wants to tell Ruby the truth, but she doesn't know what that is.

    Sam comes to her apparent rescue when she says, "Oh, your Aunt Lena seems to have fallen for someone on the radio."

    Lena rolls her eyes, because Sam is still convinced Lena would be perfect for Carter's mother. Which is absurd. She's definitely not a right fit for a woman who's already lost so much.

    Ruby perks up, ready with her questions, but Lena hears her phone ring in her handbag, and she's grateful for the excuse to escape the probe into her romances.

    "There you go again," Lena says to Sam as she turns and walks towards the couch. She dips her hand into her bag, not sure what to expect, and she basically swallows her tongue at the sight of her mother's name.

    Lena goes still, just staring at the screen. It's unexpected, the call, but she should have known Lillian would choose today to make contact. She always did need some kind of excuse to call, as if doing so at another time of the year would prove she actually cared.

    Lena takes her time, but she eventually answers the call, voice steady and cold. She has learned a thing or two from the woman. "Mother, this is a surprise," she says, and she hears Sam whisper something to Ruby.

    "Well, I couldn't not call, now could I, Lena?" Lillian says. "That would be improper."

    Lena holds her tongue, careful not to say what she actually wants to. "You're having a good holiday, then?"

    "I would be having a better one if my son weren't rotting away in a jail, but I suppose it could be worse."

    Lena isn't even going to touch that with a ten-foot pole. "Silver linings," she agrees.

    "And you, darling?" Lillian continues. "You're not spending the holiday alone, are you?"

    Lena bristles at the superior tone, but her voice remains passive as she says, "I'm actually spending the day with Sam and her daughter."

    "Ah. Yes. Samantha Arias."

    Lena almost smiles, because Lillian has never made her thoughts on Sam a secret. It's one of the reasons Lena loves Sam so much: it bothers her mother to the point of actually mentioning it.

    "You aren't still seeing her, are you?"

    "Mother," Lena says, laughing unexpectedly. "We were - wow, have you thought we're together this entire time?"

    "Well, Lena, you tell me nothing about your life," she points out; "what am I to assume when you whisk her off with you to National City?"

    Lena sighs, because they could probably work on communication. "Mother, I've never dated Sam, nor am I dating her now. I was with Jack in college, remember."

    "Ah. Yes. Jack Spheer." There's a hint of distaste, which is expected, but Lillian surprises her when she says, "I suppose you could do worse."

    "Mother."

    "You're not getting any younger, Lena."

    "I'm twenty-five years old," she points out.

    "And I was already married at your age," Lillian says. "Sure, it was a different time, but you barely have any prospects."

    Lena has absolutely no idea how they've managed to get to the topic of her love life this way, because Lillian really has no right to weigh in on everything already piling up on Lena's shoulders.

    Her left hand clenches into a tight fist, and she forces herself to breathe deeply. There are so many things she could say, but she's not planning on wasting her breath. "Mother," she says. "It's Christmas. I should get back."

    "Right, of course," Lillian says. "Don't want to be rude."

    "No, we don't."

    "Okay then, my darling," she says. "I will talk to you soon."

    Lena isn't going to hold her breath, saying a quick goodbye, and then hanging up. She doesn't move for a while, letting her phone hang limply in her hand as she attempts to uncoil from the tension talking to her mother creates.

    She's still steadying her breathing when she feels Ruby's arms wrap around her waist from the side, a quiet comfort. Lena flashes back to the night Ruby told her she would be okay, and she wishes she had the belief Ruby does.
