    "Dinner's here," Ruby lets her know, and then the two of them return to the kitchen, where Sam has opened the various food containers and set out some plates.

    Sam looks at her, eyes asking a silent question.

    Lena manages a smile, small but present. "So, what have we got here?" she asks, and Sam and Ruby are both merciful enough to allow her a reprieve.

    Lillian isn't going to ruin her mood.

    Not when she has her two favourite people in the world currently with her.

    Kara contemplates rather endlessly over the merits of doing what she's about to do, but she knows she'll regret it if she doesn't.

    Also, she really just wants to talk to Lena.

    It's the late afternoon when she calls, half-expecting to reach her voicemail, so it's a lovely surprise to hear Lena answer with a, "Hello, dear," and laughter in her voice.

    "Lena," Kara breathes, and she's - it feels significant that her breath has been stolen by merely the sound of the woman's voice.

    "Kara, this is a welcome surprise, how are you?"

    "I'm good," she says, and she means it. "I'm really good, yeah. I - um, I hope I'm not interrupting any festivities."

    "Oh, no," Lena says, just as Kara hears an excited shriek on her end of the phone. "Well, maybe just hold on a second; let me step into the kitchen before Ruby deafens us both."

    Kara wouldn't be able to stop her smile if she tried, so she doesn't. She just shifts in her seat on the back deck of the house, the sun setting in front of her.

    "And, I'm back," Lena says. "Sorry about that. Sam and Ruby can get quite loud when they're watching Jeopardy."

    "Seriously?"

    "Best to stay out of their way, I reckon."

    "You don't get involved?" Kara asks.

    "I'm not allowed to," she admits. "They claim it's unfair, seeing as I'm - "

    "You're what?"

    "Um. I suppose you would call me a genius, though my popular culture knowledge probably isn't so great."

    "Are you saying you'd be the perfect partner for a bar quiz?"

    Lena laughs. "Probably."

    "I'm calling dibs then," Kara automatically says, and then winces, because that's an incredibly forward thing to say.

    "Noted," Lena mercifully says.

    Kara clears her throat. "Well, um, the reason I'm calling is to wish you all a merry Christmas," she says. "From my people to yours, I hope you're all having a wonderful day."

    "Oh, Kara," Lena breathes. "Thank you, and right back to your family, as well. Sam mentioned you're all in Midvale with, um, Eliza?"

    "We are, yes," she says. "My kid finally passed out from all the excitement a couple of minutes ago, so I think it has to have been a good one."

    "Gosh, I wish I could pass out from exhaustion," Lena quips. "To be young."

    "I envy him, I really do," Kara says. "Also the way he can sleep so deeply. It's kind of dangerous, but he once slept through a fire alarm. I wish I had that kind of sleeping power."

    "Wasted on the youth, I say."

    "So many things are," Kara agrees, and then winces when she hears more yelling on Lena's line. "Sounds heated," she comments.

    "I - yeah," Lena breathes. "I should probably go and make sure they don't start some kind of civil war."

    "Right, yeah."

    "But thank you for calling, Kara," Lena says, and her voice goes soft with sincerity. "It was really nice to hear from you."

    "I - yeah, sure thing," Kara says, suddenly quiet herself. "Enjoy the rest of your day."

    "You too," Lena says. "Pass on my well wishes, will you?"

    "Of course," Kara agrees. "Goodbye, Lena."

    "Bye, Kara."

    When the call ends, Kara doesn't actually move. She remains seated on her deck chair, hands in her lap as she replays her conversation with Lena. Her heart is still beating a little too fast, and she feels like a teenager.

    Urgh. It's a little pathetic.

    She's still sitting there when Alex comes outside, blanket over her shoulders and two cups of hot chocolate in her hands. Alex transfers the drinks to Kara's hands, and then squeezes into the deck chair with her, draping the blanket over both their shoulders and linking their arms.

    Alex takes back one of the cups of hot chocolate, and the two of them sit for nearly fifteen minutes before Alex says, "Carter had a good day, right?"

    Kara appreciates that she's worried about it. "I think so," she says. "He definitely loved the brain model you bought for him."

    Alex chuckles. "Just accept your son's going to follow in my footsteps."

    Kara grumbles halfheartedly, but she's smiling. "I suppose there are worse things," she concedes. "He could want to follow in his father's."

    Alex winces. "Did he call at all?"

    "No."

    "Did Carter even ask about him?"

    "No."

    Alex sighs. "It's not the worst thing that he's as distant as he is, Kara," she says. "I would hate to have to share Carter."

    "I would, too," Kara admits. "But I don't want my kid to grow up with the trauma of the knowledge his father found himself a new family and just forgot about him."

    Alex sips at her hot chocolate. "I think it's inevitable at this point," she says carefully. "All you can do is be the best parent you can be and hope he doesn't feel as if he's missing out on anything."

    "I'm basically three parents right now," Kara says, and she sounds only slightly overwhelmed by it. "I'm going to end up messing up."

    "So?" Alex says. "No parent is perfect. I mean, look at Mom and Dad. They definitely weren't perfect, and I reckon we turned out all right."

    Kara looks at her for a long time.

    "I mean, okay, so I was maybe an alcoholic in college and you briefly considered dropping out of high school to follow some art curator you met at your school's art show to London, but we're okay, right?"

    Kara almost snorts into her drink. "God, Maxwell was probably a trafficker, wasn't he?"

    "Super creepy dude."

    "I'm pretty sure his accent was fake," Kara says, laughing. "And he smelled like potpourri."

    "I really hope your self-preservation skills have improved since then," Alex says.

    "Oh, totally," Kara says, leaning back and pulling Alex with her. "I am all about that Stranger Danger. Have to set an example for Carter."

    "Carter doesn't even talk to some people he knows," Alex points out.

    "He's really my favourite person, I'm telling you."

    Alex hums. "You know, not long ago, I was your favourite person."

    Kara squeezes her arm, feeling inexplicably sad at how much time they've wasted not being the kind of sisters they can be. "I'm sorry, but you're just not as cute as he is."

    "Ouch," Alex pouts. "Sam thinks I'm cute."

    "Sam also thinks your jokes are funny, so I don't really trust her judgment."

    Alex fakes heartbreak, her free hand clutching at her chest. "I'm telling her you said that."

    Kara just laughs, leaning her head on Alex's shoulder. "We really are okay, aren't we?" she whispers.

    Alex knocks their cups together. "I reckon so, yeah."

    Sam rolls her eyes when Lena mentions she's going to the office the day after Christmas, but she doesn't try to stop her. Just packs her a small container of diced fruit and tells her to keep hydrated.

    Lena doesn't expect to find many - if any - people at work, but she gets the surprise of her life when she steps into the foyer and sees Kara leaning against a wall and drinking from a bottle of water. Just from the look of her, she's obviously been working, and Lena's feet carry her towards the blonde woman before she can stop herself.

    Kara's smile spreads wonderfully across her face when she notices Lena's approach, and Lena forces herself not to blush. "Hey, you," Kara says, and Lena does not swoon. Absolutely not.

    "What are you doing here?" Lena says, voice a little shaky, because she's alone with Kara. They're alone. Even Edward isn't around.

    "Catching up on some work," Kara reveals, pushing off the wall and grinning. "I had a bit of time this afternoon."

    "Oh?"

    Kara traps her bottom lip between her teeth before she says, "Alex and Eliza have taken my son to our old home." Her gaze drifts to the right. "He wanted to get his space box, and I - " she stops, shakes her head. "They're just together right now, so I had time to kill."

    "And so you decided the best way to spend that is working?"

    "It basically increases my chances of seeing you," Kara says, eyes bright, and Lena can't mistake the intent behind those words. What is happening? "But, what are you doing here?" Kara asks. "Surely the boss should get a few more days off?"

    Lena shakes her head. "I have some rather sensitive projects currently in the works," she says. "I can't stay away too long."

    Kara perks up, actually looking interested. She looks as if she wants to ask about it, but unsure if she can.

    Lena barely thinks about what she's asking when she says, "Do you want to see my lab?" She internally winces when the words are out of her mouth, because that definitely sounds like a lead-up to something.

    Kara blinks in surprise, and then nods around her smile. "Please."

    "Follow me," Lena says, and then proceeds to lead Kara to the second floor. They ride the elevator in silence, sneaking glances at each other, and Lena really does feel like she's fifteen years old and trying to figure out if the girl she likes actually likes her back.

    Things look to be working in her favour, except for the whole part where Kara is wearing a wedding ring. Lena just gets the feeling there isn't any marriage, though, and she wouldn't be able to explain why.

    Lena opens the lab doors with the biometric scanner and invites Kara into her prized space. She's so proud of what they do here, and she starts to tell Kara about them when she asks.

    Kara listens intently, following close to Lena as she talks Kara through the Bluetooth-connected glucose monitor they're developing and a more efficient epinephrine auto-injector. Kara asks about Lena's work on finding a cure for ALS, and then gushes over all the tiny cells she sees when Lena invites her to peek into a few microscopes.

    "Those things are so cute," Kara says, and Lena physically has to stop herself from telling Kara that she's the cute one. Wow. Talk about losing her cool.

    Kara moves around the lab, taking in everything with interest. Lena knows she's not pretending, eyes genuine as she asks her questions. "I enjoyed science in school," Kara reveals when she probably catches sight of Lena's surprised face one too many times. "I was actually pretty good at it."

    "Not enough to pursue it?"

    "I reckon you should pursue what you actually enjoy doing, and not just what you're good at," Kara says. "But, I mean, I did take some classes in college. I just really enjoy creating art more than I ever wanted to follow in both my fathers' footsteps."

    "Both of them?"

    Kara nods. "Both of them. Also, my mother was a lawyer, and Eliza is a doctor. After Alex picked medicine, I felt like I was failing them all by choosing to pursue something so... I guess it felt less than my other options. Sometimes, the way people look at Art is... I don't know, it was just a lot of pressure, and I think I floundered a bit. When I eventually graduated in Art History, I couldn't find a job, and it felt like everyone had been right. I should have stuck with the Sciences.

    "Then I started working as something so not linked to Art, and I think I would have given up completely on that dream if - " she stops, sighing. "I guess you just need that one person to believe in you, you know."

    Lena does know, and she can't help the flash of jealousy that hits her in an instant. Because someone else was that person for Kara. She expects Kara to glance at her left hand, but her gaze rather drifts to something on one of the tables.

    Kara's brow creases. "Why is this here?" she asks, and she sounds a little lost.

    Lena walks towards her, getting close enough to see what's got her looking so ashen. There is an open article on the table, dated just a few weeks ago, reporting once more on the death of Cat Grant.

    One of her researchers must have been reading it and left it open. Lena closes the magazine gently, not wanting to think about the woman.

    Kara still looks a little thrown. She blinks twice. Three times.

    "Someone must have been reading it," Lena explains, shaking her head. "I'm sure Cat would get a kick out of knowing people are still writing about her."

    Kara tilts her head to the side. "You sound as if you knew her," she points out, and there is undeniable curiosity in her voice.

    "I did," Lena confirms. "We met a long time ago, when I was still a schoolgirl and she still worked at the Daily Planet in Metropolis. She was kind to me, actually noticing I existed in a room full of people. I guess she thought I would become something, one day."

    "She wasn't wrong."

    "She generally wasn't, yes," Lena says, sighing. She and Cat had an odd relationship: professional, but also more than that. The woman would call her up randomly every few months and passive-aggressively ask questions about Lena's life and tell Lena about her own. "And she was super smug about it."

    Kara smiles, as if she knows.

    Lena sighs, leaning her hip against the table. "She called me, you know," she says. "A little less than a year ago. She would do that, just randomly call. The one time she asked some really strange questions about my relationship with my assistant." She frowns at the memory, and Kara just smiles that secret smile. "But she called, and I didn't know it would be the last time we talked. She told me she was following the work we were doing here, and she asked if we were doing any brain mapping."

    Kara goes completely still in front of her.

    "Just, really, any work on the brain, which - it didn't make sense to me at the time, and I - she must have already known what would happen, because she wasn't as snarky as she usually was." She rolls her eyes a little. "She was actually sweet, which was so strange, telling me to keep doing what I was doing. She was convinced we would solve the world's problems, one day. That we'd some day cure cancer and diabetes and ALS and all those other incurables." Her eyes drift to the innocent magazine on the table. "I suppose I just hate we couldn't have done it before we lost her."

    Kara audibly swallows. "I'm sure she knows you tried," she says. "There was nothing you could have done."

    "If she'd just told me," Lena mutters, shaking her head. "The woman was so stubborn."

    Kara snorts, shaking her head, like she actually knows. "Thank you," she finally says.

    Lena looks bemused. "For what?"

    Kara just shakes her head. "Just, thank you, Lena," she says. "Thank you for all you've done, all you do, and all you will do. I get the feeling people don't tell you that enough."

    The compliment is unexpected, and Lena doesn't know how to respond.

    Kara saves her from any further awkward stuttering, asking her about another project, and Lena manages to put the interaction at the back of her mind.

    Kara, mercifully, lets her.

    Kara spends more time than she originally anticipates in Lena's lab. There's just something about the way she speaks about her work that makes Kara stay. She could probably listen to her talk for hours upon hours, losing herself in the cadence of Lena's voice.

    But, eventually, Kara has to leave, because Alex texts that they're done at the penthouse and Carter has managed to squirrel his way into getting some ice cream. He's sneaky like that, and Kara is actually quite proud he was able to convince Eliza and Alex to agree.

    Definitely her son.

    For a moment, Kara contemplates the merits of asking Lena to join them, but ultimately decides against it. There will be other times. Hopefully.

    But, first, Kara has something for Lena, and she asks the woman to accompany her back to the foyer so she can give it to her. Well, give them to her. It's easy enough to hand over the two wrapped parcels, one for Lena and the other for Sam - from Kara.

    "I think Alex would have teased me too much," Kara admits, rubbing a hand over the back of her neck. "Not that she won't find out about them eventually, but I - " she stops, deeply aware she's blushing.

    "Can I open mine?" Lena asks, and her voice is full of warmth, even a little shy. "While you're here."

    "Oh." Kara's heart rate picks up. "Yes, of course, it's yours, you can do whatever you want."

    Lena hands back Sam's present, and Kara holds it in her hands as Lena's fingers very carefully peel back the wrapping paper Kara has used. It has small cartoon puppies wearing Santa hats on it, and the repeated words, 'Santa Paws.' Kara thought it was cute, but now she's a little self-conscious of it.

    Lena helps when she says, "Cute," and finally reveals the drawing Kara sketched. It's on white cardboard which easily holds its shape, and Lena actually gasps when she sees it, her eyes widening right along with her smile. "Oh, Kara," she breathes.

    Kara shifts her weight from one foot to the other. "I - yeah, Alex sent me the picture Sam sent her, and I just - it's such a great picture, and I thought - is it okay?"

    "It's so much more than okay," Lena murmurs. "It's perfect. It's - wow, you're really talented, aren't you?"

    Kara lets out a laugh. "Lena, seriously? You hired me!"

    "I know, I know," Lena says, shaking her head. "And, obviously, I know you're talented, but this is - I have evidence right in my hands. This is beautiful, Kara. I can't thank you enough."

    "It's really nothing," Kara says, because it really is nothing. Just a few hours out of her day. It was definitely worth it to be able to see that smile on Lena's face.

    "Now, I feel terrible, because I haven't got you anything," Lena says, but her eyes never leave the pencil drawing. It's as if she's taking in every detail, studying each line and smudge, committing every little nuance to memory.

    "Hey," Kara waves her off, and then grins with a bit of mischief. "Plus, you don't yet know how much I'm going to be charging you," she adds with a wink, and positively delights in the sound of Lena's laugh. It's just so full, warm and wonderful.

    Kara wants to keep making her laugh.

    Lena sighs, content. "It really is lovely of you to do this," she says. "Thank you."

    "You already said that."

    Lena gestures to the present still in Kara's hands. "That better be a copy, or Sam's going to kill us both."

    "That was exactly my thinking," she says, amused. "Don't worry, though, you get the original."

    Lena raises an eyebrow. "Why?"

    "Excuse me?"

    "Why do I get the original?" Lena asks, and Kara curses herself for even bringing it up.

    "Um." Kara scratches the top of her head. "I wouldn't want Alex to think I'm making a move on her lady," she says, and immediately regrets it.

    "Oh?" Both eyebrows rise now. "Does that mean you're making a move on me?"

    Kara opens her mouth, and then closes it it. Opens it again. "Uh, no?"

    "You don't sound so sure about that," Lena points out.

    Kara shakes her head, steadying herself. She clears her throat. "I might not know Jack, but I wouldn't do that to him," she says, and Lena seems to go still at the mention of his name.

    "Jack," she echoes. "You're worried about Jack." She glances down, and Kara realises she's staring at the wedding rings Kara hasn't been able to take off. She's never had a reason to, but this moment merely proves it's unlikely anything could ever happen with someone, so long as she remains wearing them.

    Kara takes a step back. "You're not," she states.

    Lena sighs. "We're not - it's complicated."

    Kara knows complicated, and she shifts her body, opening it slightly and essentially offering Lena the chance to tell her about it if she wishes.

    It's a little surprising when she actually does. "We dated in college," Lena says. "I'm a few years younger than both him and Sam, and they - they were kind to me, and I loved him. But we were young, with different dreams, and things happened, and then we broke up." She picks at the edge of the card in her hands. "But he's established himself now, and he wants to try again, but I - "

    "You're not sure it's what you want anymore?"

    "I'm not sure what I want, at all," Lena admits. "How do you just know?"

    "You just do," Kara says, because she's learned that much. She was always certain of Cat, just unsure they would be allowed to work beyond what she believed in her heart.

    Lena audibly swallows. "Did you just know?"

    Kara looks down, her right hand's fingers twisting her pair of rings around. "Maybe not always," she admits. "It was hard in the beginning, but then - I think there's always just a part of you that knows, whether you like it or not."

    "So much of my life has been... difficult," Lena explains. "I just - it's nice to have something that's - "

    "Easy."

    "Does that sound horrible?" Lena asks. "It sounds horrible, doesn't it?"

    "No, it doesn't," Kara assures her, because there was a time when she dated someone to distract herself from the sheer terror of developing feelings for her boss. It had been easier and safer, and Kara would have allowed herself to lose herself in that relationship if Cat hadn't thrown an actual tantrum.

    "You don't have to say that, Kara," Lena says, shaking her head. "I know it's the safer option."

    "As opposed to what?" Kara asks, and it is a very, very dangerous question.

    Lena looks her right in the eye and says, "You're married."

    Kara was barely given time to get used to being married before she just wasn't anymore. It is such an odd thing to be a wife, but also not to be one. "I - " she starts, but stops, because she can't say she's not married. That would be an untruth. She's just married without a wife.

    Lena steps back. "What are you doing?"

    Frankly, Kara has no idea.

    It must show on her face, because Lena shakes her head. "And you wonder why I would choose someone easy," she mutters. She reaches for the present in Kara's hand. "You know where to find me when you figure it out," she says, and then spins on her heel, walking away.

    Kara watches her go, crease in her brow.

    Decisions to be made.

    Lena gets barely three metres before there are warm fingers on her wrist, halting her. She turns around to see Kara looking at her, clear conflict in her eyes.

    "You don't - I don't know - " Kara starts and stops. She actually looks as if she's in pain, and Lena wants nothing more than to ease it.

    "Kara," she whispers. "You don't have to do this."

    "I lost them," she says, and Lena goes still, a small gasp escaping from between her lips. "I lost - earlier this year, one day, I was just alone, and I don't know - I'm still figuring it out, okay. And I have a young son, and you're - I don't know if I'm - Lena."

    "Kara," Lena says again, stopping her. It hurts to see her struggle so much. "It's okay. I don't - hey, I don't expect anything from you. God, I'm so sorry."

    "No, you don't understand, I'm - "

    "Kara."

    She blinks behind her glasses, blue eyes wet and shining. "I have no idea what I'm doing," she whispers.

    Lena can't bear to see her looking so lost. "You don't have to know," she says, eyes dipping to look at Kara's collar. Her eyes are currently too blue; they almost hurt to look at them. "You don't - you have - there's time."

    "There is?" Kara asks.

    Lena reaches out to touch her, the fingers of her right hand gentle against the skin of Kara's cheek. "There is," Lena confirms, and they both probably need to hear it. Everything feels so intense, and Lena can't figure out why that is. They barely know each other. This is the first time she's even touching the woman. "There is," she says again, reiterating the meaning. Making sure Kara believes her.

    "Okay."

    "Okay."

    "I have to go."

    "Then you should."

    When Kara leaves, Lena makes her way to her office, two presents in hand and a certain war going on in her chest. Of all the things she expected to learn today, none of it was about Kara's actual relationship status or the way her skin feels under Lena's fingertips.

    Needless to say, Lena doesn't get any work done. Really, all she does is stare at the drawing Kara has given her, browse through Supergirl's virtual gallery and try in vain to come up with her next move.

    There is no next move.

    Or, whatever she's about to do next is probably going to be the wrong one. It's probably in her best interest to leave Kara alone; let her work through whatever she needs to work through for them even to consider something.

    Something.

    Just the idea of it tugs at the edges of Lena's mouth, pulling them upwards. It's like nothing she's experienced before. It's strange and unexpected, and she's twenty-five for goodness' sake. Some woman shouldn't be making her feel all twisted up inside.

    Especially not when she has -

    She has Jack.

    In whatever capacity she has him.

    Lena stares at the drawing again, absorbing every detail of her own face. She's had a few portraits done - she's a Luthor, you see - but this is the first time she's been captured in only graphite. It is a beautiful thing, to be given a snapshot of how Kara sees her.

    The problem, it seems, is that Kara wears glasses.

    Kara wouldn't exactly say she's distracted, but there's a very real part of her that isn't fully present when Carter picks through his Space box, pouring everything out on the table and marvelling over every item as if it's been years since he's seen them.

    At least he's managed to inherit Cat's dramatics.

    Carter is quiet as he focuses, and Kara chooses to watch him. It's really one of her favourite things to do. Always has been. Even before they had any kind of real relationship; he fascinated her. Just something about him.

    Everything about him, in fact.

    She decided, really early on, that she would do everything in her power to keep him safe and happy. Anything to keep him smiling that smile she loves so much.

    Dear God, she thinks she might actually be able to make his Christmas wish come true.

    Not any time soon, of course, but there's this feeling settling in her bones that, if not today, and if not with this particular woman; there will be a some day. She'll move on. She'll start living again.

    She might have even already started.

    "Mommy," Carter says, getting her attention. "Will we be able to go to the moon when I'm older?"

    Kara smiles, because trust her little space explorer to keep holding onto his dreams. "If you work really hard, you know you can do anything."

    Carter's little brow creases. "Does that mean you won't come with me?" he asks.

    "I'm not an astronaut, Carter."

    "Do I have to be an astronaut to go to the moon?"

    "As far as I'm aware, my heart."

    Carter seems to consider it. "Okay," he says, and then returns his attention to his book on the Space Race.

    Kara just sits and watches, focus entirely on this moment. There are so many other things she could be thinking about, and probably should, but there's simple comfort found in sitting in her son's presence and just enjoying him.

    She's still sitting there when Alex joins them, sliding onto the stool beside her and resting her chin on her shoulder. Together, they watch Carter mutter to himself, reading some words out loud.

    It's stupid cute.

    Alex knocks her head against Kara's. "I have a question."

    Kara hums. "Would you like to share it?"

    "What are we doing for New Year's?"

    "Is that the question?"

    "I - yes?"

    Kara thinks it over. Her last New Year was a subdued affair, just spent at home with Cat and Carter. He was out like a light by nine o'clock, and the two of them stood on their balcony when it was time, enjoying the fireworks. It was a good day, in her memory. "Didn't Lucy already call you about the party she and James are hosting?" Kara asks.

    "She did," Alex confirms. "Are you planning on going? Because, I thought we could all go."

    "All?"

    "You, me, Sam," Alex says; "Maybe Sam can invite Lena, if it'll make her more comfortable having someone she knows other than me there."

    Kara's heart rate immediately spikes at merely the mention of Lena's name. She can practically feel it thumping in her fingertips.

    "Kara?"

    "Hmm?"

    "Does that sound like a good plan?" Alex asks. "Will you - are you - " she stops and sighs, unable to find the words to phrase her question properly. "Is that something you'd be okay doing?"

    "Eliza would watch Carter?"

    "She would," Alex confirms. "I know Mom has a better social life than the two of us combined, but she's planning on staying home."

    Kara snorts, because Eliza really does socialise more than her daughters. "Okay."

    "Okay?"

    Kara makes a sound of confirmation as she returns her attention to Carter. "I guess I could do with a night out," she finds herself saying, surprising both herself and Alex.

    "Yeah?"

    Kara nods once. "I'm doing this thing where I'm starting to live my life again," she says. "Or, well, I'm trying to. Baby steps and all that."

    "I know your friends are going to love getting to see you," Alex tells her. "I know I've missed it."

    Kara knows they still need to have a proper sit-down and talk about everything that led up to their disjoint, but today isn't the day. "You just like that I bring you food," she lightly accuses.

    "You always did know all the best places," Alex says, sighing dreamily. "That thing you brought me the other day, from Wiley's... holy smokes, I would sell my soul for it."

    "Please don't," Kara says, almost immediately. "Your soul is priceless."

    Alex makes a choking sound. "What?"

    Kara just rests her own head against Alex's, soaking up her sister's presence. And Carter's.

    It's something close to perfect.

    Chapter 6

    VI

    Lena doesn't see Kara for the next few days.

    It isn't entirely by design - though, she's convinced she's not complaining - because there's another crisis with which she has to deal. She actually has to fly to Metropolis to handle an oversight that set the manufacturing of one of their upcoming devices extremely behind schedule.

    Kara texts only once while she's gone, and Lena thinks it's better that they're spending some time... thinking.

    Well, Lena spends a lot of time thinking. About Kara, and about what the woman represents. Because, in Metropolis, there is Jack.

    Jack, whom she almost forgets to contact at all. It's when she's on the phone with Sam talking about their plans for New Year's that Sam asks if Jack is going to be in National City that night.

    Lena suddenly hopes not.

    "Alex has these friends who are throwing a party," Sam tells her, stifling a yawn. "She wants us to go."

    "Us?" Lena asks, stretching out on her hotel room's entirely too large bed. The sheets feel like magic on her bare legs, and she would stay here forever if she could.

    "You and me," Sam says. "And Alex, obviously."

    Lena forces herself not to ask about Kara, as much as she wants to. "Who are these friends?"

    "I'm not entirely sure," Sam says. "She mentioned they work with Kara, but they've known one another longer."

    Ah. There it is. Kara's name.

    Lena's heart reacts, stuttering a little, and an involuntary smile tugs at her lips. She shouldn't be missing some woman she barely knows, but that's the feeling that fills her chest. It is both cold and warm, and she both loves and hates it.

    "And you want to go?" Lena asks. "Not just because Alex wants to?"

    "I actually do," Sam confirms. "I don't have plans, otherwise. Ruby's spending the night at Kristen's house, and we both know you would skip all the parties you've been invited to."

    "Ugh," she says. "We all know it would just be for appearances, and I would rather spend the evening in the comfort of my own home. Nobody asks me about my family that way."

    "I'm pretty sure Alex's friends have more tact," Sam says.

    "You really want me to come with you, don't you?"

    "I mean, I wouldn't be asking otherwise." Sam sighs. "Alex is - she's really just fucking weird, you know?"

    "Um."

    "When it comes to her friends, and when it comes to Kara," Sam explains. "Like there's this massive secret they all share that they won't tell, and I - I don't want to have to deal with all of that by myself. I want to meet Alex's people, and I want her to meet mine, so you should come, and we'll get drunk and enjoy being as young as we like to pretend we are."

    "I actually am young."

    "Shut up."

    Lena laughs. "Okay, we'll go."

    "Yeah?"

    Lena traps her bottom lip between her teeth, thoughtful. "Will - um, is - do you know if Kara will be there?"

    It's really a miracle Sam doesn't laugh at her. She's worryingly quiet, instead. "As far as I know, yes," she says, tone careful. "Lena?"

    "Hmm."

    "Please tell me you're not actually interested in a married woman."

    Lena closes her eyes. "She's - Kara isn't married anymore."

    "But she wears rings," Sam points out, as if Lena needs the reminder. "I've seen them myself."

    "I know, Sam," she says. "That's why I said she's not married anymore."

    "But, how could - oh." Sam lets out a breath. "Oh. That - yeah, that makes a lot more sense now. God. She's so young. Poor Kara."

    There are things Lena could say in response, but she's already said enough. It doesn't seem like something Kara broadcasts, and Lena suspects there are circumstances behind the death she wouldn't want getting out. Lena won't ask what Kara won't tell.

    Now that she knows where they both stand in the sense of 'I want you, too,' Lena's willing to wait. She's truly a patient woman.

    She's a lot of things.

    "Probably don't bring it up," Lena finds herself saying, recalling the conflict in Kara's eyes. The pain that continually lingers there, even when she's smiling that gorgeous smile of hers.

    "Not until she does," Sam vows, her voice low. There is something shared there, maybe, but Lena has always done Sam the courtesy of not bringing it up, either.

    This is something they do for each other.

    "Thank you, Sam."

    "For what?"

    Lena wouldn't we able to say. Lots of things. Everything. "Just, thank you," she says.

    "You're being weird," Sam says, and then promptly hangs up.

    Lena has just enough time to laugh before her phone vibrates with a received text from Sam.

    I love you too, you big weirdo ❤️

    Lena smiles, alone in her hotel room, and then sighs. With a simple navigation of her phone, she forces herself to call Jack. She won't hear the end of it if she doesn't.

    When Nia calls on what is the last Wednesday of the year, Kara is bursting with things to tell her, but forces herself not to. It is alarming just how much Kara would be willing to share about L's letters, Carter's old new obsession with space and Lena's smile.

    Instead, she says, "I actually have plans to go to a party on New Year's Eve," with all the disbelief she feels that she actually agreed to such a thing.

    She wouldn't be able to say she didn't agree just on the odd chance she sees Lena. She wouldn't admit it to anyone - let alone herself - but she's missed seeing her. They just - they have a lot to work through, it seems.

    At least, Kara does.

    "A party?" Nia questions, sounding both intrigued and proud. "That's great news, isn't it?"

    "I think so," Kara tells her. "I'm actually looking forward to it. A little bit. I'm trying not to feel guilty, because I know this is what my wife would want. To experience things again, even if they're without her."

    "I'm glad you've managed to reconcile that," Nia says. "It's a day-by-day thing, and you make progress as you do. Next year, you might even have someone to kiss."

    Kara laughs, mainly in slight alarm, because she suspects, if she plays her cards right, she might have already found someone. "Maybe," she allows.

    "Are you someone who makes resolutions?" Nia asks, moving them along.

    "Not really," she admits. "I like setting goals as much as the next person, but resolutions have never been a thing that have stuck. Nobody in my family pays much attention to them."

    "Do you reckon it's something you could start?"

    Kara considers it. "If you're expecting me to put 'find a new wife' on the list, then that's a hard no," she says, and Nia laughs. "I just don't see the point in making those kinds of plans anymore, I suppose. I planned for a lot longer with my wife than I got, and that didn't work out so well for me. It's just easier not to, I guess."

    "That makes sense," Nia says, which is lovely validation for Kara. "It's just that we're having a New Year's Resolution show, and a lot of our listeners are very invested in your finding happiness once more."

    Kara sighs. "Everyone remains under the impression I'm not happy," she says. "Or that I need to find love to reach that elusive happiness people believe I'm lacking."

    "Is that not the case?"

    Kara spends a moment thinking it over. "I think, at this stage in my life, I should be able to be happy with myself. Isn't that the ultimate thing? Being content in your own company, or something like that?"

    "Are you?"

    "You know, Nia," Kara says; "Ask me again next week and I might actually have a proper response for you."

    What Lena has learned in the years she's known Sam is that she doesn't do nervous well.

    Not at all.

    Not even a little bit.

    Alex texts her an address in downtown National City, and Lena has her car take them. Her driver offered to work tonight, claiming he needed the distraction while his daughter was spending the evening with his ex-wife. He's big and burly, and Lena usually doesn't bother to argue with Dave.

    "You have to like her," Sam tells her, expression deeply serious. "I don't know what I'll do if you don't."

    Did Lena also mention her best friend has a tendency to be overly-dramatic? "Everything is going to be fine," Lena assures her. "I mean, I'm used to people having preconceived ideas about me."

    Sam winces. "You remember that?"

    "Alex had a few words to say about me when you first told her you work for L-Corp? Yes, I remember."

    "She was uninformed," Sam says. "I made sure she learned all she needed to know."

    "And what was that?"

    "That there is no Sam without Lena, and I definitely wouldn't leave custody of Ruby to someone whom I thought was unworthy," Sam says, and makes a point of not looking at Lena at all.

    It's a good thing, too, because Lena's sure her facial expression must look horrendous. It's not something they talk about - mainly because of Lena - but Sam's just voiced something that's remained unspoken between the two of them for a long, long time.

    Lena chooses to take it as a blessing when they reach their destination and Dave pulls the car over to the side of the road. Sam just reaches for her hand and gives it a quick squeeze, and then they're both climbing out of the car and into the chilly night.

    Lena knows they're not going to speak about it again.

    Which is a good thing, too, because Lena's suddenly sure she'll be unable to form words again when she sees Kara. Sam assured her the New Year's Eve party would be something casual, which is why Lena's dressed down - well, as far as she can manage in designer jeans and a dark green cashmere sweater.

    Kara has, too, in a sense, but she's not in anything that has paint on it or is oversized.

    Nope.

    She's wearing dark grey fitted pants and a white button-up with tiny gold polka dots that fits just perfectly snug to her form.

    Sam just laughs at Lena's reaction to the sight of her, snickering and whispering, "Total beefcake." She's still laughing when Alex spots them and immediately makes her way through the various bodies gathered in the main living space of James and Lucy's impressive apartment.

    Lena is forced to pay attention when Sam's nails actually dig into her arm. She would roll her eyes if she weren't sympathetic to Sam's position. She obviously wants this meeting to go well - needs it to - so Lena will tease her about how neurotic she's being later.

    Alex is both exactly what Lena expected and absolutely not. She's very obviously smitten with Sam, her entire body turned towards her at all times. She's a bit of a nerd, quirky in a way you wouldn't notice if you just focused on the stoic persona she portrays.

    Her eyes are sharp and intelligent, and she has the kind of secret smile that matches her sister's. Though, what does strike Lena as odd is how many times she glances over her shoulder to check on Kara.

    Kara, who is moving around the room with another brunette woman, smile on her face and champagne in her hand. Apparently, Lena looks for her almost as much as Alex does.

    Then, Kara is right in front of her, grinning a little dopily. She's definitely already had quite a bit to drink, apparently - or she just has a low tolerance. Lena is introduced to the woman with her, Lucy, and then they're both gone just as quickly. Even Sam looks a little bewildered.

    Alex shrugs. "It's been a while since Kara's been out and about," she explains. "Lucy's very obviously excited."

    Sam nods in understanding, and Lena forces herself not to ask more questions. For all she knows, Kara hasn't talked to Alex about anything that may or may not be happening between the two of them.

    "Chances are also high she's going to fade before midnight, anyway," Alex adds. "I can count on one hand the number of times she's actually been awake long enough to welcome in the new year."

    "Seriously?" Sam asks.

    Alex nods, and then stops talking about Kara completely, as if she's just caught herself doing it. Sam sends Lena a look, as if to say, see what I mean, and then Alex asks about Ruby.

    Lena leaves them a few minutes later, hunting for some stronger alcohol. She's not looking for Kara, not really, but she doesn't exactly complain when she happens upon the woman in the middle of a heated debate with someone named Siobhan about Salvador Dali. They're surrounded by a small group of people, both of them talking animatedly as they attempt to get their points across.

    Lena doesn't really pay too much attention to anything Kara is actually saying, because she's rather lovely to look at, and this is really the first time Lena has been allowed to study her in all her glory.

    Lena's also aware she's not the only one.

    Maybe it's really for the best Kara still wears her wedding ring, because Lena suspects people are lying in wait for the moment she confirms she's single and looking.

    Though, things are looking in Lena's favour when Kara drifts towards her once she's satisfied she's won whatever argument she was having with the Siobhan woman.

    "You are drunk, my dear," Lena tells her, quietly marvelling at the way Kara gets very close to her, but does not touch her.

    Kara lifts her hand to indicate the space between her thumb and forefinger. "Just a little," she says. "I haven't had this much to drink in, like, many months."

    Lena smiles, because Kara is very cute like this. Almost child-like, with bright eyes and a wide smile. "Then you should have paced yourself," she points out.

    "Lucy's trying to get me drunk enough to sing karaoke," Kara says, and Lena's convinced she's joking until Lena's caught in a discussion with Alex about a paper she wrote on a little while later, and Lucy brings out the karaoke machine.

    "Oh, boy," Alex says, rolling her eyes. "We're in for it, now."

    "Why?" Lena asks, carefully sipping her drink.

    Alex shrugs slightly, eyes flicking from Kara to where Sam is enjoying playing bartender and preparing cocktails for a handful of people. She's very in her element, and Alex constantly looks relieved things are working out in her favour. "Because Lucy only takes out that thing when she wants to get Kara to sing," she finally says.

    "Because she's really good, or because she's really bad?"

    Alex lets out an unexpected laugh, relaxing further into the couch they're sharing with another two people on the other side of her. "The former, thank god, because, as much trouble as Lucy has actually getting Kara up there; she has staying power once she's caught the karaoke bug."

    "She's a mic hog?" Lena asks, exaggerating her shock.

    "With good reason," Alex says, sounding amused, defensive and proud all at once.

    Lena already believes her, but it really is kind of Kara to prove her sister right. She starts with a duet with Lucy, one voice easily out-singing the other. But they have fun, and Lena hasn't ever seen Kara look that young before.

    Alex seems equally affected, but in a different way. She looks as if she never expected to see that smile and hear that brand of laughter ever again. But there it is, and it is beautiful.

    "You don't talk about her," Lena says after the third song Kara has sang as a solo, her voice soft and crooning. She brings it up only because she knows it bothers Sam.

    Alex glances at her. "No, I don't suppose I do," she says. "I'm not sure what things I can say or not, so it's easier to say as little as possible."

    Lena nods, because it actually sounds as if there are official documents barring Alex from saying things, and isn't that an interesting situation?

    "I just follow her lead," Alex says. "So, whatever she wants to tell people; I will too."

    "She's lucky to have you."

    Alex's face pinches, pain flashing in her eyes. It's gone just as quickly, and she snorts. "Make sure to remind her, yeah," she says, intending for it to be a joke, but Lena's sure she means it.

    Lena makes a note to do just that, eyes searching for Kara once more. Except, she's not where she was a few minutes ago, and a quick sweep of the room reveals nothing. Lena doesn't panic, because that would be ridiculous, but she does excuse herself from Alex with the intention of finding the woman's sister.

    Thankfully, it doesn't take her all that long. Kara is on the balcony, standing alone against the edge and looking out into National City as if she can find the answers to the Universe just in the buildings and lights. Lena almost doesn't want to interrupt her musing, but she glances over her shoulder, and Lena is powerless to keep away.

    Kara is magnetic, it seems.

    "You found me," Kara says, voice a little raspy after all the singing she's done.

    "I found you," Lena confirms. "Aren't you cold?" she asks, because it's chilly out here and Kara isn't wearing anything over her shirt.

    Not that Lena is complaining, of course.

    "I'm a polar bear," Kara immediately says, grinning. "Cute, but deadly,."

    "Indeed."

    Kara's eyebrows rise as she turns her body to face Lena. Her hip is pressed against the balcony's railing, arms in front of her. "You think I'm cute?"

    "You think you're cute," Lena tells her, but, yes, she thinks Kara is cute.

    Kara doesn't reply. She just kind of stands there and keeps her gaze focused on everything Lena. The silence exists between them for long enough that Lena grows antsy. She feels exposed with the way Kara is watching her; as if she can read everything about her like it's written all over her face.

    "What are you doing?" Lena asks, needing to speak.

    Kara smiles, looking just left side of tipsy. Her eyes are shining, mouth pulled into a sly smile. "Just looking."

    "At what?"

    "At you," Kara says, smile turning dopey. "You're very pretty."

    "Is that so?"

    "I really like your eyes," Kara continues. "They're so green." She blinks. "And your mouth."

    "Also green?"

    Kara giggles. "Also pretty," she says. "I like it."

    Lena is amused, charmed and slightly embarrassed all rolled into one, and she shakes her head at how silly she feels. "What are you doing?" Lena asks again.

    "Nothing," Kara tells her. "Just looking at you."

    "Because I'm pretty?"

    "No." Kara shakes her head hard enough to make herself dizzy.

    Lena frowns slightly, because that's -

    Kara sways forward. "You're beautiful," she says. "It is devastating. All I want to do is make art when I look at you. My fingers twitch with the desire to sketch and paint, and I want to touch every part of you, because, Lena, I swear it's as if you've been sculpted by the gods."

    Lena blinks against the light in Kara's eyes, wondering how she can be so eloquent and inebriated at the same time. It's really not fair. Nor is the way she gets to say things like that, just so easily.

    "Lena?"

    "Hmm?"

    "Can I paint you?"

    "Excuse me?"

    Kara sways forward again, getting perilously closer. "Paint you."

    "Paint me," she echoes, heart thudding in her chest. "I - that seems - "

    There is a God, it seems, when the balcony door slides open and a group of people come tumbling out. They're loud and obnoxious, and their arrival seems to sober Kara right up. She snaps back, eyes a little wide. "I - Lena, I'm so sorry," she says. "That was so - "

    "Kara."

    "I'm going to go," she says, nodding once. Before Lena can even get her to stay and talk about this thing some more, Kara has ducked away, weaved her way through the new group and disappeared from sight.

    Lena knows too much about disappointment, and there's a very real part of her that wants to go after her. But it's too scary, just putting herself out there. It's probably in her best interest to remain where she is, out here in the cool night air. The freshness should do her some good, surely.

    Not really.

    Lena listens to the group that just emerged, hearing them complain about a man named Mike, and then passing around what looks like a joint. She feels impossibly old, all of a sudden. Or young.

    Somewhere in the middle.

    Nowhere.

    Everywhere.

    With a sigh, she glances at her wrist for the time. Not long to go now, and she's standing out here, trying to convince herself she doesn't want what she actually does.

    Kara.

    She wants Kara.

    It's the last day of the year, she's far too young to be moping about, and there is a woman who thinks she's beautiful. Who wants to paint her - whatever that means. What on Earth is she doing out here?

    Lena spins around quickly - too quickly, based on the wave of dizziness that hits her - and stalks back inside. The room looks even more packed than it was when she left, and Lena reasons everyone is gathering for the impending countdown.

    Pairing up and hoping for a shared kiss.

    Well.

    She's on the lookout, too, isn't she?

    Only, when Lena does eventually find Kara with the intention of claiming a midnight kiss for herself; the blonde is curled up and fast asleep on the living room couch.

    Kara can count on one hand the number of times she's had a hangover - in the last year, at least - but it is a horrible, wonderful thing. She wakes up on Alex's couch, half-hanging off, and she feels so wonderfully young for the first time in a long time. It is blissful, and she would love nothing more than to remain where she is for all of eternity.

    But she has an impatient bladder.

    Kara has to give herself a bit of a pep talk to get to her feet, and the world tilts with her. She's a little off balance as she makes her way to Alex's bathroom, stifling a yawn and just managing to avoid knocking her shin against the low table. She's done it before. There's actually a scar on her leg from one night in college.

    Hmm.

    That was a wild night.

    Last night was wild in a different way, and Kara slips into the bathroom as quietly as she can. She stares at herself in the mirror for a moment, barely recognising herself, before she shakes her head and uses the toilet.

    It's the new year.

    She also probably made a complete fool of herself in front of Lena.

    Okay.

    No.

    She definitely did. What is wrong with her? She should know not to go around asking women if she can paint them. That's borderline creepy, and would be even worse if she were a man.

    No wonder Lena looked so alarmed.

    Kara washes her hands, and then splashes her face with some cold water. She's definitely awake now, despite the yawn that immediately attacks her body. She shakes her head, flattens her hair, and then leaves the bathroom.

    She'll have to apologise to Lena.

    She also has to figure out if she's actually ready to want someone else this way.

    But, first, she needs breakfast.

    And coffee.

    There is a box of pastries waiting for Lena on her desk when she gets into work on the second day of the new year. She immediately knows whom they're from, and she tosses a look over her shoulder at Jess, who's followed her into her office.

    Jess smiles, all innocence. "Special delivery," she says. "There's also a note."

    Lena waits for Jess to finish telling her the day's schedule and leave, before she takes her seat behind her desk and retrieves Kara's note.

    The handwriting is loopy, and far too illegible for someone who is an actual artist. It brings a smile to her face before she's even read the words, heart rate spiking.

    Lena,

    I just wanted to say sorry if I made you uncomfortable the other night. Inebriation is never an excuse. Please accept these pastries as a token of my apology. Jess might have clued me in to which is your favourite bakery in the city, so please enjoy.

    Kara

    Lena reads the note another two times, before she drags the box closer and peaks inside, smile growing at the sight of all the calories. What would her mother say?

    What would her mother say?

    Lillian wants her to find someone to settle down with so badly, and Lena can't figure out why. She doesn't, for a second, believe her mother just wants her to be happy. That would be - just, no.

    With a quick shake of her head, Lena gets to her feet and leaves her office. For a moment, she contemplates just going to Sam's office, but there's a very real part of her that wants to lay eyes on Kara right now.

    She heads for the elevator, deeply aware of Jess' eyes on her, knowing smile on her face.

    "Stop that," Lena grumbles, heat on her cheeks.

    "I haven't said anything," Jess defends, sounding amused.

    Lena just keeps walking, calling an elevator with her knuckle and waiting in silence. She doesn't even know what she's going to say when she sees Kara. Thank you, perhaps, and tell her she didn't actually make Lena uncomfortable. Just surprised her, is all.

    By the time she gets to the foyer, she's unusually nervous. There's a lot the two of them need to talk about. So much Lena would like Kara to know about her and where she stands. She doesn't think that'll happen today, but she still wants -

    She just wants.

    Lena doesn't hesitate as she ducks into the enclosure, gaze focused down. She really doesn't want to see what the piece looks like before it's ready. She calls Kara's name, and then hears a paintbrush drop.

    "Sorry," Lena says, wincing.

    Kara laughs, and it is a glorious sound. "It's okay," she says. "Hold on, I'm coming down." There's the sound of feet descending a ladder, and then Lena hears her say, "Bud, you going to be okay in here for a little while?"

    Lena's heart beats double-time when a smile voice says, "Of course, Mommy."

    Kara laughs again. "All right, all right," she says. "We'll just be outside, okay?"

    "Okay."

    "Can you say hello to Miss Luthor, please?"

    "Hello, Miss Luthor."

    Lena looks up just enough to see a little boy hunkered in the corner, knees bent to his chest, tablet in his lap and a small hand waving at her. "Hello there," she returns, and he immediately drops his attention again.

    It is - God, he's so cute.

    Kara moves closer to her then, and Lena allows herself to be ushered out, so she can lift her head properly. The door closes behind Kara, and then they're standing right in front of each other. Entirely too close.

    "Sorry about that," Kara says. "I know it's not really professional to bring your kid to work, but he's still out of school and Eliza was busy, but Alex is coming to get him once her shift ends.

    Now that Lena has actually touched Kara before, she has less qualms about doing it again. Her fingers rest on Kara's forearm, stopping her rambling. "It's okay," she says. "He's very cute."

    "He knows it, too," Kara quips, her smile soft and lovely. "At this point, telling him would just go to his little big head."

    Lena wants to know more - particularly about their respective ages - but she actually came here for a specific reason. "I hope you know the pastries were unnecessary," she says, and Kara's smile grows.

    "Well, you're welcome."

    Lena squeezes her forearm before dropping her hand. "I just mean, you didn't make me uncomfortable."

    "You sure about that?" Kara asks; "Because you haven't looked me in the eye even once."

    Lena stills. She hasn't, has she? "They're just very blue," she finally says.

    "And yours are very green," Kara returns, almost automatically.

    "And you find them very pretty, apparently."

    Kara groans good-naturedly, which makes Lena laugh. "I almost forgot about that part."

    "You also had quite the opinion about my mouth, if I recall," Lena says, enjoying the blush on Kara's cheeks. She also doesn't miss the way Kara's eyes flick down for a beat. Her own breath catches.

    "Well," Kara says. "You are a very stunning woman, Lena."

    Lena audibly swallows, because the way Kara says it makes it sound like fact. Something undeniable and true.

    "I've made you uncomfortable again," Kara says, taking a step back.

    "No," Lena hurries to say, hand darting out to touch Kara again. If she's not careful, she'll get too used to it and just never stop. "I just - nobody ever just says things like that. Not to me."

    "That's a shame," Kara muses, and her eyes flit over Lena's face. "It baffles me that more people aren't constantly struck dumb when they look at you."

    Lena's own face flares. Oh my God. How old is she? This is honestly just ridiculous. "Kara," she says. "You can't just say things like that."

    "Why not?" she asks, and Lena wonders if she's being intentionally obtuse. "They're true."

    Well. Lena won't even dream of arguing with that. "Okay."

    Kara grins. "Okay."

    "I need to get back to work," Lena says.

    "I should, too," Kara replies. "Also need to make sure the rascal hasn't got into my paints."

    Lena nods, taking a step back. "I didn't say it before, but thank you," she says. "For the pastries."

    "Of course, Lena," Kara says. "Anything to make you smile."

    "There are easier ways to do that," Lena informs her.

    "Oh?" Kara asks, clearly intrigued. "Tell me more."

    Lena doesn't. Just smiles behind her secrets and spins around, already walking away. She gets a few metres before she risks a look over her shoulder. Kara is still standing there, watching her and smiling as if she knows something Lena doesn't.

    Which, given how things are playing out, is probably true.

    Lena just smiles back, and continues on her way to the elevators, heart racing. She feels both young and old, giddy and mellow all at the same time.

    This is just something else Kara does to her.

    It's on their way home after an afternoon spent with Alex that Carter says, "I thought you liked L."

    Kara glances at him through the rearview mirror. "Excuse me?"

    "L," he says, as if that'll clear that up for her. "I thought you liked her."

    Kara blinks, suddenly sure she's missing something important. "I do, Carter," she says, because she does. L is an unwitting part of her daily routine. Whomever she is.

    "Then why were you flirting with Miss Luthor?" he asks, and there's accusation in his voice that reminds her too much of his mother.

    "Carter."

    "What about L?" he presses. "She's perfect for you, Mommy."

    Kara sighs. "But we don't know anything about her," she points out.

    "It doesn't matter," he says, and his brow is creased in disapproval. "I like her. You like her. And she likes us."

    "Carter," she says again. "We haven't even met her."

    "Because you haven't even tried," he says, and his voice rises in a way she's rarely heard. "You haven't even tried to find her."

    "Carter."

    He folds his arms across his chest, turning his face away, and Kara can't quite figure out where or how it went so wrong. Isn't this what they all wanted? Carter, Alex, Eliza, KX3P and all their listeners. She's found someone, and she actually likes that someone.

    Kara just sighs again, choosing not to fight this battle while they're in a moving enclosed space. It feels dangerous to talk to her son about feelings she's still trying to understand. She just didn't anticipate he would be so against Lena. It's unlike him.

    But, what is like him is to latch onto a person or idea and hold onto it as tightly as possible. L is his choice, and this is exactly what Kara was so worried about.

    She supposes she's been lucky that he hasn't pushed for L, but it looks as if that luck has run out.

    That idea is just expounded when they get to Eliza's house, and Carter stomps up the stairs to his bedroom without saying a word to her or Eliza.

    Eliza, who just looks as stumped as Kara feels when she turns curious eyes at Kara. "What's that all about?"

    Kara just shrugs and heads to her own bedroom.

    As if she wasn't already conflicted enough.

    It isn't anything unusual for Lena to work late, and it also isn't anything foreign for Sam to come bursting into her office on any given day. What is different is the look on her face, and the way she moves around Lena's desk, ignoring all her questions, and tunes into KX3P on Lena's computer.

    The first thing they hear is Carter's voice, small and a little whiny as he says, "And she keeps smiling at the most random things."

    Lena tosses a look at Sam, curious.

    Nia tells Carter, "But I thought this is what you wanted," as carefully as she can manage.

    "But it's wrong," he says, and his voice is higher than they've ever heard before. Panicked in a way that makes Lena deeply uncomfortable. "She's wrong," he adds. "Mommy's not supposed to like someone else. She's not right. I'm - I have to - " he stops, choking on an unexpected sob. "She doesn't fit the list, Nia."

    Lena feels for him in all the worst ways, just now realising what must have happened. His mother must be expressing interest in a woman and Carter is figuring out just what that means.

    Sam places a hand on Lena's shoulder, squeezing gently.

    "But if she makes your mother happy - " Nia tries.

    "No!" Carter screeches. "They're not - it's not supposed to happen this way."

    "Carter."

    "You have to help me," he says. "Mommy can't - she's supposed to - Mommy." He actually yelps, there's the sound of a fumble, and then a different voice is on the line.

    "Nia," Carter's mother says, and she sounds exhausted. "This probably isn't a good time."

    "I - yes, he doesn't seem on board with everything, all of a sudden."

    Carter's mother hums. "He saw me just talking to one woman the other day, and now he's convinced I'm - " she stops. "Look, I should talk to him. It was - I'm sorry."

    "It's okay," Nia says, just in time for Carter's mother to hang up.

    Sam immediately reduces the volume to background noise, and the two of them sit in silence for a full minute. Lena can't quite get the pitch of Carter's voice out of her mind, replaying in a terrifying way.

    "You knew something like this would happen, didn't you?" Sam finally says, turning slightly and sitting on Lena's desk, facing her. "Right at the beginning."

    Lena leans her head back. "We can talk about wanting change as much as we want, but the truth remains we're very rarely ready for it when it comes."

    Sam swings her legs a little. "Imagine being the woman Carter's Mom was talking to," she murmurs, and something niggles at the back of Lena's mind. Huh. "Do you think they'll be okay?" Sam asks, her right hand disappearing behind her leg, almost tucking it under her thigh.

    "I hope so," Lena says.

    "What would you tell them, if you could?" Sam asks, and there's something particular about the tone of her voice. Lena hears it, but she doesn't quite register it. "If you could send them the words to make it better, what would you say?"

    Lena closes her eyes, thinking it over. "I think what she needs to hear right now is that she's done nothing wrong. Any steps forward in the romantic sense are going to be an adjustment to them both, and the fact there's been a reaction from him shouldn't be surprising or alarming. The worst thing she could do now is take steps back in her progress, you know? Carter is young, and he's experienced some horrible things, but he is young, and she needs to do things for herself as much as she needs to do them for her son." She sighs. "Life is such a long time to spend alone."

    Sam gently kicks her chair. "You're not alone," she makes sure to say.

    "I know, Sam," she says, opening one eye. "But there's a difference between my having you, and your having Alex."

    Sam could ask about Jack, but she doesn't.

    "It isn't an easy thing," Lena says, and she's talking to Carter's mother again. "Moving on. Finding things to make you happy. I can talk a good game as much as the next person, but I still struggle. I feel the losses; carrying them with me every day, and they catch you out in the worst ways. Especially when you least expect them." She breathes out. "You know, when I first went to live at Luthor Manor, I still had this idea of what a perfect family was. I wanted a Mom and Dad and older brother like any other kid who'd just been uprooted from all she knew, and it was so disappointing.

    "And, isn't that life? Nothing ever works out the way we imagine, envision, desperately hope for. I learned that for the first time at the age of four, and I've been learning it ever since. We just learn to live with it and make the most of it, and that's what Carter and his mother have to go through. They have to go through it, because they don't have a choice. There is an end of the grief tunnel, I'm sure of it."

    "Are you?" Sam asks, and Lena's eyes snap towards her. "I just mean - Lena, you never talk about it."

    "My mother has been dead for twenty-one years, Sam," she points out.

    "We both know I'm not talking about your mother."

    Lena sucks in a breath. "There's an end to the grief tunnel," she repeats. "I just haven't reached it yet. And I know it's not fair to expect better of anyone else, but Carter's mother is prepared to resume living life. And Carter does want that. He just wants it on his own terms, and that isn't how the world works. He's seven. He has time to figure it out, which I'm sure he will. He's already learning that life isn't fair."

    "It's the number one thing I wish I could save Ruby from," Sam admits.

    "Is that why you won't let Alex and Ruby meet?" Lena asks. "You're worried she'll end up hurt if you don't work out?"

    "Which one?" Sam asks, but it's rhetorical. "I'm certain they'll get on like a house on fire. Alex is really good with children, and she's got this spirit about her that - she's just really great. I'm attached, and I know I'll be devastated if something goes wrong. Forgive me if I'm worried about my kid experiencing a similar pain."

    "You don't have to explain yourself to me."

    "What's worse is that Alex isn't even pushing for it," Sam bursts out. "Like, she'll ask about Ruby and how she's doing, and she'll mention things she'd like to show her, but she just - God, she's just really great."

    "You said that."

    "We deserve good things," Sam declares with a nod. "You, me, Alex, Kara, Carter's mother, Carter, literally everyone good in this world." She tilts her head back. "Speaking of Carter's mother, are you going to write her a letter now?"

    "Oh my God, Sam."

    "Come on, Lena," Sam presses. "We both know you're interested."

    "I'm not," she counters.

    "I've known you for ten years, you know? I know when you're lying."

    "I'm not interested in her," Lena repeats. "I don't even know her. I just admire her, for how brave she's been. I know she must be feeling conflicted, especially after Carter's reaction, but life deserves to be lived, and maybe she needs to stop thinking about what other people would want for her and start deciding for herself what she wants."

    "Even if it's this other random woman?" Sam presses.

    Lena rolls her eyes. "I'm not heartbroken over it, if that's what you're getting at."

    "Because of Kara?"

    "Oh, my God."

    "I saw the way you were looking at each other at the party," she says. "It was positively indecent. It was like she was memorising every inch of you." Sam looks delighted all of a sudden. "I am certain she'd be a generous lover."

    "Please tell me you didn't just say that."

    "She would worship you."

    "Please stop."

    "She must have stamina, too."

    "Sam, stop talking."

    "And she - "

    Lena shoves her legs away, soft enough that Sam could resist. She just drops off the desk, laughing. "I have work to do," Lena tells her.

    "You should go home."

    "You first."

    Sam winks at her, and then leaves her office. Lena watches her go, feeling both heavy and light. She's tempted to increase the volume on KX3P again, but decides against it.

    Wherever Carter and his mother are in the world; Lena hopes they're okay.

    Kara can count on one hand the number of times she and Carter have yelled at each other. He's normally very reasonable for a seven-year-old, amenable and understanding when presented with facts. He doesn't usually attach his emotions to his decisions.

    Until now, it seems.

    Kara feels like the most awful human being when he actually starts crying, and she doesn't know how to make it clear to him that it is not realistic to hedge all his bets on a letter from a woman they probably won't ever meet.

    Carter cries himself to sleep on Wednesday night, and then doesn't say a word to her all of Thursday. And, on Friday, he just sits, sullen, in the corner of her enclosure, and Kara gives up trying to cheer him up when she's received her thirteenth monosyllabic response.

    She just paints, glancing at him every few minutes. It sits heavy on her shoulders that he's struggling so much. She knows it has nothing to do with Lena, and she's quite certain he's also relieved L isn't actually a real person.

    Kara suspects they're going to have to go back to the grief counsellor. They probably shouldn't have stopped seeing her, anyway.

    "Mommy?"

    Kara's head snaps up, convinced she's imagining his speaking to her. "What's up, Bud?"

    "May I go to the toilet?"

    Kara doesn't exactly deflate, but it is a little disappointing.

    Anyway.

    She starts to climb down the ladder, but he's already on his feet and moving to the exit.

    "I can go by myself," he declares and disappears before she's even stepped onto the ground. She rushes out behind him, just in time to see him disappear into the men's toilets.

    She sighs.

    Edward waves at her, and then points at his own eyes before pointing at the doors in which Carter's just slipped. Kara smiles, grateful, and then slinks back into the enclosure. She's just so exhausted. Why can't anything she does be right?

    She slumps onto the second step of the ladder and pulls out her phone. Best if she sees if Dr Gomes can squeeze them in.

    If ever Cat's name will be good for something.

    When Lena steps out of the elevator on the foyer level, she has a minimal list of expectations. They're constantly in crisis, so she's half-listening to Jess and Brainy fill her in on the newest one, and half-focusing on where her feet are carrying her.

    Admittedly, there's a healthy chunk of both those 'halves' sneaking looks at the enclosure in the hopes of catching a glimpse of Kara.

    Which is really why she doesn't notice when an unsuspecting little human being ends up barrelling right into her. He gets knocked back far more than she does, and she immediately reaches out to steady him at his shoulder.

    "You okay there?" she asks, checking him for injury.

    "Sorry," he mumbles, not quite looking up. "I wasn't watching."

    "That's okay," she tells him, because it is. Obviously, she already knows he's Kara's son, but it's the first interaction they've had, but she's not nervous. Nope. Not at all. What's there to be nervous about? "I wasn't paying attention, either."

    "Mommy says we should always watch where we're going."

    "Your Mommy is correct," she agrees; "but I won't tell if you won't."

    His head lifts, beaming at her. But it falters in the next breath. "Oh."

    Lena tilts her head to the side, confused by the reaction. "You didn't hurt yourself, did you?" she asks, suddenly worried.

    He studies her closely and long enough to make anyone else uncomfortable, but Lena recognises the curiosity. "Do you like cats?" he asks, completely out of the blue.

    "I - yes, I do like cats," she says, which is true - though, there's no point in mentioning she has a slight allergy to them. "Especially the big ones."

    "Lions?"

    "And leopards," she confirms.

    "My favourite is the tiger," he tells her. "I like stripes."

    "Does that mean you like zebras, too?"

    He bounces a little, his smile back in full-force. "They're so cool," he says. "Aunt Alex took me to the zoo, and we saw all the animals. The giraffes are so tall. Even taller than Mommy."

    Indeed.

    Nothing she expected.

    Because she stands there for entirely too long and talks wild animals with a tiny boy with these adorable front teeth and eyes so bright that they match Kara's in spirit, if not colour.

    They even switch to talking about the animated movie Madagascar, and Brainy does a pretty spot-on imitation of King Julien that has them all laughing. Jess doesn't even pressure her to hurry, which says a lot about the crisis they're facing, as well as the cuteness in front of them.

    He's in the middle of telling them about his favourite Gloria scene when he lets out a little squeak, says, "I have to go," and then rushes off towards where Lena has just seen Kara emerge from her small enclosure.

    He runs straight at her, wrapping his arms tightly around her waist and gets a completely bewildered expression from her. Lena watches for a while, seeing him talk rapidly at her, and then point in Lena's direction.

    Kara immediately tells him to stop pointing, and they're both flushed red when they see her watching them.

    Then they wave, completely in sync. Which is just - Lena melts a little.

    Lena waves back, and then finally allows Jess and Brainy to continue leading her out the front doors to where she has to put on her CEO-boots and make stuffy men cower in her presence.

    All in a day's work.

    They get ice-cream with Alex, and Kara is not the only one completely thrown by Carter's very sudden mood change. Alex keeps shooting looks at her as Carter prattles on about Big Cats and zebras and when they can go to the zoo again.

    And if Miss Luthor can come with them next time.

    Kara nearly chokes on her own ice-cream when Carter says that, and Alex laughs loudly as she pats Kara's back just a little too hard.

    "Uh huh, Kara, can Miss Luthor come with us?" Alex asks, clearly teasing her.

    Kara shoots her a look, but she's just doubly amused. She knows Carter and Lena talked, and Lena has already managed to win him over somehow. Kara knew he would like her, if he just took the time to know her, but even she has to admit this complete turnaround is unexpected.

    Not that Kara is complaining, of course. Carter definitely needs to talk to more people, and the two of them share several interests, it seems. She suspects her son might end up a man of science, so the more people he has to help with that; the better.

    Not that Kara anticipates Lena being around for so long.

    No.

    That would be -

    That would be presumptuous.

    Kara sighs, resuming eating her ice-cream. Carter is distracted with his tales, and Alex is humouring him enough that Kara can pull out her phone and text Lena.

    She's missed her, which is such a foreign thing.

    My son literally won't shut up about you, she sends, feeling a little thrill at the mere idea of talking to Lena. There's something happening between them, though it's unnamed. It's almost as if they're waiting for the other to take that all-important step forward.

    Lena doesn't reply until much later, when Kara has already tucked Carter into bed and has curled up on the couch in front of the fire with her book. Eliza is dozing in her armchair, and there's just something so wonderful and simple about the moment.

    Then Lena texts back, and Kara's heart literally skips a beat in her chest at the sight of her name. She laughs to herself, because, seriously, she's twenty-seven years old.

    All good things, I hope.

    As if anyone could ever say something bad about you, Lena.

    I'm a Luthor, darling, Lena texts back, and Kara can practically hear the disdain in her voice. It comes with the territory, I'm afraid.

    Well, the rat is very fond of you, Kara tells her. He wanted me to ask you if you'd be interested in joining us the next time we go to the zoo. She panics that she's overstepped when it takes Lena a little longer to reply.

    Kara, dear, did your son ask me out on a date before you did?

    And, wow, okay, Kara definitely wasn't ready for that. Her mouth opens and closes as she tries to come up with a suitable response. Because the answer is yes, Carter did.

    He's braver than I am, Lena. She breathes out. Also, is that something I'd even be allowed to do?

    Well, please tell him that I would love to accompany him to the zoo the next time you go. There's a short wait. You're allowed to do whatever you want to do, Kara.

    Even rob a bank?

    I don't, for a second, think you actually want to do that.

    What I want to do is take you out, but I can't realistically tell if I'm actually ready for that, Kara reveals. She knows Lena will understand, and Kara's decided to be truthful about where she stands. She's learned a lot about herself in relationships, and she knows what mistakes not to make.

    She just needs to figure out if she's actually ready. It's as simple as that.

    Then we'll go to the zoo, Kara, and we'll have a nice time, Lena sends. It doesn't have to mean anything more than that. I told you there's time.

    Kara breathes out in relief, and then easily switches their topic of conversation to something lighter: the book she's reading.

    They text for nearly an hour, and Kara doesn't read a word of Shantaram. She'll catch up on it another time, she decides, pouting slightly when Lena mentions she should get some sleep if she's going to be up in time to make it to Ruby's early morning soccer game.
