    Kara reluctantly bids her goodnight, and then sits for a while. Eliza is asleep in her chair, and Kara should probably send her to bed, and turn in herself. Maybe she'll be lucky enough to get some proper sleep tonight.

    Lena slides her sunglasses over her eyes as she emerges from her car, feet squelching on the grass. She's just on time, given Sam sent her almost fifteen reminders to arrive before Alex does.

    Today's the day Alex and Ruby meet, apparently, and Sam is nervous to the point of being irritating. Lena's glad to be the person Sam can show all her crazy, of course, but Lena just wants to shake some sense into her.

    Sam might not be able to see it for herself, but Lena can, as clear as day. Alex is so obviously committed; it's not even funny. And Lena's sure Alex and Ruby will get on well. They both care about Sam; there's no other way.

    Still, it's Lena who ends up sitting with Alex as Sam prowls the touchline and yells at the field in short bursts. Lena just lounges in her foldable chair, sipping her iced tea, and feels very soccer-mom-ish.

    "Is she always like this?" Alex asks, leaning over and looking a little alarmed at the sheer volume of Sam's voice. "This, um, passionate."

    Lena hums. "She's very invested, Alex," she says. "Ruby wins, Ruby's happy. Ruby loses, she's not. Sam is very interested in making sure her kid stays happy."

    "Lena, she just called a kid on the other team a mouth-breather."

    "Technically, she's not wrong," Lena says. "Dean very obviously has a deviated nasal septum."

    "Oh, my God."

    Lena hands her a bottle of iced tea from the cooler Sam brought. "You're a doctor," she says. "Maybe you should take a look at him."

    "I'm a neurosurgeon."

    "I'm pretty sure the nasal cavity is very close to the brain," Lena says.

    "I suppose I could refer him to someone," Alex muses. "I kind of make a point of staying away from treating people I know."

    Lena glances at her. "There's definitely a story there."

    Alex presses her lips together. "Kara mentioned that she told you her - " she stops. "Um. She came to me, asking for me to do something to help. Anything. I told her I would take a look, just because I couldn't - she has these eyes, you know, and I made the mistake of giving her the impression something could be done." Alex sighs heavily. "But there was nothing any of us could do, and I - I can't ever forget the look of devastation and betrayal she left me with.

    "We didn't talk for a while. Then time ran out, and I couldn't face her, and she couldn't face me, and it just - we've really struggled to get back to anything resembling normal since our worlds imploded." Alex opens her bottle and takes a sip. "So, yeah, I stay far from people I know. It's just safer for my relationships that way."

    Lena keeps her eyes on Alex. "You don't still think she blames you, do you?"

    Alex shrugs. "We haven't talked about it," she says; "but there are moments when I can tell she still holds onto that resentment."

    Lena audibly swallows. "She loves you," she says, and Alex offers her a grateful smile. "I - I hate my brother," she says after a while, and Alex's eyebrows rise at the unexpected confession. "Or, I really, really want to hate him, but he's - I mean, he's my brother, you know?"

    Alex remains silent.

    "I should hate him," she says; "because he tried to take me down with him - instead of him - when I had no idea he was doing anything illegal. That's immediately grounds for hatred, but it's - it's a lot harder to hate the person I remember playing chess with me in his hotdog pyjamas."

    Alex smiles, gentle and understanding. "I have pizza slices on mine," she says. "Kara got us matching sets a few years ago."

    Lena shouldn't be picturing Kara in her pyjamas with the woman's sister sitting right beside her, but she can't help it. It must be an adorable sight.

    Alex opens her mouth to say something, but they both startle at Sam's sudden shout, Alex almost dropping her drink. Sam is practically running along the field, arms waving, because Ruby has the ball, headed for the goal, and -

    Sam practically leaps two feet in the air when Ruby shoots and scores, and Lena just laughs at her enthusiasm. Alex just looks lost in love, which is - yeah.

    Alex offers Lena a smile, and then goes to stand with Sam to cheer on Ruby for the rest of the game.

    It allows Lena to imagine a time in the future. Maybe Kara and her son would join them one Saturday, and Ruby could have an entire team of supporters. It's a nice thought, filling her with warmth.

    Without giving it much thought, she takes out her phone and snaps a picture of Sam and Alex's backs and sends it to Kara with the caption, how long do you think we have until the wedding?

    Kara texts back immediately with a plethora of excited emojis, rings and hearts. My guess is as good as yours, she adds. I'll let you know when Alex asks me to go engagement ring shopping with her. It might even be next week.

    Sam would pass out, Lena tells her. But I get the feeling she'd say yes.

    Chapter 7

    VII

    The letter arrives on Sunday morning with the rest of the mail. Carter's interest in the letters coming in from KX3P has waned - which is a mercy, truly - but sometimes letters slip through and catch his attention.

    It helps that both Kara and Carter would be able to identify L's handwriting just about anywhere.

    It's become some kind of routine for the two of them to curl up together and read L's letters, and this one is no different.

    Carter climbs into her lap as soon as she's settled into her seat, and her arms wrap around his waist. He places the letter in her hands, as carefully as he can manage after the first time, and then they read in silence.

    Dear Carter's Mom,

    I hope you won't mind, but I listened to Wednesday's interrupted segment, and thought writing a letter would help. I suspect I know what you're both going through, because I've learned a few things about myself over the years. We can talk about wanting change as much as we want, but the truth remains we're very rarely ready for it when it comes.

    I think what you need to hear right now is that you've done nothing wrong. Any steps forward in the romantic sense are going to be an adjustment to you both, and the fact there's been a reaction from Carter shouldn't be surprising or alarming. The worst thing you could do now is take steps back in your progress, you know? Carter is young, and he's experienced some horrible things, but he IS young, and you need to do things for yourself as much as you need to do them for your son. Life is such a long time to spend alone.

    I think we've had it established by now that it isn't an easy thing. Moving on. Finding things to make you happy. I can talk a good game as much as the next person, but I still struggle. I feel the losses; carrying them with me every day, and they catch you out in the worst ways. Especially when you least expect them.

    You know, when I first went to live with my new family, I still had this idea of what a perfect family was. I wanted a Mom and Dad and older brother like any other kid who'd just been uprooted from all she knew, and it was so disappointing.

    And, isn't that life? Nothing ever works out the way we imagine, envision, desperately hope for. I learned that for the first time at the age of four, and I've been learning it ever since. We just learn to live with it and make the most of it, and that's what you and Carter have to go through. You HAVE to go through it, because you don't have a choice.

    There is an end of the grief tunnel, I'm sure of it. I just haven't reached it yet. And I know it's not fair to expect better of anyone else, but you are prepared to resume living life. And Carter does want that. He just wants it on his own terms, but that isn't how the world works. He's seven. He has time to figure it out, which I'm sure he will. He's already learning that life isn't fair.

    I want to end by repeating that you deserve good things. I also suppose my interest in you is oddly misplaced. I know we don't necessarily know each other, but I wanted to tell you that I just admire you, for how brave you've been. I know you must be feeling conflicted, especially after Carter's reaction, but life deserves to be lived, and maybe you need to stop thinking about what other people would want for you and start deciding for yourself what you want.

    Please take care,
     L

    Kara doesn't know what to make of the letter at first. She gets the impression this letter was solely for her eyes, but maybe it's not such a bad thing that Carter has read it, as well.

    Carter turns in her lap to look at her face. "She also had a new family," he says, sounding thoughtful. "Like you, and like me."

    Kara smooths a hand over his curly hair. "It seems so," she says.

    "She sounds sad," he points out. "We should find her, so we can give her a hug."

    Kara sighs, because she kind of wants the same thing. "I'll see what I can do, okay?"

    "Okay," he says, and then hops right out of her lap and scrambles out of the room.

    Kara sits a while, rereading the letter and trying to figure out how she feels about what's been written.

    This is the heaviest letter. Yet, always, L knows exactly what Kara needs to hear. The words are written with no pretence, aren't even sugar-coated, and this is what Kara needs.

    With a sigh, she gets to her feet and carries the letter upstairs to her bedroom. She intends to put it with L's other letters in the drawer of her nightstand. It's a special spot, holding some of her most important possessions.

    The first card Carter ever made for her. A copy of her adoption certificate. The first pair of glasses her foster father got for her.

    Cat's wedding ring.

    Kara freezes when she sees it, heart beating a little faster in her chest. Kara might have buried Cat with it, but the woman was very particular about her end-of-life wishes.

    Without giving it too much thought, Kara reaches for her own wedding rings and slowly pulls them off. She expects to feel something - horror, relief, guilt or even lighter - but nothing changes.

    There is just a weight missing from her hand. She doesn't panic. Not even when she lays the rings beside Cat's and closes the drawer. Not when she turns around and leaves the room, and not even when Eliza does a bit of a double-take when she notices Kara's bare hand.

    It is a huge step for her, and she actually manages to get some sleep.

    If Lena had to consider the parts of her she would be willing to share with the people around her, this wouldn't be one of them.

    Even Sam doesn't get this part of her. Nobody does.

    Not until Kara.

    There are several dates in the year that creep up on Lena in the worst ways. She avoids thought of them at all costs, but the calendar doesn't care about her feelings, and the days roll around every year.

    Lena is quiet, morose, when she arrives at work on Monday morning, and Jess picks up on her mood immediately. "Cancel everything," Lena declares and then closes herself in her office.

    She would remain blissfully alone if Kara didn't decide to surprise her with lunch. She pokes her head inside, looking concerned, and Lena doesn't have the heart to send her away. After a wave of her hand, Kara slides inside and presents her spoils.

    "Potstickers," Kara says, moving to sit on Lena's couch. "They make everything better."

    Lena doesn't quite believe that, but she gets to her feet and goes to join Kara on the couch. "I'm sorry if I'm terrible company," she says. "I'm not - it's not a good day."

    "That's okay," Kara tells her, and she sounds so genuine. "I have days like that, too. You don't have to explain yourself to me. You should eat something, though. As much as you can manage."

    Lena watches her as she takes out the food, spreading a rather abundant selection over the low table. "That's, um - a lot."

    "I love potstickers," is Kara's only explanation, and Lena won't even question it.

    "Will you tell me about your day?" Lena asks, taking the chopsticks from Kara when she hands them over.

    Kara waits long enough to make sure she's put something in her mouth to start her tale about the case of the missing paint colour. "You laugh, but it was a serious matter, Lena," Kara says, pouting. "I needed it for a critical blend, and it just - it was nowhere to be found."

    Lena listens as Kara describes her detective skills and subsequent search for the colour she still hasn't found. Her eyes are bright, hands and voice animated, and Lena can almost forget what today is.

    But not quite.

    It's while Kara is trying to decide which filling of potsticker she wants next that Lena very quietly says, "I lost a baby."

    Kara immediately abandons her debate of flavours.

    "Maybe not quite a baby," she says, more to herself. "I've never quite figured out what gestational age makes what's inside a baby, but I - I lost her. Maybe the fact I even knew she was a girl counts as a baby, right?"

    Kara audibly swallows, and Lena sees her rest her hand in the space between them. An offer. Just there if Lena wants it.

    It is the first time Lena notices the hand is bare.

    Lena doesn't move. "It's why everything with Jack is so complicated," she says. "We were - God, we were so young. I had no business believing I could be a mother. Not at nineteen. I was still growing; still figuring myself out. I would have been terrible."

    Kara sits perfectly still, silent even though it looks as she has so much to say.

    "It's almost funny, you know," she says. "I started to accept it; to plan for this new life I was going to end up living. Because it alters everything you've had planned for your own life, you know? Everything changes. Everything. And I started to love this person growing inside of me, so of course that's when - there was - " she stops, her arms closing around her abdomen. Protecting herself. "I woke up one night and she was gone. Just like that."

    Lena's memories of that night are spotty at times, but she has some things she'll never be able to forget. Like how much blood is a lot of blood, and how quiet death without life can be. Like how pain can start in one part of her body, and spread to every extremity. How it can linger.

    Lena has memories of feeling empty in every sense of the word, and she can't -

    Warm arms slide around her shoulders, ripping her from her thoughts, and she goes stiff. For just a moment, she can't recall where she is or whom she's with. But then Kara is whispering something nonsensical in her ear, and Lena finds herself relaxing into Kara's embrace.

    She's really very warm, is Lena's first thought. And the second is that she could have really used comfort like this that night.

    Kara holds her for a good chunk of time, and it feels important and monumental in a way that an ordinary hug wouldn't be. Not between two women who are so clearly interested in each other.

    Lena isn't really thinking about that right now, though she's a little embarrassed.

    "Don't be," Kara says, as if she can read her mind. "I, um, did you name her?" she asks, soft like a secret.

    Lena smiles gently. "No," she says. "I had a list, though. Girls have those, right: all the names they'd want to name their child."

    Kara nods. "I think I've had my list since I has eight," she reveals. "Added and taken away a few, of course."

    "Were you responsible for naming your son?" Lena asks, brow creased at the realisation she doesn't actually know Kara's son's name.

    Kara shakes her head. "I think his name would be Noah, if I had my way," she reveals. "But, no, I wasn't involved in naming him at all." She smiles at Lena's confused expression. "I wasn't yet in their lives when my wife gave birth to him."

    "Oh." There is something there. Something right there in front of Lena, but she just can't see it.

    What she can see is Kara's face, entirely too close to hers. She can see the small scar on her brow, and she can smell her perfume: something sweet and a little floral.

    "Hey," Kara says, smile barely there. "You okay?"

    "I'm sorry about all of this," she says.

    "Please don't do that," Kara counters. "This is never something you have to apologise for, okay?" She knocks her head gently against Lena's. "I'm just - I'm sorry you had to go through that."

    Lena breathes deeply. "I want that, you know," she says. "I want the spouse and the family and the school runs and the lack of sleep and - just, I want all of it."

    "You'll have it," Kara declares, and Lena is inclined to believe her.

    Lena's voice is barely audible when she says, "Even if I can't have children of my own?"

    Kara must hear the diagnosis in her voice, because her hug tightens. "Well, I mean, there are always options," she says carefully. "You will have the family of your dreams, Lena. I know it."

    "You're very kind, Kara."

    "I'm also usually right."

    "Thank you." Lena sighs. "I'm sure this isn't what you expected when you brought me lunch. Thank you for that, too, by the way."

    "I just want to know you," Kara tells her. "All of you. I don't get to pick and choose."

    "Well, I'm not always doom and gloom," she says, burying her face in her hands. "I must be a sight."

    "For sore eyes, maybe," Kara immediately says, small grin on her face. "You truly are frighteningly beautiful."

    Lena feels heat on her cheeks. "Still want to paint me?" she asks, in an attempt to lighten the mood.

    Kara doesn't quite allow it. "It would be an honour," she says, ever so serious. "May I?"

    Lena blinks. "Right now?"

    Kara smiles. "Any time you want, Lena."

    'Any time,' turns out to be the very next day.

    Now, Kara probably wouldn't admit to being nervous, but there's a very real part of her that doesn't want to mess up this piece. It's Lena. It has to be perfect.

    It's Lena, so it's already going to be.

    Kara invites her to the gallery, because it's easier than going to the penthouse or even Midvale. There's too much to explain, and Kara just wants to paint.

    She wants to paint Lena.

    On canvas, sure, but she also has this inexplicable urge to get her paints on Lena's skin. She is a canvas, herself.

    She's also visibly nervous when she arrives. It's late enough that Kara's sent everyone home, and it's just the two of them. Kara opens the door for her when she knocks, and then locks it, hoping the action doesn't come across as too creepy. She just wants to keep them safe.

    Kara turns around to find Lena looking at the paintings against the left wall, her back perfectly straight and her focus on a piece Kara painted when she was fifteen years old. It's not one of Kara's favourites - not by a long shot - but it's Eliza's favourite, and she insisted Kara display it.

    With the way Lena's looking at it, Kara might reconsider her stance.

    "You were young here, weren't you?" Lena says, glancing at her. "You're - it looks young."

    Kara moves towards her. "It's the one I painted when my adoption was confirmed," she explains. "It was - it was also around the time we lost Jeremiah."

    "You can see your conflict."

    Kara looks at the painting, narrowing her eyes slightly, because the colours are conflicting. It's one of the reasons she's not its biggest fan, but she supposes that's a story in itself. "Sometimes, I still feel that way." She pauses. "A lot of the time, actually."

    Lena turns her body to face her. "Including right now?"

    It is the easiest thing to say, "No."

    Lena keeps her eyes on her, watching her as if she can see the truth just in her features. "We don't have to do this, you know?"

    "I want to," Kara says, because she really, really does. "I just - do you want something to drink?"

    "What's the strongest thing you have?"

    "Uh, coffee?"

    "Seriously?"

    Kara grins, relaxing slightly. "Whiskey?" she offers. "I think we have Scotch; I'm not sure. Alex is the one who stocks the bar."

    It takes another fifteen minutes to get to the studio, Lena constantly stopping to study different paintings and ask questions of them. Eventually, Kara is able to pour her a drink, hand it to her, and then continue to set up her paints.

    Lena splits her attention between watching her and looking around the studio. It's really quite a mess, Kara taking full advantage of the term 'artistic space.'

    "So, how does this work?" Lena eventually asks. "Are you going to draw me like one of your French girls?"

    Kara gives her a perplexed look, and then lets out a laugh when she realises to what Lena is referring. "While I appreciate your reference, uh, no, I'm not."

    "Because I'm Irish?"

    Kara rolls her eyes, but can't help the way her body thrums at the lilt in Lena's voice, her accent just peeking through. "It's actually pretty simple," she says. "You just sit on the couch in as comfortable a position as you want, I'll snap a few pictures, sketch an outline, and um, we'll see how much painting I can get done before you get sick of sitting here with me."

    Lena eyes her. "Will I be allowed to talk?" she asks.

    "Of course."

    Lena nods just once, and then carefully takes a seat on the rather garish blue couch Kara has in here. It's comfortable, though, and that's what counts.

    Kara works in silence, moving around with her camera in her hands. If she can get the snapshots quickly, then they can both relax that bit more. Because Lena just looks so stiff as she sits there, holding herself perfectly still, as if one wrong move will alter Kara's painting completely.

    It won't, but Kara's determined not to tease her about it.

    "It's not a portrait," Kara tells her as she sets her camera on a counter, satisfied with what she's managed to capture. "I don't really do those. Not in the literal sense, at least."

    Lena's head tilts to the side. "So, how are you going to paint... me?"

    "In warm colours," Kara says, reaching for a stick of graphite. Her fingers twitch, gaze on Lena. She's waited for this opportunity since the first day she laid eyes on her: to be able to put down on paper the beauty Lena wears so easily.

    "Warm colours," Lena echoes, sounding sceptical.

    "You'll see," Kara assures her, and Lena nods, satisfied.

    The silence that follows is surprisingly easy. Comfortable. Kara usually doesn't like having people around when she works, but Lena seems to be an exception. The woman understands losing herself in her work more than most, and Kara appreciates that.

    Though, everyone has a limit, and it's been nearly fourteen minutes of quiet when Lena very carefully says, "Tell me where you got the name Supergirl."

    Kara's hand freezes where it's just getting the curve of Lena's nose. "I - um." She sighs. "It was my wife's idea," she finally says, and Lena's facial expression both softens and drops simultaneously.

    "Oh."

    Kara slowly gestures around them. "All of this was actually her idea," she explains. "I was - I wasn't much of an artist in the public sense when we met. I didn't think I was all that good, to be honest, even though I went to school for it, but she, um, didn't really give me the excuse of hiding anymore. So, she gave me this name one random day as a bit of joke, I think, but she also gave me the belief and opportunity to pursue it full time.

    "It's all so big and important and such a part of me, in a sense, and yeah, I've struggled with holding onto this thing I love because so much of it is now attached to her. It's just taken a while to figure out that's not a bad thing." Kara could say more, but she doesn't. There's only so much you can say about your late wife to the woman you intend to pursue, surely. She doesn't think, for a second, Lena wouldn't let her, but there will be time for that in the future.

    In this moment, instead, she just paints.

    Lena just watches her.

    Kara's not an idiot - usually - so she knows there's something happening here. It's charged and demanding to be addressed, but they're both playing something like 'Chicken' to see who's going to move first. As if they're barrelling towards each other like unstoppable forces, and they're just waiting to see if the other is going to save them, or let them crash head-on.

    Kara blinks first, because she was always going to. She knows Lena is waiting on her - she's the one more visibly burdened with grief - and Lena has been so kind and understanding.

    So, Kara blinks, drops her paintbrush with a bit of a thud, and then moves towards Lena.

    In another life, she might consider herself a predator moving towards prey, but Lena's eyes are focused on her, open and welcome. Bracing for what's to come.

    What would come, if Kara didn't actually trip over some tarp on her floor and actually fall onto her hands and knees. "Fudge nuggets," she mutters under her breath.

    Lena can't seem to decide whether to laugh, check if she's okay or make a dirty comment about Kara's position.

    Kara huffs out a breath, checking her own hands for damage. Those are the money-makers, after all. "You can laugh if you want to," Kara tells her, only slightly annoyed, mainly because she's so embarrassed.

    Lena lets out a single soft laugh, and it's a lovely sound. "I didn't know you were this clumsy," she points out.

    "Oh, totally," she says, not bothering to get to her feet as she shuffles on her knees the rest of the way to the couch. "There are first aid kits everywhere, just for me."

    Something happens to Lena's face, almost as if she's having a thought that just won't catch. It spends a moment hovering there, before she shakes her head, clearing it, and then shifts to make some space for Kara on the couch.

    "What were you even doing?" Lena asks her, as if she doesn't already know.

    "Well, Lena, if you must know, I was going to tell you there isn't a blend of green on this good earth that matches your eyes, and then I was going to kiss you," she says. "But then that happened, and I don't even know - that was just supposed to be a lot more smooth and romantic."

    Lena is suddenly very quiet, and Kara might be pleased with herself if she hadn't just managed to screw this all up.

    Kara sighs, already planning her escape, but then Lena's hand settles over one of her own, fingers cool against her skin.

    "Why don't we try again tomorrow?" Lena offers. "No pretences. No nothing. Just you and me, no tarps in the vicinity."

    Kara laughs. "You'd want that?" she asks. "Still?"

    Lena nods, ducking her head a little. "I would, Kara," she confirms.

    "I - yeah, okay," Kara says, heart beating rapidly. She doesn't even bother to wonder if she's going to be able to get any sleep tonight.

    Lena barely manages any sleep.

    She tries, of course, but she's too wired - and excited - about what the next day could bring. She suddenly feels her age, and isn't that a terrifying, amazing thing?

    Little sleep would usually equate to her being in a mood but Lena can't help feeling light and unburdened as she makes her way to L-Corp in the morning, heart beating a little too fast to be strictly normal. It's a new feeling. Not something she's ever allowed herself to experience. Being a Luthor in high school, and then being so young in college, didn't really give her the opportunity to experience crushes and first loves without the burden of family and expectations.

    It feels different today.

    She feels ready to take those steps towards Kara. The dance has been fun, of course, but Lena is ready to keep her feet still and have Kara standing right in front of her.

    What a cliché.

    Lena practically rolls her eyes at herself as she arrives at the foyer, spotting Kara standing at the far left of the building and immediately starting towards her. Kara has her back to her, shoulder casually leaning against the wall as she talks on the phone.

    Lena uses the opportunity just to look at her, taking in the length of her hair down her back and the lines of her legs in her jeans.

    It's honestly not fair that she gets to look that good in casual wear.

    "No, I have a few more things to do in the city before I come home," Kara is saying into the phone, and Lena debates getting her attention before she's done. She wants to see Kara's smile.

    She wants to taste it.

    "I have to stop by the gallery, and then make sure Alex has actually slept in the last twenty-four hours," Kara tells whoever is on the other end of the line, and Lena's smile blooms, only to fade to nothing with Kara's next sentence. "Uh huh, well, you can tell Carter I'll be home in time for Nia's call, so he doesn't have to worry."

    It is -

    God. It's like the entire world has just screeched to a halt.

    Wait.

    Did Kara just say -

    "He's not allowed to answer that phone, Eliza," Kara says. "Especially after last week. It's really only a matter of time before people end up figuring out who we are."

    Lena takes a step back, and then another. She turns right around and walks away without Kara ever seeing her.

    It makes no sense.

    And, yet, it makes all the sense.

    Lena's nostrils flare, because of course Kara is Carter's mother. It's been right there in front of Lena's face, but she hasn't been able to see it.

    Carter's mother has a mother and sister, currently living with her mother after the death of her wife. Recently back at work, meeting new people and learning things about herself.

    Also, the fucking llamas.

    Lena almost snorts at how ridiculous she's been. How could she not see it? No wonder Kara's been so cagey in the past. She knew Lena listened to her segment on KX3P.

    Kara is Carter's mother.

    Kara is Carter's mother.

    Lena stops walking, heart thumping. This can't - she can't -

    They can't do this. She's Lena Luthor for goodness' sake, broken in places people can't even see. She's the last thing Kara needs. Definitely the last thing Carter needs.

    If she's supposed to be the person Carter mentioned to Nia, then Carter said it himself just last week. It's not even a question.

    Lena believes with all her heart those two precious souls deserve the entire world, and Lena is never going to be the one to be able to give it to them.

    Decision made, she spins right back around and marches back to Kara.

    She's determined.

    It has to be done.

    It's for the best.

    The first thing Kara registers when she hangs up the phone with Eliza is the sound of heels, steps loud and determined. She turns around, eyes finding Lena immediately, and the smile that spreads across her face is involuntary.

    It takes Kara entirely too long to realise Lena isn't smiling back, which, yeah, why isn't Lena smiling?

    Kara's smile has softened by the time Lena is standing in front of her. "Good morning," she says, because it is a good morning. Carter is happy and healthy, and there's a beautiful woman standing in front of her.

    "Morning," Lena says, and her voice doesn't hold the warmth it usually does when she's talking to Kara.

    Okay.

    Something definitely happened.

    "Are you - " Kara starts to ask, but Lena interrupts her.

    "You haven't sent me an invoice."

    Kara blinks. "Excuse me?"

    "For the work you're doing," Lena says. "The first time we spoke, you said you would charge me for the work, but I haven't received any kind of bill."

    Kara's brow creases. "I was kidding," she says, because she's pretty sure Lena knew then and knows right now that she was joking. "This is a favour, remember?"

    "That's an abysmal way to run a business, Kara," Lena says, tone sharp, and Kara feels completely off balance. What could have happened between last night and this morning?

    "It's not about the money, Lena," she says, because it's not. Even if Kara weren't currently absurdly rich, this piece is a favour.

    "This is a transaction," Lena says. "You do the work, and I pay you. That's how it works." She pauses. "Between professionals."

    Now, Kara has been called oblivious several times in her life, but she's receiving the meaning of those words very clearly right now. "Professionals," Kara echoes.

    "Because things should remain professional," Lena says, nodding her head. "Which is why I require an invoice."

    Kara takes a step back, deeply confused. When and how did everything go so wrong? "Yeah, okay," she says. "I'll send you an invoice."

    "To my email," Lena says, and Kara hears that she isn't to contact her through her private phone number. What is going on? Seriously. What is actually happening?

    "Your email," Kara repeats dumbly.

    "Oh, come on, Kara," Lena says, voice devoid of emotion. "It's not that hard."

    Kara doesn't even know why she flinches, but she does. She hasn't been on the receiving end of such disdain in such a long time, and she doesn't even know where it's come from. It takes a moment to school her features, because she's so out of practice. "Okay," Kara says.

    Lena nods once, and then spins around without another word. Kara just watches her walk away, somewhat convinced she just woke up in a parallel universe. What other explanation could there possibly be? Kara certainly doesn't know.

    Maybe Sam would know.

    Sam must know. She knows everything about Lena. Kara's even been a little envious of how much Sam has been let into Lena's life, and Kara wants that. She also wants to figure out what's happened in the last twelve hours. Did she do something wrong and Lena's just not telling her?

    Kara isn't really worried about running into Lena as she makes her way to what she knows is Sam's office, just from the plaque on the door. She's seated behind her desk when Kara pokes her head inside, and she looks predictably surprised by Kara's presence.

    Kara's never visited her before.

    "Hey," Kara says; "got a minute?"

    "For you, always," Sam invites her inside. "This is a surprise. What can I do for you?"

    "I - " Kara starts and stops, taking in the office. "You haven't, um, noticed anything off about Lena this morning, have you?"

    "Off?"

    "I don't know," Kara says, stepping closer to the desk but making no move to take a seat. She's too wired to be able to sit still. "She was just... off."

    Sam's brow furrows. "She was fine when I talked to her on the phone on my way in," she says. "Maybe a little hopped up on coffee, though."

    Kara sighs. "I don't understand," she says. "We were fine last night."

    Sam's eyebrows rise. "Last night? What happened last night?"

    "Nothing," Kara squeaks out, feeling heat on her cheeks. She looks around again, eyes eventually settling on Sam's desk. "So, this is where your magic happens, huh?"

    "It's super boring magic," Sam complains. "But enough about me; give me the details."

    "Nothing happened," Kara says, which is unfortunately true. "But something could have."

    Sam positively lights up. "You like her."

    Kara steps closer, eyes squinting a little as her gaze lands on something on Sam's desk. "I - yeah, I mean, she's great, who wouldn't?"

    "No, you like like her, don't you?"

    Kara walks around to be able to read the words properly, and Sam watches her curiously.

    "Are you okay?" Sam asks when Kara hasn't said a word in a full minute.

    Kara points at a piece of paper on Sam's desk, fingers trembling as some kind of sick truth starts to seep into her bones. "Is that - Sam, is that your handwriting?"

    "What?"

    "This here, is it your handwriting?" Kara asks, and her voice is a little high.

    "What, yeah," Sam says, clearly confused. "Horrendous, right? Ruby says my words look like little worms."

    Kara turns to look at her as if she barely recognises her. "That's your handwriting," she says, and it - why is that -

    That's Sam's handwriting.

    But, dear God, it's also L's handwriting.

    It doesn't make sense. Nothing makes sense. Why would - Sam couldn't have written those letters, but the handwriting is unmistakable. It's just that the L in the letters can't be Sam, because the letters refer to someone else. They just don't fit her. They fit -

    Kara suddenly feels sick.

    They fit Lena.

    Kara sucks in a sharp breath.

    Sam looks visibly worried. "Kara, hey, are you okay?"

    Kara steps back when Sam reaches out to touch her. "What - what am I doing here?" she asks. "What is this? Who are you?"

    "Kara, what are you talking about?"

    "Is it all - what is - " She rounds on Sam. "Oh, my God, did Alex put you up to this?" she asks, horrified. "All some ploy to get me out of the house and living again? Giving me some reason to keep going beyond my son?"

    "Kara, I honestly have no idea what you're talking about."

    "I can't believe this," she says. "I can't - what did - I liked her." Her voice is high and panicked, overwhelmed with far too many realisations at once. "I liked them both. My - my son likes her.

    Sam gets to her feet, arms outstretched, because it's pretty obvious Kara is losing it. "Kara, please, I don't know what you're saying."

    "You know," Kara accuses. "You both do. God dammit, you all do."

    "Kara?"

    "No," she snaps, shaking her head. "I don't - how could you do this to me? How could you?"

    "Kara?"

    "No." And then she's marching right out of the office. She's aware of Sam following behind her, but there's little else in her mind beyond seeing Lena.

    Seeing L.

    She bursts right into Lena's office, which is a surprise to herself, Lena, Sam and Jess. Lena startles enough that her pen actually flies out of her hand.

    Kara just stands there and stares at her.

    Slowly, Lena gets to her feet, because the silence must be unnerving. "Kara, what on Earth are you doing?"

    "Looking at you," Kara says, and she's sure she's not the only one who hears the wonder in her voice. "That's all."

    "Kara?"

    "You're even better than I imagined," she whispers, and then she's gone, spinning on her heel and walking right out.

    She needs to be anywhere but here.

    "What just happened?" Lena asks, eyes darting between Sam and Jess as soon as Kara is gone. "What was that? Sam?"

    Sam looks equally baffled. "I don't know," she says. "She just came into my office asking if I noticed something off with you, and then she asked about my handwriting, and then she was blowing up and coming here and now she's gone."

    "What?"

    "What what?"

    Lena sighs and looks at her assistant. "Jess, it's okay," she says. "You can leave us."

    Jess looks between them for a moment, and then quietly walks out, gently closing the door behind her.

    Sam barely waits to hear the click before she says, "You're acting weird."

    "What?"

    "Kara was right," she says, and Lena flinches at merely the sound of her name. Sam definitely doesn't miss it, and her eyes narrow. "What happened?"

    "Nothing."

    "That is a lie," Sam says, and her arms cross over her chest. It's her Mom-pose, and it has terrified her and Ruby for some years now. "Tell me what's wrong."

    "Nothing."

    Sam tilts her head to the side, which is definitely the pose that'll get her answers. "I'm going to ask you one more time," she says. "What happened with Kara this morning that had her coming to my office and asking me if something was wrong with you?"

    Lena retakes her seat, sighing. "Nothing, really," she says. "I just asked for an invoice for the piece."

    "A what?"

    "The bill for the artwork she's working on," Lena says, and she can't even look at Sam.

    "Okay... I don't see where it all went so wrong," she says. "Were you mean? Did you do it in a mean way?"

    "I'm not Ruby, Sam," Lena points out, irritated.

    "Then stop acting like a teenager."

    "I told her we had to keep things professional, okay," Lena snaps, jaw clenched. "I asked for the invoice, and then I told her to email me, and that's that, okay?"

    Sam stares at her in disbelief. "No, not okay," she says, dropping her hands. "Why would you - you just - you told me how much you like her just the other day. What happened?"

    "Nothing happened."

    "But something almost did," Sam says. "I don't understand, Lena."

    "What's there to understand?" Lena deflects, determined to get back to work. "I hired her to do some work for me, and I have to pay for it. It's really a simple transaction, Sam. Don't you do them every day?"

    "There," Sam points. "That tone and those words. You're being - "

    "What?"

    "You're being a Luthor."

    Lena goes still at the sound of the words. "Well, that's what I am, isn't it?"

    "Don't you dare," Sam says. "Don't you dare belittle my best friend by equating her to the members of that family."

    "Sam, please."

    "No," Sam snaps, and Lena very rarely sees her this riled up. "You're going to stop pushing us all away and tell me what the hell is going on."

    Lena breathes deeply. "I don't meet the expectations of the list."

    "What?"

    "I don't meet the expectations of the list," she repeats, as if it'll make the words make more sense.

    "What list?" Sam asks. "What are you talking about?"

    "Carter's list."

    Sam just looks even more confused. "What does Carter have to do with anything? What is happening? Has everyone in this place lost their damn minds?"

    "I am not patient," she says, and Sam looks even more lost. "I'm younger than her. I don't make her laugh. I am not immune to her puppy dog eyes. I suppose I have a lot of money. Perhaps I'm considered smart. I'm allergic to cats." She shakes her head. "We are not compatible."

    Sam doesn't say anything for a full minute. "You're making absolutely no sense."

    "Don't you see, Sam?" Lena says, and her voice sounds ugly to her own ears. "This is who I am. Me. Just me. What could I possibly offer them?"

    "Who? Who are you talking about?"

    "Carter," Lena shouts, because why isn't Sam getting it? "Carter's mother."

    "What do they have to do with anything?"

    "They have everything to do with everything," Lena says. "And I know Carter wouldn't choose me."

    "For what?"

    "To be the new wife."

    Sam looks at her as if she's lost her mind and, honestly, Lena feels it in this moment. "Lena, honey, I don't know why you're talking about Carter when we're supposed to be talking about Kara."

    "Sam," Lena sighs, looking at her. "Don't you see that we are?"

    Sam shakes her head. "No, the only way we would be talking about - is if - " The realisation happens slowly, and then really quickly. "No way?"

    Lena nods, solemn. "Kara is Carter's mother," she confirms. "She's the woman we've been listening to for the past few weeks, getting so invested in her finding happiness, and she's been right in front of us the entire time."

    Sam still looks shocked. "Kara is Carter's mother." She shakes her head. "Kara is Carter's mother," she repeats. Then: "Oh, my God, Kara is Carter's mother."

    "I know."

    "No, no, oh, my God." Sam's eyes are wide, and she looks horrified. "Lena, oh, my God, she saw my handwriting. She saw my handwriting. Oh, shit, she recognised my handwriting, but I - you - she - oh, my God."

    If it were possible, Sam looks both pale and flushed at the same time. "Sam," Lena says, worried. "Are you okay? You look like you're about to pass out."

    "Lena," Sam whispers. "Lena."

    "What?"

    "I did a thing."

    Lena isn't looking forward to what's coming. She kind of just wants to go home and crawl into her bed and forget all about Kara. "You did a thing."

    "You're going to hate me."

    "Unlikely."

    Sam shakes her head. "I wrote to her," she confesses. "I wrote to Carter's mother."

    Of all the things Lena expected to hear, that's definitely not one of them. "Okay..."

    "As you."

    Lena's eyes snap up. "Excuse me?"

    "It's just that you said you weren't going to write any actual letter, but you had all this wonderful stuff to say to her, so I kind of recorded what you said and then wrote the letters myself, and then sent them, but she was in my office today and saw my handwriting, and I swear I didn't know it was her, and now she thinks we all just played some cruel prank on her." Sam's eyes go impossibly wider. "Oh my God, she thinks Alex orchestrated the whole thing."

    Lena continues to stare at her in complete silence.

    "Lena, I'm so sorry," Sam says. "I didn't - I thought she deserved to know what you thought of her. She wasn't supposed to end up in our lives. It was - you know I never could have predicted this."

    Sam knows. She knows how much Lena despises deceit. It's already difficult enough to trust people, and now Sam has -

    God, what has she done?

    "Lena, please say something."

    Lena sets her hands in her lap and drops her gaze to the pages in front of her. "You should call Alex," she finally says.

    "Lena?"

    "Go and call Alex," Lena says. "Before you end up ruining another relationship because of this mess."

    "Lena."

    She retrieves her pen and gets back to work in silence. Sam stands there and watches her for another two minutes before she leaves the office, deciding to do damage control somewhere else.

    Lena isn't relieved she's gone, because having her in the room gave her an excuse to hold it together. Alone, her shoulders slump and her bottom lip trembles. Alone, she can fall apart.

    Alone, she shall remain.

    For the first time since she awoke to a dead wife, Kara returns to the home they shared for only a few short months. She's avoided so many things from Before, choosing to immerse herself in a world where Cat couldn't exist.

    Now, she's stepping back into a time where she was at her happiest.

    It feels so out of reach, lost to a time that belongs to someone else. She's so different now. Aged in a way that only life experience can make her. She's seen things and done things. She's loved and lost in an intense, profound way. She's struggled with taboo feelings and fought tooth and nail to convince herself and all those around her that love is love and so it shall remain.

    Nothing is different about the penthouse. It's clean and very still. Kara makes sure a cleaning service comes in every fortnight, because Cat would kill her if she let their home just deteriorate.

    It's just that, the moment Kara steps through the front door, she knows she and Carter are never going to live here again. The realisation is actually freeing, and she doesn't even feel guilty about it. Whatever Cat would want doesn't matter in this moment, because she's not here.

    She's not here, but Kara is.

    So is Carter.

    They're still here, and they're going to figure it out. They're going to make it work. It's going to be good. Kara will make sure of it.

    She takes a quick tour, just familiarising herself with the rooms once more, lingering a while in Carter's bedroom and pausing before she pushes herself into entering the master suite.

    The first thing she sees is Cat's silk robe hanging over the back of the chair at her dresser, and Kara's eyes immediately pool with tears. Cat loved that stupid thing.

    Heck, Kara loved that stupid thing.

    Kara can't bring herself to take any more steps inside, so she stands just inside the door and breathes. Just breathes. It's a deceptively difficult thing to do when you're actually thinking about the action, and Kara dedicates all her brain power to it.

    In and out. In and out. In and -

    Her phone rings in her pocket but she doesn't move to answer it. She doesn't even bother checking the Caller ID.

    There's an entire world out there she has to face. There's so much she has to do; difficult conversations she has to have, but she's going to stand here for a little longer.

    Her phone rings again, and she reaches into her pocket to switch it to silent.

    It keeps ringing, vibrating now, and Kara suspects the caller must be Alex. Because Sam has to have contacted her.

    Kara doesn't quite understand exactly what's happened. Her worst case scenarios are just that: the worst. She's certain that's not really the case, and the confusion is almost worse.

    Still, she's going to stand here a little while longer.

    For the first time in her own memory, Lena tells Jess to cancel all her afternoon commitments and to make sure she's not disturbed. It's not as if she's planning to use the time to work.

    No.

    She rather steps onto her office's balcony and just stands. Hides.

    There was a time in her life when she would face this kind of thing head-on. Before Jack, maybe. Before she allowed herself to believe she could have the life Lillian had her convinced she didn't deserve.

    It is such an odd thing to have and to lose. People claim that's better than not having had at all, and the jury is still out as far as Lena is concerned.

    She's not sure how long she sits out there, undisturbed, before Sam comes to find her. Lena doesn't even turn around when she hears Sam move to stand in the open doorway behind her. She's not sure she wants to hear what Sam's going to say.

    She's suddenly sure of it when Sam says, "I think Alex just broke up with me."

    Lena closes her eyes, sun shining against her skin.

    "She said, 'I'm sorry, I can't do this right now. I have to find my sister; we can talk about this later,' and there's really only one way to read that."

    Lena doesn't think that's true, because it really just sounds like Alex has prioritised her sister in this moment, and she'll deal with whatever relationship trouble they're having after that.

    But maybe Lena's wrong. She's been wrong before.

    Instead of responding, she holds out her left hand in something like invitation, and Sam immediately moves forward to take it. Her grip is tight, palms pressed together, and they stand side-by-side, forearms leaning on the railing.

    "I really like her," Sam whispers.

    "Me too," Lena whispers right back, though she's talking about a different sister.

    "I'm pretty sure I'm actually in love with her," Sam amends, voice so quiet that Lena barely hears her over the wind this high up in the air.

    Lena doesn't say, me too, because that's a wild thing to say about someone she's barely known a month but, honestly, it wouldn't be too far off.

    The rest of Kara's day goes rather simply.

    When she leaves what was once her home, she goes to see Alex. Her sister is a place of comfort, and Kara has had a wild day so far. She needs a hug and assurance that what she's thinking happened really didn't happen.

    She doesn't really pay attention to what she's doing until she's in the middle of the hospital where her wife should have found salvation and finds she's standing in front of what she knows is the Residents' Lounge.

    Dr Vazquez is the first one to see her, and her own eyes are wide. "Kara," she says, pushing off the couch she's sitting on. "You're here."

    Kara manages a smile. "Hey," she says, because this is just another person she's hidden from since everything about her life changed in the blink of an eye. "Sorry to bother you, but have you seen Alex?"

    "She should be in the garden in the back," Vazquez tells her. "I think she's been trying to reach you."

    As if on cue, her phone begins to ring in her pocket, and she smiles her thanks to Vazquez before she goes to locate her sister. It's easy enough once she knows where to look, and she even manages to sit on a bench beside Alex, just as Alex bites into the phone, pick up the damn phone, you big stupid baby.

    "Well, that isn't a very nice thing to say about your little sister, is it?" Kara finds herself saying, and Alex practically jumps out of her skin.

    "Kara!" she squeaks. Then: "Kara! You're here! Where have you been?"

    Kara gets wrapped in a hug immediately, arms tight around her, and it's a beautiful kind of pain. "I'm sorry," she says, and her voice is filled with emotion. "I'm sorry I ever accused you of not trying. I'm sorry I expected so much of you. I'm sorry I ever expected you to save her, and then blamed you when you couldn't. I'm sorry I held onto my anger for so long and wasn't here for you the way you needed. I'm sorry, Alex. I'm so sorry I ever blamed you for something that is nobody's fault."

    As if possible, Alex's hug gets tighter, and Kara sobs into her shoulder, everything hitting at once. Because, dear God, she thinks she's actually come out of the worst part of her grief.

    It feels as if she's scaled a wall and dropped onto the other side in a free fall.

    Alex has caught her.

    "You're okay," Alex whispers into her hair. "You're okay."

    Even if she's not currently, she knows she's going to be. Beyond this day, beyond the events of this morning. Even beyond the past year she's just experienced.

    They sit for a while, Alex just holding her together, and then she says, "I don't really know what's going on right now, but Sam called me in a panic, and I - can you tell me what happened?"

    Frankly, Kara was kind of hoping Alex would be able to tell her.

    "Kara?"

    "Do you remember L?" she says, because maybe Alex can help her make sense of it. "The letters I told you about? The woman who told me what I needed to hear? The woman who sent Carter that radio?"

    Alex nods. "The woman you won't admit you have a bit of a crush on," she says. "What does she have to do with anything?"

    "Sam wrote those letters," Kara says, and Alex goes still. "It's her handwriting, but the person in the letters is Lena."

    "What?"

    Kara rests her head on Alex's shoulder, not really wanting to think about it any more than she has. "Also, I like Lena," she admits. "A lot."

    "Oh."

    Kara hums. "What do I do now, Alex?"

    The fact Alex doesn't have an immediate response is particularly telling, because she usually has answers for Kara's questions. Instead, the two of them just sit there on that bench, soaking up the sun, and maybe it's the best they'll get.

    Eventually, Alex has to get back to work, and Kara leaves. She goes to the gallery to check in with things and surprises Winn with the strength and length of the hug she gives him.

    Kara goes home next, arriving just as Eliza returns home with Carter, and Kara hugs them both very tightly. Eliza gives her a curious look, as if she's trying to figure out what, if anything, is so different about her.

    Frankly, Kara wouldn't be able to say.

    Kara spends the rest of the day with Carter, sitting with him as he works on his homework, and then accompanying him outside when he decides to go exploring. She makes him a snack, and then gets him into the bath. They sit in the kitchen while Eliza makes dinner, Carter telling them stories from his day.

    They eat dinner.

    Eventually, Kara puts Carter to bed.

    Kara talks to Nia for only nine minutes when she calls.

    And then Kara gets back to work.

    Chapter 8

    VIII

    Lena doesn't sleep a wink.

    She doesn't even both trying, really. The only reason she goes home is because she can't stand the sight of her white office; not when she keeps looking up and seeing Kara standing at her window and looking unreal.

    She does a lot of pacing at home, contemplating what to do. She doesn't know. It's probably best if she just leaves it as it is, forgets everything do with Kara and Carter, and goes about her life as if she never even learned their names.

    She can do it. She's convinced she can do it.

    It's the best course of action at this point. Kara can go on thinking the worst of her, and that's fine. It's fine. Everything is just fine.

    When the sun rises, Lena gets ready for work. She downs two scalding hot cups of coffee before she leaves for the office, and she forces thoughts of perfect blue eyes and a gentle smile from her mind as she starts her day.

    Something is different when Lena arrives at work, and it is immediately noticeable.

    The structure enclosing the space around the mural is gone. In its place, there is a red curtain covering the extent of the wall, and Lena knows - she just knows - it is unlikely she's ever going to see Kara again.

    It's numbing, the realisation, and she's on something like autopilot as she ascends the elevator to her office, absently greeting Jess and nodding periodically as Jess explains her upcoming day.

    She hears none of it.

    She hears even less when there is a particular email in her inbox, an attachment on a short email, and Lena's not sure she's going to be able to survive the day.

    Miss Luthor,

    Please find invoice attached as requested.

    The Supergirl Team

    The email hasn't even come from Kara. For a striking moment, Lena expects to open the attachment and see a bill for some crazy amount. Art is worth millions these days, and Lena's quite certain she would pay any amount presented at this point.

    In the end, the amount to be paid is a dollar.

    One single dollar.

    Lena laughs, because there's really no other reaction. She laughs and she laughs until a sob sneaks out, and she forces herself to stop.

    She breathes deeply, trying to steady her racing heart.

    There is another email in her inbox. It's from Sam, and Lena immediately knows what it is. It's the type of email she always receives on a Thursday morning, and she's hesitant to open the link to Carter's mother's - Kara's - weekly segment with Nia.

    She also knows she won't be able to focus on anything until she's listened to it.

    Now, Lena knows Kara is Carter's mother. It's been established in her head and her heart, but it still manages to catch her off guard when she hears her voice.

    "Nia, hi," Carter's mother says, and it's Kara's voice. Of course, it's Kara's voice. "Sorry, let me just go inside where the wind isn't blowing. It's just picked up."

    Lena closes her eyes, leaning her head back and listening. She can see Kara in her mind's eye now, imagine her moving through her home, mouth tugged into the smile that holds many a secret.

    "I'm back," Kara says, puffing out a breath. "Sorry about that."

    "Of course," Nia says. "How are you doing?"

    Kara doesn't immediately respond, and Lena suspects she was weighing her options. She had to know Lena would hear the segment eventually. "You know, Nia," she starts; "I actually have some news."

    "Oh?"

    "About Carter," Kara says. "He's found her."

    "Excuse me?"

    "He found the woman who meets his list, apparently," Kara explains. "Actually, he found her a few weeks ago when the letters first started coming in. She wrote to us, and we - I guess we were in agreement she was something special." Her voice goes soft and warm. "Then she wrote again, and it was - she was talking to us, you know. The two of us. She even sent him a radio, which he absolutely loves. We've taken it apart and put it together so many times, and he has to listen to it every single day now."

    "That's amazing," Nia says, and she obviously means it. "We weren't sure anyone who wrote in would make the cut."

    "Neither did I," Kara confesses. "She actually wrote again. A third time. The last one. It's the letter that made me start believing, as well. That maybe my son is a bit of a Casanova, because he sure knows how to pick them."

    Lena feels a smile tug at the edges of her mouth, threatening but not quite following through.

    "We wanted to find her, you know," Kara says. "But all we had was her handwriting and a single initial: L. It wasn't much to go on, and I started to think she didn't actually want to be found. She would have given us more to work with if she did."

    "You wanted to find her?" Nia asks. "You, specifically?"

    "It was Carter's idea first, but I resisted," she admits. "I wasn't ready for what we might find, and I didn't know what I would even say to her at the time."

    "But you know now?"

    "I do," Kara says. "I would tell L thank you for being kind and gentle with your words. Thank you for worrying about us even though you didn't know us. Thank you for caring about Carter more than you ever did about me. Thank you for helping me accept that moving on with my life isn't an insult to my wife. Thank you for allowing me the opportunity to reach this realisation at my own time.

    "I would tell her she's played a role she didn't expect, helping me come to terms with my feelings regarding my past, present and future loves," she says. "I would tell her Carter and I can be what she needs in return, if she wants that. I would tell her I finally feel ready."

    "For what?" Nia asks, and it's unnecessary.

    "To love again."

    Lena sucks in a breath, because that's -

    "Oh," Nia says, and she sounds surprised. "That's - to be honest, the first time we spoke, I wasn't sure this day would come so soon," she says. "Not that it isn't amazing, of course, but - "

    "I know," Kara says with a small laugh. "I'm probably as surprised as you are. I just - I think one of the biggest things I was so afraid of was somehow betraying my wife, which I know is silly, because she probably would have married me off before she even passed." She sounds certain of it, which makes Nia laugh. "But then I was scared of being left behind again," she adds. "My parents did it, my foster father, and then my wife. I didn't think I could handle loving someone else who would also leave me."

    "I - I'm sorry to hear that," Nia says, and she sounds suitably thrown by how much personal information Kara has given her. It's almost as if she's given up on all the cloak and dagger and reached some kind of decision within herself.

    "But I'm not scared anymore," Kara says, bypassing Nia's apology. "I'm not scared of getting my heart broken. I've already survived some of the worst things in the world, and I'm still here. I'm here, and Carter's here, and I have an amazing second family, and I will love again."

    "Wow."

    "I thought this was what you wanted, Nia," Kara says, and she sounds deeply amused. "Growth."

    "I suppose it's because it sounds as if it's just happened so fast on our end, when you've been working on it the entire time."

    "Constantly a work in progress," Kara agrees. "I just wanted to thank you, as well. I know you didn't expect any of this when you answered Carter's call, but I appreciate your talking to him and helping us both get through what happened with his mother. I think we're both better for the experience."

    "Wait," Nia says, pausing. "This sounds like a goodbye. Why does it sound like a goodbye?"

    "That's because it is," Kara says. "I wasn't always so sure, but I know we're going to be okay. That's the best we were always going to get, isn't it?"

    "What? No," Nia says. "We're supposed to find your new wife. We haven't met Carter's Christmas wish."

    "Christmas is over, Nia," Kara points out.

    "We haven't helped, though," Nia counters.

    "Oh, but you have," Kara tells her. "You've done more for us than you ever set out to accomplish, and that is a lovely thing. I'm forever grateful."

    "Wait," Nia says, and she sounds panicked. "But, wait, we can still help. We can help you find L."

    Kara chuckles. "Oh, but Nia, I already have," she reveals.

    "What?"

    "We've met," Kara tells her. "We've talked. I'm pretty sure we've almost kissed a few times."

    "Wait. What? Why wouldn't you lead with that?"

    Kara laughs, and she sounds so young. So wonderfully unburdened for what sounds like the first time. "I don't know if I should be apologising."

    "So, wait, you two are - are you together?"

    "Oh. No."

    "Oh."

    Kara clears her throat. "This is the funny part, Nia."

    "There's a funny part?"

    "We've met," Kara says. "We've talked. We've -

    " - almost kissed a few times," Nia finishes.

    "Because she's gorgeous and perfect, and she has these green eyes I could lose myself in," Kara says, and Lena feels a blush spread on her cheeks. "The funny part is that I'm Carter's mother, and she's L," she says. "And, until earlier today, neither of us even knew it."

    Nia actually gasps, which would be a ridiculous reaction if Lena also weren't thrown by Kara's very casual confirmation. "Seriously?" Nia asks, and her voice is higher than Lena has ever heard.

    "Funny, isn't it?" Kara says. "I ended up falling for her twice."

    And that's where the link's recording ends. It just cuts off, and Lena scrambles to check if that's actually the end, because Kara - she said -

    Lena replays the end, and then again. A fourth time. That's - she definitely said what she said, which is - that means -

    Lena gets to her feet immediately, rounds her desk and stalks right out of her office towards Sam's office, surprising Jess, who scrambles to follow.

    Sam is sitting at her desk, coffee in hand, and Lena will think about how it seems she's just been waiting for her arrival another time. Because Sam is here, and Sam has to have listened to Kara's talk with Nia, so she has to know what Kara said.

    What they've both heard.

    "Sam," Lena says, standing perfectly still in the middle of Sam's office. "Sam."

    "Lena."

    She sighs, defeated in the worst way. "What have I done?"

    "You're Carter's mother, right?"

    Kara's head snaps up from where she's texting Winn to see a woman standing just at her side. She's smiling hesitantly, wary about interrupting. "I - hi," she says, internally kicking herself for hanging around after dropping Carter off at school.

    "Hi, I'm Anita, Troy's mother," she says, holding out her hand, and Kara has to shift her phone to her other hand to be able to shake it.

    "Kara," she confirms, needing a moment to place who exactly Troy is. One of the boys in Carter's class. Ah. One of the boys who's been mean to him since he started at this school. "Is, um - can I help you with something?" She doesn't mean to sound impatient, but there's a lilt to her voice that Anita must pick up on.

    Anita clears her throat. "I - right, I was just - did I hear you on KX3P last night?" she asks, and Kara does her best not to react.

    Well, she reacts with a confused frown. "KX3P?" she asks. "Isn't that the radio station?"

    Anita's hopeful look fades to disappointment, and Kara internally applauds her acting skills. "It is, yes," she says. "You have no idea what I'm talking about, do you?"

    "I don't," Kara lies. "Sorry."

    "That's too bad," Anita says, and it is an obscene thing the way her eyes drag down and back up Kara's body. They're in a school's parking lot, for goodness' sake.

    Kara suddenly wishes she didn't take off her rings so soon. It could have saved her from the awkwardness of this moment.

    "Um."

    Anita steps closer, opens her mouth, and then snaps it shut when Kara's phone actually rings.

    Kara is so relieved to see Alex's name, and she says, "I should probably take this." She gets to her feet before Anita can say anything, turns away and starts walking as she answers with an, "Boy, am I glad to hear from you."

    Alex laughs, obviously bemused. "Did I just save you from something?"

    "Some mom was just hitting on me," Kara says, just far enough that Anita probably won't hear her. "In the parking lot."

    "Dude."

    "Don't even."

    "You attract all sorts, don't you?"

    Kara tosses a look over her shoulder, unsurprised to see the woman still watching her. Kara's never really thought she has a type. Not in the physical sense, at least, because Cat and Lena aren't physically similar.

    And yet.

    Kara shakes her head. "It seems I do," she agrees. "But, anyway, you called. Why did you call?"

    "I - well, I was checking in on how you're doing," Alex says, voice softer. "Yesterday was... a lot."

    "Which part?"

    "All of it."

    Kara steps off the sidewalk and hops a little to get to her waiting car. "Is it weird that I'm actually okay?" she asks.

    "Truthfully, a little, yeah," Alex admits, sounding sceptical.

    Kara laughs, fishing for her keys in her pocket. "I don't know what it is," she says; "I just feel - I guess I feel lighter, somehow. Like, yesterday was this massive eye-opener that I actually have come out the other side of my eternal grief." She unlocks her car and opens the driver's door, sliding in. "I managed to feel something for someone else, Alex, and I don't feel guilty about it. It's like - Cat would be happy. She would be proud, and I - I think this is one of the reasons I - "

    "What?"

    "I have always survived grief," Kara says. "My parents, Jeremiah, even Cat, but I've - " she stops trying to find the best way to explain herself.

    "But what?" Alex presses.

    "I don't think I've ever lived grief," she finally says. "I don't know if that even makes sense."

    "I think it does," Alex says. "I can never know what it's been like for you, but losing Dad broke pieces of me I think are still broken. It just - I hate that you've lost so much."

    "But I have you," Kara says, smiling gently. "And Carter and Eliza, and Winn and just - I have people, and I never want to lose sight of that again." She clears her throat. "I guess that's why I'm okay. That's what this whole experience with L has taught me. And Lena. Both of them. Sam too, I guess."

    Alex is worryingly quiet on the other end.

    "Have you talked to her since?"

    "Not really," Alex admits. "We're supposed to meet for coffee later, so I guess things will get cleared up then. I still don't really understand what happened, to be honest."

    Neither does Kara, but she's not burning with curiosity anymore. Things have happened in her life that were always going to happen. She's said her piece, and she has a life to get back to living.

    "Alex," Kara says. "Please don't think there are sides here. Don't do anything stupid."

    "I've already failed you once before."

    "Oh, Alex."

    "I can't - I can't bear the thought of you looking at me again and just not seeing me," Alex says. "I don't think my heart could handle the way things were, and I can't - I won't be with Sam if she's somehow hurt you - just if - you are too important, okay?"

    Kara sighs. "Okay," she says, because Alex needs to hear that Kara understands that. She also needs to hear: "But please don't be an idiot."

    "Kara."

    "Whatever happened, we'll work it out," she says. "You like her."

    "I'm pretty sure I'm in love with her."

    "Well, then, you better not let her go," Kara says. "Because, I mean, the chances of your finding someone who can put up with your awful hours, terrible moods and competitiveness, and is actually hot, are very minimal."

    "I resent that," Alex grumbles.

    "I'm okay," Kara tells her. "I'm okay, and you're allowed to be, too."

    "Promise?"

    "I promise," Kara vows, and finds that she actually means it.

    Lena spends the entire morning in Sam's office. Jess pops in a few times, brow creased in worry, but Lena waves away her concern each time.

    She and Sam say very little to each other; the two of them sitting together and pointedly not discussing what's led them to this point.

    Well, not until Sam receives a text from Alex asking if they can meet for coffee at their coffee shop later. Sam stares at her screen for a long time before she says yes. Of course, yes.

    Lena absently wonders if Kara will ever contact her again.

    "You should come with me," Sam tells Lena once Alex confirms a time.

    Lena immediately shakes her head. "That's a terrible idea." They both know it. "You need to talk about it with her. Tell her exactly what's happened."

    "And what am I supposed to say when she asks why you did a complete one-eighty on her sister?" Sam asks, eyes narrowed.

    "You make it sound as if we actually had something between us," Lena deflects, and Sam glares at her hard enough that Lena feels like a child. "Nothing happened," she repeats. "Nothing happened, Sam."

    "But something was happening," Sam points out. "You were - God, you were giddy, Lena. She - you like her. We both know you do. Why are you acting as if everything that's happened in the last month has meant nothing?"

    Lena clenches her teeth. "I can't be what they need," she says, forcing out the words. "I don't - they deserve better than me."

    Sam sighs. "And, what about what you deserve?"

    Lena doesn't respond.

    "Why won't you ever just take your own advice?" Sam asks, and she sounds unbelievably sad. "Do you think Kara is an idiot?"

    "Excuse me?"

    "Do you think she would have felt something for you if you were actually unworthy?" Sam asks, and her voice is pointed. "That she's managed to get out of believing she would never feel again for someone else because of you? That she met you, and had that all change?"

    "It definitely didn't happen like that," Lena tells her.

    "It really did," Sam counters. "You heard her. She said it practically for everyone to hear. She's even trending on Twitter again, people clambering to find and reunite her with L."

    "I still haven't forgiven you for that."

    Sam sighs. "But you will."

    Lena glares a little. "Why would you do that, anyway?"

    "Because you are kind and gentle and you care so much, and I love you and want you to be happy, and I am still convinced you can find all the good things you deserve with the woman who is Carter's mother." Sam smiles. "It just really worked out well that the woman also happens to be Kara."

    "It doesn't even matter, because she's never going to talk to me again," Lena says. "Sam, you didn't - God, you didn't see her face. She - she actually flinched."

    Sam sighs again. "Masters of our own destruction, it seems."

    "I haven't even talked to Jack about all of this," Lena says. "I just - the highs and lows have been exhausting. Is this what being in love is supposed to feel like? Putting that person so far ahead of your own self that you barely know what to do with yourself?"

    Sam doesn't say anything in response, and Lena lifts her head to see that Sam is practically gaping at her.

    "Sam?" she asks, concerned. "What is it?"

    "Lena," Sam returns, voice gentle. "Nobody said anything about being in love."

    Her brow creases. "What?" Then: "Oh." She laughs, sharp and short. "I guess that happened, huh?"

    "When did that happen?"

    Lena lifts her shoulders, but doesn't drop them, her mind going back through all the interactions she's had with Kara. There's no single moment she can recall when she felt, well, would you look at that, I could fall in love with you, because maybe every moment felt like that.

    "How did you manage to do that?" Sam asks, and she sounds astonished. "Are you for real, Lena? Because, I mean, that definitely changes things."

    "Changes things, how?"

    "Well, then, we can't just sit idly by and let things work themselves out," Sam says. "Are - do you want to be with her?"

    Lena's pretty sure she's wanted that from the moment she laid eyes on Kara. Sweet, adorable, passionate Kara, who has brought nothing but colour into her life. "Yes," she finds herself saying. "But I'm - "

    "No," Sam cuts her off. "I asked a question, and you answered it. Now, we just have to figure out how to fix this mess we've made."

    "I made a mess of it before she even found out about the letters," Lena says.

    "Does that mean you forgive me?" Sam asks, hopeful, even if it's been just a few minutes.

    Lena isn't lying when she says, "No," and Sam deflates. The two of them are probably going to have to talk about it a lot more, because Sam did this thing behind Lena's back; used her words and posed as her. It was for something good, ultimately, but Lena feels used and betrayed in a way she can't quite put into words.

    Sam is the closest person in her life, and she's said many, many things to her.

    What else could be out there?

    Lena sighs. "I should probably get back to work," she says. "You have to go meet Alex."

    "I still have two hours," Sam says. "I know I'm not going to be able to get any work done in that time, so we're going to make plans."

    "Plans?"

    Sam nods, leaning forward. "We're going to get your Supergirl back," she declares. "And we're probably going to need a hell of a lot of help to do it."

    When Kara asks about how her meeting with Sam went, Alex doesn't say much more than, we're working on it, and Kara reckons that's as good as she's going to get.

    It feels weird not having to go straight to L-Corp when she gets into National City, but she steers her car towards the gallery, instead. She's really neglected it, her attention elsewhere, and she makes a mental note to give Winn a raise for keeping everything ticking along. Maybe she'll send him on vacation somewhere exotic. He could use some sun.

    Winn, as always, is happy to see her, confirming he sent the invoice to Lena with a confused smile. He can tell there's a story there, but Kara isn't quite ready to tell it.

    In fact, she's going to paint it.

    It's really the best way she can express herself - especially after everything that's happened just in the last few days. Her fingers actually twitch with her desire to get them working, and she just about manages to listen to all the information Winn tells her, before she closes herself in her studio and gets to work.

    Only, is it accurate to call it work? It doesn't feel like it is, the art practically flowing through and out of her. The need to create some unstoppable force.

    God, it can be such a rush. Something impulsive, itching at the back of her mind until she can get access to a pen and paper; to paints and charcoal and some kind of canvas. Something.

    It thrums just beneath her skin. Her father once told her that's what passion felt like. Something inexplicable and attached to something or someone. She didn't understand it at the time, because she took up art only after they were gone, and science brought her too much pain.

    Huh.

    Look at what else she allowed grief to rob of her.

    It almost took art, too.

    Kara spends hours in her studio, working without any real purpose, and she emerges only when her stomach grumbles in hunger and protest. She does tend to lose herself in her craft and forget to eat, which really just showcases her priorities.

    She can't escape Winn's questions this time, the two of them taking a walk around the corner to their favourite diner.

    "We've never charged anyone only a dollar," he starts, with zero tact.

    Kara just bites into her meatball sub, buying herself some time. She's not really sure how to explain the decision behind that. She doesn't need the money, and she would have done the entire piece for no commission, but Lena insisted on a bill.

    Well.

    "We should do it more often," Kara says, thinking about it. She hasn't taken any commissions for more than a year, and, even then, she wasn't as invested in that side of the job of being an artist. She's always just painted for herself. "Just randomly surprise people with all the art in the world."

    Winn actually looks interested in the idea.
