He's had to find other interests, joining Kara as she opened her gallery after Cat shut down CatCo Worldwide Media before it could grow to its full potential. He took his compensation and invested in many things - including Kara - and now he's comfortable.

    Cat left so many of them just comfortable.

    "We could call it the Supergirl Project," Kara adds, an idea building in her head. "Not exactly vandalism, but we can ask people if they would mind having artwork on their buildings, and then just go around creating large pieces of art around the city. Making it beautiful one wall at a time."

    Winn really looks interested, now. He leans forward, elbows on the table and his food abandoned. "We should do it," he says. "We can even recruit freelance artists to join, start a website and keep everything official. Provide supplies and encourage people to participate. Especially women. Girls in art."

    Kara nods slowly, enjoying the way he's taken the suggestion and run with it. It's definitely an idea for them to explore.

    Next week.

    Just not today.

    Today, all Kara needs to do is get through the rest of the afternoon and evening without thinking too hard about her piece's reveal at L-Corp. Kara has seen it only once in completion, but that was at four o'clock in the morning and she was running on fumes.

    If anyone has seen it in its entirety, it's Winn, who oversaw the taking down of the scaffolding and the hanging of the curtain. It's done now, though, and there's nothing she would change about it.

    So, she needs to get through today and maybe tomorrow, and then they can embark on their new project.

    She also thinks she's going to have to tell Carter she's met L.

    Well.

    So has he.

    At first, Kara isn't sure how to say the truth to him, because she doesn't know if she'll end up breaking his heart. He's just always so full throttle, all or nothing, and she doesn't know if she can properly explain that L and Miss Luthor are the same person, while making sure he understands Lena isn't -

    She's not -

    She doesn't want them.

    Huh.

    Imagine if Lena knew Kara was actually married to Cat. Goodness. Kara almost wants to be there for that moment. It would be one for the books, surely.

    In the end, Kara actually leads with, "How would you feel about moving back to National City after this school year ends?"

    Now.

    Kara has an idea of how he might react, but it catches her way off guard when he throws himself at her and practically yells in her ear.

    "Can we go tomorrow?"

    She's pretty sure she's lost hearing, but that's -

    "I love Grandma, but Midvale is - I don't really like it here," Carter says, pulling back far enough to look into her eyes. "They're mean to me, and all the teachers talk about you and I miss my room, and this place smells weird sometimes."

    Kara steadies him in her lap, a little amused but a lot heartbroken that she's brought them to this place because she couldn't handle being in National City without Cat.

    Now she can.

    Maybe she always could, and she just didn't want to have to.

    The new semester's just started, but Kara thinks she could get Carter back into his old school easily enough. He is Carter Grant, despite what anyone says, and his name will always carry weight, even if his mother is no longer around.

    "Tomorrow is Saturday, Bud," she tells him.

    "That's even better," he points out. "It's the weekend. Aunt Alex can help us move again."

    "Carter."

    He blinks at her with those eyes that are so familiar. Maybe too innocent for his own good, really. "Please," he says, and Kara is already making mental plans.

    Lena wears a red dress, which is more burgundy than anything.

    She doesn't really think too much about it until she's zipped up the back and arrived in the foyer of L-Corp, when Jess takes one look at her and says, "You look warm."

    Lena blinks. "Excuse me?"

    "The colour," Jess clarifies. "It's warm."

    As if Lena needs another excuse to be thinking about Kara right now. She just smiles at Jess, and then asks if everything is ready for the upcoming event. She knows things are already going to plan, but she also knows she's going to be revealing a mural she's never seen to a room full of press and investors in an hour's time.

    She doesn't think Kara would have -

    Kara wouldn't have left it unfinished, surely. She just doesn't seem like the kind of person to do such a thing. But, then again, maybe Lena would deserve it, based on everything she's already done to Kara.

    Lena just can't stop seeing that look on her face.

    It'll haunt her for a lifetime.

    Lena encounters Sam next, who is standing with Brainy and telling him a story about Ruby. Things are still a little tense between the two of them, she and Sam, but Lena knows she'll come to accept what's happened for what it is.

    Lena ruined whatever was happening with Kara long before Sam ever did. It's just been compounded by their deceit, and Lena is going to have to accept that there is little to no chance of Kara -

    Just, there's no point of even thinking about it.

    Lena does the rounds for the next hour, sometimes joined by Jess, as they meet with investors and members of the Board. It's been a full year since L-Corp's new branding, and it takes everything Lena has not to look every one of the men who doubted her in the eye and say, look at me now, motherfuckers.

    Just the bought brings a smile to her face, because she's been grossly underestimated in so many aspects of her life, and this is one of those moments where she can be proud of her accomplishments. And she is.

    It's just -

    She knows, if not for their last moments together, Kara would be here. Smiling that smile and looking at Lena with those eyes. Expression proud, but always holding secrets. Full of silent support.

    Lena knows Kara would look stunning at an event like this. She doesn't know how she knows that, but she's certain of it. It's an expectation she would have had of the night, which is -

    What she definitely doesn't expect is for her mother to be in attendance.

    It shocks Lena to stunned silence when she sees Lillian enter the foyer where the event is being held, and it takes Jess purposefully nudging her to get her moving once more.

    Lillian is here.

    That's definitely not something Lena saw coming.

    "Did she RSVP?" Lena whispers to Jess, watching as her mother moves through the crowd, carefully avoiding certain people and making sure to greet plenty more. It is immediately obvious her destination is actually Lena, and Lena has the childish urge to turn and hide away somewhere.

    This night is already stressful enough without adding Lillian Luthor to the mix.

    "Not that I'm aware," Jess informs her, and Lena would roll her eyes at her mother's audacity if she weren't worried about getting reprimanded for such an action by said woman. She really doesn't need to be treated like an insolent child in front of her colleagues and employees.

    Jess saves her from actually having to greet her mother in person by informing her it's time for proceedings to begin. Ordinarily, Lena would avoid having to stand up front for any reason other than presenting their latest products, but she jumps at the opportunity in this moment.

    She's proud of everything they've managed to accomplish in the last year, and this is how she's going to ensure every doubter that the work isn't nearly halfway done. She's set lofty goals for this company, and this first year has been only a stepping stone on the way to bettering the world, for everyone.

    Those words make up the bulk of her short speech, basically explaining the reason they're even gathered here today. Then she says, "And, to mark the occasion, I've commissioned a piece from the extremely talented artist known as Supergirl." Her heart stutters a little in her chest at merely the mention of Kara's alias. "It's a mural, showcasing this company's journey through its many years of operation, and highlights its most important triumphs and failures.

    "Because, if we are to continue growing, dominating the markets and being an enviable force of good and change in the world, then we cannot forget nor can we escape everything that has come before this very moment." She breathes deeply, heart beating steadily, because these words might fit her professional life, but she's never quite managed to apply her own mantra to her personal life. "We can only keep learning, and we keep striving." She manages a smile. "So, without further ado, may I present the reason we've all gathered here this evening."

    Lena pulls the ribbon herself, releasing the curtain that drops to the ground and reveals the piece of art Kara spent hours upon hours creating, just for her.

    It is -

    Dear God.

    It is beautiful.

    It is everything, and so much more than she could have ever imagined it to be. It steals her breath right from her lungs, and she's vaguely aware that she's not the only one who actually gasps when the mural is shown in its entirety.

    Lena had an idea of what she wanted, but even her own mind couldn't have come up with this masterpiece. Her heart goes still in her chest, and she knows -

    She knows.

    Kara has given her the greatest gift Lena has ever received, and Lena knows the price she's paid is far more expensive than any single dollar.

    Lena is so enthralled in taking in every colour and nuance in front of her that she barely notices when her mother comes to stand at her side, approaching without Lena registering her movement.

    Not until she's speaking, catching Lena's attention.

    "Darling," Lillian quietly says, staring at the mural as if she's never seen art before. "Who painted this?"

    Lena forces her own eyes away. "Excuse me?"

    "Who painted this?" Lillian asks again, not even expressing irritation at having to repeat herself. She looks legitimately spooked, and Lena can't figure out why.

    "Supergirl," Lena reminds her. "I mentioned the artist in my speech."

    "Who is she?"

    Lena frowns, because this is a strange line of questioning. "Mother?"

    Lillian turns to face her, eyes a little narrow. "Are we looking at the same mural, Lena?" she asks, and it sounds rhetorical. "Because you are clearly not seeing what I'm seeing."

    "And what's that?"

    "Love."

    Lena goes still at the sound of the word, and she has to resist her automatic response to question what her mother even knows about love. It wouldn't be fair, and Lena holds her tongue.

    Her facial expression must give her away, though, because Lillian's own expression falls slightly. "Oh, Lena," she says.

    "Mother."

    "You must see it," Lillian says, tone severe. "Look."

    Lena looks, unsure what she's supposed to be seeing in the mural. It is stunning, of course, and exactly what Lena asked for, though even she couldn't have envisioned it coming out so perfectly. It's almost as if Kara tapped into her mind and managed to create exactly what Lena wouldn't have been able to articulate another way.

    The colours tell a story so true to the Corporation's legacy, but it's the colours closest to the end of the wall that make the painting.

    They're just so warm.

    "Mother," Lena says again, but her tone is different. Less a question asking for an an explantation, and more a plea for understanding.

    Lillian hums to herself. "I have taught you many things, Lena," she says; "and one of those was to see and observe everything."

    Lena does not mention that Lillian has also taught her to question everything, which can present a double-edged sword.

    "Tell me about this artist."

    So, Lena does. She made a vow to improve communication with her mother, so why not dive right into the number one thing plaguing her mind - and heart? Perhaps her mother will have a different take on it than she or Sam has. Or even Alex or Kara.

    But all Lillian ends up saying after Lena has completed her abbreviated tale about how she managed to mess everything up with Kara is, "I suppose I taught you that, as well."

    "What?" Lena asks, as articulate as ever.

    And, of course Lillian would be cryptic about it, turning her body to face the mural once more. All she says is, "It's what you deserve," and Lena just doesn't understand.

    So she looks, too, and she just keeps on looking.

    Alex complains the entire time.

    Not even a little bit of complaining, but the kind that starts from the moment Kara decides to make the move back to National City, and all the way until the last box has been situated in its new place.

    Which is really Kara's old place. She didn't quite let go of it, at Cat's insistence. Cat liked the little apartment, for some reason, and Kara was sentimental enough to sublet it on only a short term lease.

    It's just lucky the place has been empty since the start of the year. Almost kismet, if Kara were really to think about it.

    She knows it's temporary, but it brings Kara the kind of comfort she's convinced Alex understands, despite her endless complaints. Boxes are too heavy, there are too many towels, and why do we have to do this on an actual Sunday, Kara?

    Kara does feel a little bad, because it's Alex's day off, but Kara also knows Alex wouldn't want to disappoint Carter, either. He's truly very powerful, and he's starting to figure it out. At this point, there are too many people in the world who would give him anything he wanted.

    Just ask KX3P. They bent within a short phone call.

    Kara does promise Alex pizza for her troubles, and she delivers on said promise as soon as they're done. Kara has to rush Carter through his dinner, because it's already late, and he's starting at his new old school in the morning.

    He pouts, of course, but then he yawns three times in quick succession, and there just isn't an argument to be made after that. Before he scampers off to bed, he hugs Alex so hard that she turns a little green at the thought her pizza might all just come right back up.

    Kara just shoots her a smile, and then goes to tuck him in. The second bedroom of this apartment is really just a glorified office. It's tiny by most standards, but Carter doesn't seem to mind that Kara used this room as her home studio when she still lived alone.

    Carter yawns another four times while he's brushing his teeth and using the toilet, and Kara has to temper her smile every time. He crawls into bed without a fuss, comforted by the familiar toys and bedding despite this strange and new place.

    Kara's a little worried the first night will be difficult, but he looks too exhausted to be thinking too much about the foreignness of this home. All he really says is, "I'm glad we're here," around another yawn. "You already look happier." And then doesn't say a word more, slipping into slumber with zero fight at all.

    Kara sits a little while longer, watching his chest rise and fall in a steady rhythm. It constantly amazes her that this is her life, a mother to this precious, perfect boy. As difficult as things have been and can get; this moment has made everything worth it. Carter, here, happy and healthy; Kara would face all the Universe's villains to keep it that way.

    When she finally returns to the living room, it's to find that Alex has tidied up their dinner and put the leftover pizza in the fridge. She's sprawled out on the couch, humming to herself, and Kara resists the urge to sit on her, lest pizza actually really come back up.

    "Scoot over," she says instead, and Alex acquiesces with a groan.

    "I think I ate too much," Alex complains, gently rubbing her stomach. "Why'd you buy so much pizza?"

    "Right," Kara deadpans as she drops onto the couch and rolls her eyes when Alex immediately puts her legs in her lap. "It's all my fault."

    "Exactly."

    "Not a grown woman not being able to tell when it's enough pizza."

    "Hush."

    Kara just chuckles, absently picking at the cuff of Alex's skinny jeans. Alex remains silent - whether on purpose or because she's too stuffed to say anything, Kara doesn't know. It does give her the silence and space to wrap her head around the day as a whole.

    The weekend.

    The last week.

    "I think we could have been friends," Kara eventually says. "In another life."

    Alex glances at her. "I have never actually witnessed the two of you in the same room, but I get the feeling you're talking such horse shit right now."

    Kara can't stop her laugh, sharp and unexpected. "Alex," she lightly reprimands. "I'm trying to tell you that, in another life, maybe me and Lena could have been - we could have been friends."

    "And nothing more?"

    "I just - there's a world out there where being her friend would be enough."

    Alex raises her eyebrows. "But not this one?"

    "Not this one," she confirms. "I thought there was something there, you know? Between us. I thought she felt it, too."

    "What makes you think she didn't?"

    Kara traps her bottom lip between her teeth. "When we met, the first thing that struck me about her was that she was so warm," she says, and thinks of the colours she's been using on Lena's 'portrait.' "It practically oozed out of her; radiated off her in all the best ways, and I was - I was drawn to her from the very beginning." She sighs. "I just didn't believe anything they said about the coldness of a Luthor. I didn't even believe anything she said about being a Luthor." She pauses. "Not until Lena, herself, was standing right in front of me and proving its accuracy."

    "Kara."

    "It changed so quickly; like a switch was flipped, and I wouldn't even know what to believe at this point," she says. "Like, which part was real? Was any of it? How does L fit in, and did they know who I was?"

    "They didn't," Alex confirms for her, but it doesn't help settle her thoughts as much as she hoped it would. It is a relief to know there wasn't some conspiracy going on behind her back, but there are still too many things that don't make sense.

    Kara could obsess over it all, but she's choosing not to. She already has enough going on.

    "I fought my feelings for Cat every step of the way," Kara says. "But I didn't do that with Lena. Not in the way I did before. I just - I liked her, and I had to figure out if I was ready for what that actually meant for me, and I'm - if nothing else has come out of this; at least I know that I can feel something for someone again, and that's something I was so worried about."

    "Kara," Alex breathes, tone heavy.

    "I'm going to be okay."

    "I know you are."

    Kara manages a smile that slowly fades. "I just - I know it sounds silly to put it this way, but I guess I just thought she actually liked me."

    Alex opens her mouth, and then immediately closes it. Opens it again. "She did," she says. "She does."

    Kara glances at her. "Maybe," she allows, because even she has to admit she couldn't have imagined it all. She's a creative person, but even she has her limits. She knows Lena was looking at her just as much as Kara was looking at Lena. "It doesn't matter, though," she says, and she sounds certain of it.

    Alex looks curious. "Why?"

    "Because I am tired of chasing people who would rather hurt me than face their feelings for me," she reveals, the memory of Cat feeling heavy today.

    Alex looks sympathetic, and a little amused. "Damn, Kara," she finally says; "maybe you actually do have a type."

    Kara laughs, soft and tired. "Maybe," she agrees, sighing. "It really is all for the best, though."

    "Why do you say that?"

    Now, when Kara laughs, it sounds just that bit more upbeat. Genuine. "I'm pretty sure Lena would keel over when she found out I was actually married to Cat," she says, and Alex can't even dispute it.

    Lena's sleep schedule has never quite settled, and she's awake before her alarm even goes off on Monday morning. She feels tense, antsy in a way that worries her enough that she emails Jess to cancel her morning meetings and rather schedule time for her in the labs.

    She needs to stop thinking of what might have been.

    What could still be, if only she could find the correct words to make sense of just what's happened. She thinks she has things somewhat figured out, but the reality is that Kara has unsettled Lena in a way she's not sure she'll be able to shake. There have been girls and women before her, between Jack then and Jack now - even before Jack - but nobody in Lena's dating history has made her -

    It's just a lot.

    And the labs help where therapy doesn't.

    Lena goes straight to the second floor when she arrives at the L-Corp building, her eyes taking in the mural for the first time in the daylight. It's truly a magnificent piece, telling a story with colours and textures, and it is a bargain to have paid only one dollar for something so magical.

    Lena stands and admires it for a full four minutes before she gets moving. Edward gives her a particularly knowing smile, and she imagines he must have seen her growing affection for Kara play out right in front of him. She wonders if he was witness to the terrible end of their premature courtship. What must he think? Is he as disappointed in her as she is in herself?

    Lena forces the thoughts from her mind, rather focusing on the several ongoing projects that need her attention. Which would help if it actually worked.

    It's never happened to her before, you see. She's usually able to compartmentalise; place things in mental boxes to be dealt with at a later time - or never. She's usually able to set aside her emotional turmoil and focus on her work; finding solace in the science she can actually understand.

    Kara does not allow her such respite.

    It takes four hours and nineteen minutes for her frustration to reach its peak. Even the promise of curing the world's diseases can't stop her mind from constantly drifting to Kara - and Carter - and their last moments together.

    What could have been.

    But, it takes four hours and nineteen minutes for Lena to figure out just what she's going to do. What she's always known she was going to do. What Sam has been trying to convince her to do from the very beginning.

    Because it takes four hours and nineteen minutes for Lena to throw her pen in frustration, get to her feet to walk about the lab, and happen across the ridiculous magazine that had Kara looking rather spooked the first and only time she was in here.

    Lena stops, reaches for it, and opens it to the page with the article on Cat Grant. She wouldn't be able to say why she does it, but there's this strange compulsion to read the words that were written. Not for any particular reason, in her own mind, but -

    There is a single paragraph - a quotation from Cat, written in size seven font - that gives Lena pause. Alters her perspective in a way she's sure she's had sitting at the back of her brain for a while. Forces her to acknowledge what she's always known.

    "I have been in a constant, never-ending and persistent fight in my professional life. My entire career has been built on opportunities I have had to scrape and scramble for, and I thought it would apply to every other aspect of my life as well. Listen carefully, because I won't ever repeat this, but I am mature enough to admit I was wrong. Not everything has to be a fight, and I had to learn that the hard way in my personal life. Because, when you have to fight for everything else in your life, you begin to believe you don't deserve to have anything given to you so freely. Like love." She chuckles to herself, eyes a little faraway, as if she's lost in a perfect memory. "I have learned this lesson through my son, of course, but also through my current and, hopefully this time, forever love. It has taken me years and years, but I've finally accepted it is what I deserve."

    Lena drops the magazine on the desk. Turns right around and walks out of the lab. Rides the elevator to her own floor, bypassing Jess' empty desk and walks right into Sam's office without bothering to knock.

    Ignores the bewildered look Sam gives her, obviously busy with work.

    "Sam," Lena says, the name coming out as a question, statement and exclamation all at once. "I think I'm ready."

    Sam stares for a moment, because she's very obviously in the middle of a phone call with some very important investors. "Can this wait?" she asks, looking conflicted.

    "I think I'm ready to write that letter to Carter's Mom," she declares, and Sam has never hung up the phone so fast in her life.

    Chapter 9

    IX

    The first red flag is that there is no postage stamp on the envelope.

    Also, it's not-so-innocently sitting on the end of her bed, and there's really only one way it could get there, because nobody even knows she's now at this address. Someone - probably, definitely named Alex - put it there.

    The handwriting is not one she recognises, but that means very little, because she already knows who the letter is from before she even reads the first word.

    Dear Carter's Mom (Kara),

    I suppose I should start by introducing myself. My name is Lena - or L, if you prefer - and it seems that I have done the impossible and fallen in love without even realising it. Admittedly, it's not the first time it's happened to me - there was a pair of girls in boarding school that I was especially fond of, but nobody has managed to leave me quite this unmoored until you.

    Before I even knew you. From the first Wednesday evening I heard your voice on the radio; heard you speak of a love you found and lost, and weren't sure you would be willing to go through again. You left me affected from the moment you told mere strangers that you would do all you could to make your son's wishes come true; that his happiness was all you wanted.

    I decided, even then, that I wanted the same. It truly never occurred to me I could, perhaps one day, have a hand in making his wish come true.

    I imagine this might be difficult to believe, given we've, technically, never actually met - and known it. It's, perhaps, one of the reasons I felt compelled to say words to you at all - that were later actually transcribed and delivered to you without my knowledge - because you didn't know me and I didn't know you, and there is a certain comfort to be found in anonymity.

    Because, you see, the real truth is that I heard something very particular in your voice that I immediately recognised. Not exactly grief, but rather the longing that comes with it. For something; someone; an elusive future. Just something more. Something to hold onto.

    I've had the feeling for so many years, constantly searching for that one thing that always felt as if it were missing. I wouldn't have been able to name it, but I had myself convinced I would know it when I found it. It has taken me a while, but I am mature enough to admit when I'm wrong.

    I didn't know it.

    I found it, and I just didn't know it.

    But I've since figured it out, and that's the reason I'm writing this letter.

    My best friend has wanted me to put my own words to paper since your first ever broadcast, but I've resisted doing it myself until now. I didn't feel as if I had anything to say that you would want or need to hear, but I was wrong. It seems I've been wrong a lot.

    In my family, nothing has ever been given freely. Not love, and not kindness or attention. There was always something expected in return. It has taken me too many years to figure out not everyone is like that. I've just come to accept that of Sam - you might know her as my best friend - and, at times, Jack, who has always suggested, but never felt compelled to push for more.

    To be perfectly honest, I still don't know where you fit in with all of that, but I do know I was terrified of what it meant for you and me before and after I figured out Carter's mother and Supergirl are one in the same. What it would mean for Carter that I didn't live up to the very specific list he wanted the new person in your life to meet.

    All I knew, in that moment I learned of the connection, was that you and Carter deserved more than I could ever offer, and it has been brought to my attention that is the wrong way to think. I hurt you in the process, and for that I'm sorry. As a Luthor, I could not see how I could ever live up to someone you could need or want, and it consistently baffles me that you saw something in me at all.

    You, who has already lost a great love. You, who has shown such strength in being willing to try again. You, whom I know deserves the world and all the galaxies.

    I am aware we know so little of each other. These last few weeks have been exhilarating and terrifying and life-questioning, and I wouldn't trade them for anything. I have learned far too much about myself; about what I'm allowed to want and about how much I'm willing to go after it.

    It doesn't have to be a fight, but I am willing to do so, if required. I know I told you once that I found comfort in the easiness of being with Jack, but I also think I've always known that's not what I want. I don't think I ever have, if I'm being honest. I want none of that, when all I want is blue eyes and a smile full of secrets.

    You.

    And Carter.

    If you'll have me, after all of this.

    Because I think we could be happy, though I wouldn't take my word for it. You'll probably have to see it for yourself to believe it; to decide if I'm actually worth the fight in return.

    So.

    If you're willing, I would very much still like to go on that non-date to the zoo. I will be there this Saturday at noon, waiting by the tiger exhibit. Not with flowers, but with donuts. Because, if we were ever to have a 'thing;' donuts would be it, and I reckon that's pretty special.

    Please will you join me. I think we deserve an actual chance.

    I'll be waiting.

    L (Lena Luthor)

    The contents of the letter shouldn't be as surprising as they are, but Kara truly believed that she would never hear from Lena again. At least not in this capacity, and definitely not so soon. She wasn't naïve enough to believe she could avoid her forever, especially when Alex and Sam are bound to be everlasting, but this letter is unexpected.

    She doesn't even know what to do with it, so she just sets it back on the end of her bed and walks out of the room. She follows the sounds of voices into the living room, finding Alex and Carter building a red London Bus out of Lego - which Kara knows is way beyond Carter's age, but none of them cares about that in this moment - on the carpet.

    Alex glances at her, eyes a little nervous. As she should be, because this is -

    Kara doesn't need her sister to play messenger, and she thinks she gets that message across as she settles on the floor beside Carter and doesn't mention the letter at all.

    But neither does Alex.

    In fact, the person who ends up mentioning the letter inevitably ends up being Carter just the next morning, when she's trying to get him to eat his breakfast before they have to leave for school.

    One would think Kara would be more careful with things as important as that kind of personal confession within a written letter, but she really does leave the letter out on the end of her bed, and she suspects that part of Alex's ploy is to get her son involved. She's less likely to resist that way.

    Because, now, Carter wants to go to the zoo.

    On Saturday.

    At noon.

    To give L - Mommy, she's also Miss Luthor! - a hug, he claims, with this grin on his face that merely proves he's desperately trying to contain his obvious excitement, and to tell Lena that he's sorry she doesn't believe she would be perfect for the list.

    Because she would be.

    She likes cats.

    Oh, how Kara wishes her mind worked as purely as her son's. She wishes she didn't have to consider everything else in her life now. She wishes she could be as full of belief as her son - or her younger self.

    She's hurt, sure, but she's also unsurprised by it. Maybe she wasn't actually ready. Who knows? She definitely doesn't. All she does know is she and Lena were getting somewhere, but they weren't quite there, and it's that potential she's actually mourning. She could continue to do so; essentially moving on in a different direction to the path she was on merely a week ago.

    Away from Lena, instead of towards her.

    But then Carter tells her he wants to go to the zoo, and Kara rather firmly tells him no.

    The word surprises them both, Carter's eyes widening, and Kara going still. It is so rare for her to deny him anything for which he explicitly asks, and they seem to have clashed quite severely the past few weeks. In relation to L and Lena.

    Carter gets over his surprise quickly, though, and says, "Aunt Alex said you would ignore what the letter says."

    Kara makes a mental note to order a salad for Alex when she next takes her lunch at the hospital, just for that.

    "Don't worry, Mommy," he says, lifting his spoon of cereal up to his mouth. Casual and so at ease. Not like her, and not at all like his mother. Too much like Alex, if she's completely honest. "You'll change your mind."

    There are flowers waiting for Lena, once more, on Wednesday morning, and she merely stands and stares at them for long, long minutes. She already knows they're from Jack, an apology for missing L-Corp's first birthday. She told him it was unnecessary for him to attend, but -

    She really just didn't want to see him. She doesn't, though she knows the two of them should probably talk. Preferably before Saturday. Before she potentially stands in front of Kara and offers her all the parts of her she's willing to share. She knows exactly what she needs to tell him, but -

    Lena sighs, vaguely aware of Jess coming into her office behind her. She turns her head to look over her shoulder. "He's going to be in town, isn't he?"

    "Wants to know when you're free."

    Lena nods slowly. "Make a reservation at Le Rendezvous for Friday evening."

    Jess' eyebrows rise, because it's definitely not a romantic restaurant. Not even a restaurant suitable for wining and dining business colleagues. It's just a restaurant, where Lena intends to put an end to whatever Jack has been trying to start for some months now. "Noted, Miss Luthor."

    Lena finally moves towards her desk. "I'm also going to need you to order a dozen donuts for Saturday morning," she says, managing a smile as she rounds her desk and settles into her chair. "Also, check with Sam for when she's free today, and then schedule a meeting for us as soon as possible."

    If possible, Jess' eyebrows rise even higher. Lena doesn't generally schedule meetings with Sam unless they involve other people. But, just the two of them; it's usually an impromptu thing.

    Lena makes no further comment, turning her attention to her computer to check her emails. "This morning looks light," she comments, which is both good and bad.

    Gives her all the time in the world to wonder and stress about the fact she asked Sam to ask Alex to deliver her latest, first and maybe-last letter to Kara.

    Outwardly, she's as calm and collected as she's always been, but her heart is beating faster than normal, and she can't seem to sit still. Restless fingers and shifty eyes. It's just so unlike her.

    This is what Kara has done to her.

    Goodness, the woman has ruined her. Lena can't even be mad about it, because she's wanted this, hasn't she? Something that, perhaps, wasn't so easy. Something true and real. Something she wasn't so sure she could deserve.

    This is what Kara has done to her.

    It continually plays on her mind as she gets through her morning, making decisions without giving it too much thought and rushing through her various meetings. She's aware she's not acting as she normally would, and only a handful of her colleagues seem to notice.

    None of them is brave enough to bring it up in conversation.

    Except Sam, of course.

    The two of them have a late lunch in Lena's office, Jess ordering food for them from Noonan's. It's already three o'clock and Lena would love nothing more than to go home right now. But she has a four o'clock meeting with the company's PR team - which, yay for her - so she knows she can't leave.

    Sam, to be fair, looks equally as over this day as Lena feels. That doesn't stop her from saying, "Have you heard anything from Kara?"

    Lena forces away her urge to wince as she shakes her head. "Have you?" she asks in response.

    Sam snorts in disbelief. "It's hard enough hearing from Alex, at the moment," she reveals.

    Lena tilts her head a little. "I thought you two talked it out," she says, frowning slightly. "You said Alex delivered the letter."

    Sam sighs. "She did deliver it, and we did talk it out, but - "

    "But what?"

    "I can't quite figure out if she actually meant what she said, or if she just said what she thought I wanted to hear," Sam says.

    "And, what exactly did Alex say?"

    Here, Sam hesitates, and Lena gets the feeling she hasn't been the only one being stupid when faced with the seriousness of an actual relationship.

    "Sam," Lena says.

    "She told me she loves me," Sam finally says, the words falling out of her mouth like a confession she doesn't really want to make. "When I asked her why she would help us, and you, and Kara; she said it was because she loves me, and knows I wouldn't ever hurt anyone willingly - let alone her sister - and I - "

    When she stops abruptly, Lena already knows what happened after. "And you didn't say it back."

    Sam looks away, conflict all over her face. "I don't - how could she still love me after what's happened - let alone at all? Why would she even - "

    Lena abandons her food, because, good God, why are she and Sam so damn similar? And so damn stubborn and borderline pathetic?

    Lena basically pushes her carton away and groans a little too loudly for the confines of her office. Jess must be able to hear her through the door, but Lena doesn't even care.

    Sam looks at her curiously. "Lena?"

    "Alex told you she loves you," Lena says; "and Kara - "

    "Basically told you the same thing, through a radio show."

    Lena shakes her head. "If I'm going to do this, then so are you," she declares, tone heavy.

    Sam stares a little wide-eyed. "Do what?"

    "Believe her."

    While Kara is proud that she's the one to hold off longer than Alex; she still feels a flare of irritation when Alex asks her about the letter. It's a bit of a relief that Alex doesn't actually know the contents of the letter - nope, that's only Carter, who has no sense of privacy - because Kara's sure that would make the situation more difficult.

    She doesn't need Alex telling her what to do.

    She also doesn't need Alex to tell her what Kara already knows. That she would be an idiot not to go and meet Lena; just talk to her and look into her eyes to see her truth. Learn what's truly in her heart. It would be easy; something so simple, just to show up and see what happens.

    But.

    There was a time when Kara wouldn't question it, though even she wouldn't be able to explain what has her so hesitant. Just that she is. Maybe it's that Lena left it to her to take that step when she was ready, and then she did, and Lena didn't -

    Lena did a complete one-eighty, and it turns out it was actually because Kara also happens to be Carter's mother. Kara now knows it's actually because of Lena, and not her, but that doesn't make Kara any more comfortable with the situation as a whole.

    She's been in a situation remarkably similar to this - with Cat - and it just seems simpler to avoid it all together. Kara has already expended a lot of energy in just the last few weeks - emotionally, mentally and physically - and it's taken a lot out of her to get to this point in her life.

    She's exhausted.

    She needs some time to recharge. Maybe she should go out and get some sun.

    Still, when Alex asks the inevitable question, Kara should be ready. Only, she's not. Not even a little.

    "Are we ever going to talk about whatever is in the letter?"

    Kara looks away from her dinner, eyes a little narrow, because Carter is sitting right there. Always just these little ears hearing things they shouldn't. She doesn't want to involve her son in all of this, but Lena, Sam and Alex all took that option away from her the moment L's letter was signed with Lena's name and left for Kara - and Carter - to find.

    Carter suddenly can't wait to talk to the woman about cats, ice-cream and radios, and not necessarily in that order.

    Carter, who seems to perk up at the mere mention of the letter. Carter, who immediately tells Alex the most important takeaways from said letter. Carter, who has no sense of privacy.

    "She said she's sorry, she loves Mommy, and she wants to meet at the zoo on Saturday at noon."

    Alex's eyes snap towards Kara, wide and curious and concerned. "Is that true?" she asks, voice a little too high. "Kara, seriously, is that what she said?"

    "That's what she said," Carter confirms for her, looking as if he's thoroughly enjoying himself. "But Mommy says she's not going."

    Alex just continues to stare at Kara.

    "And she won't take me, either," Carter adds. Then makes sure to look at Alex when he says, "If I can't get her to change her mind, will you take me?"

    "Carter," Kara warns, and his mouth snaps shut. "You're done there," she points out his now-empty plate. "Take it to the sink, and then go and finish your homework."

    Carter looks as if he wants to argue for a moment, but then he just shakes his head with a pout and does as instructed.

    Alex doesn't even wait until he's out of earshot to ask, "Seriously, Kara, is that all true?"

    Kara would deny it all, but she's never really been able to lie to her sister. She's tried in the past, but it just doesn't sit well with her. "She didn't exactly phrase is so simply but, essentially, yes," Kara finally confesses.

    "Kara."

    "Alex."

    "Why wouldn't you go and meet her?" she asks. "After all of that."

    "All of what?"

    Alex continues to stare at her. "She wrote you a letter, telling you exactly what you want to hear, and you're just - what? You're not even going to meet her?"

    There is something in Alex's voice that doesn't quite fit with Kara's situation. It doesn't not fit, either, but there's a layer to her tone that's just off.

    Kara sets her fork against her plate, eyes on her sister. "Alex?" she asks, voice barely a whisper. "What is it?"

    Alex keeps her own gaze stubbornly away. "She told you what you wanted to hear, did she not?" she asks pointedly.

    Kara nods slowly, because Lena did. She explained her sudden change in behaviour, apologised, confessed deeper feelings, and then asked Kara for another chance. In the grand scheme of things, Kara couldn't have asked for anything more. Which is why it's continually baffling to even her why she's not eagerly awaiting Saturday afternoon.

    "Then why wouldn't you just meet her?" Alex asks. "I would give anything to - "

    "Alex."

    Her eyes snap up. "I told Sam I love her," she says. "For the first time. It was - we were talking about you and Lena, and she wanted to know why I would be helping by agreeing to deliver the letter, and I just - it came out, and I thought - " she stops, sighs. "I've been so careful, you know? I didn't want to overwhelm her with how quickly I fell for her, because I did, Kara. It was almost instant, but she's kept me at arm's length for so long, and I don't usually chase women this way, but I've been so damn careful, and then I - I just told her, because it's all that should matter in this crazy situation, and she didn't even - " she stops.

    Kara gets to her feet and rounds the table to wrap Alex in a hug that they both need.

    "She said nothing," Alex chokes out. "And I know I shouldn't expect her to say it back, but I - she just said nothing, and I can't - what am I supposed to do with that?"

    Kara has no words for her; nothing to soothe her heart in this moment. She could offer platitudes, but she knows Alex doesn't want to hear any of them. Instead, she says, "We both know just what people will do when they're scared."

    Alex blinks slowly. "Is that what you're doing?" she asks. "Not meeting Lena because you're scared?"

    Now, Kara knows she meant what she said when she told Nia that she's learned a lot through this experience. She was scared of feeling again, because she doesn't want to have to go through another loss.

    And yet she already has.

    Not in the profound ways of before, but enough that it burns her chest just to think of Lena and how close they were to being something. But she's experienced it, and she's survived it. Lived through it, and it's shown her that she can go through it all again and be okay.

    That being in love and being loved in return is always going to be worth it.

    So, no, she's not scared. She's just -

    If anything, she's hoping Alex will figure it out without Kara having to put into words what she doesn't even know she's feeling.

    When Kara doesn't respond, Alex sighs and rests her head against Kara's shoulder. Everything feels so heavy; the two of them caught at this precipice in both their personal lives.

    Alex has always been the braver one.

    Kara just follows in her footsteps, as she's always done. Learning from her older sister in all the ways that matter.

    "Sam loves you," Kara tells Alex. She knows it, even if she's met Sam only a handful of times. While Kara can be oblivious about many things in her own life, she's not completely blind. Not when it comes to the people who love her sister almost as much as she does.

    All Kara wants in her life is for the people around her to be happy and loved. It doesn't get any simpler than that.

    But.

    If that's so true, then why wouldn't she want the same for herself?

    On Thursday morning, there is a familiar email from Sam waiting for Lena in her inbox. It's surprising in the sense that Lena was convinced Kara wasn't going to be taking any more of KX3P's calls, so she's not sure what to expect of what Sam has sent.

    Still, she opens the link and listens to the recording.

    It isn't Kara on the line.

    It's not even Carter.

    It's actually Alex.

    "She's with Carter right now," Alex starts. "I'm the sister, by the way. The older, much cooler and totally hotter sister." She laughs, easy. "She'd kill me if she knew I were talking to you, but I'm convinced she'd curl up next to me and hear what I have to say, regardless."

    Nia clears her throat, always a tone of surprise whenever she learns more about Carter, Carter's mother and their family. "You're with her?"

    "We just had some dinner, yeah, and Carter managed to rope her into an entire chapter of Harry Potter, so I'm currently on her couch listening to them giggle about Gilderoy Lockhart through his open door," Alex says. "We probably have more time than I initially thought."

    "How is she doing?" Nia asks, and she's always been so sincere. Lena makes a note to make another donation to the radio's wish fund. "We've been thinking about her and Carter this past week. Hoping they get their happy endings."

    Alex lets out an amused breath. "Happy endings," she muses, and then clears her throat again. "She's doing okay, I think. Sort of. I think she's convinced of it, which makes me want to believe it, but - "

    "But?"

    "You know, Nia, my baby sister is literal sunshine," she says. "I know people probably wouldn't believe me when I say that, given what she's told you about the last year of her life, but she is - she is so good. So full of belief. So damn pure." She puffs out a breath. "It's what makes it so difficult to tell when she's breaking apart inside. I don't even think she's conscious of how she hides it, but I know her now. I didn't always.

    "When we first met, I resented her," Alex admits. "This interloper coming into my family, stealing my parents' attention and just being a gigantic pain in the butt from the get-go. She kept me awake at night, she rarely ate like a normal person, and don't even get me started on just how embarrassing she was in high school. It makes me cringe just thinking about it.

    "I'm not proud of the way I acted the first few months after she came to live with us, because - it was - she was just so sad. Her entire world had changed. Her parents were gone; her home. She was in a new town, with new people and a new everything. I can't even imagine what that must have been like, and I've always been in such awe of her for how she managed to get through that grief and become this wonderful person, who's always determined to see the very best in people.

    "Which is why I have to admit I was sceptical when she first met her wife," Alex says. "I was pretty hard on everyone she dated before, because I'm her older sister and it comes with the territory, but it was different with the two of them. They were boss and employee first, and the woman never did treat my sister with the kindness she deserved until - well. The two of us have just now discovered that she seems to have a bit of a type. Women who hide their affection for her by being mean to her." Alex pauses. "Huh? I suppose, in a completely non-romantic way, I was probably the same."

    "Does L fall into that category?" Nia asks, and Lena actually startles at the sound of her voice. She almost forget she was there.

    "She does," Alex confirms. "Sort of. I think we've always known that L is complicated."

    "Does that mean you've met her?"

    "I have," Alex says. "I'm in the middle of trying to get my sister to take another chance, as it were. I'm just wary of pushing too hard, at this point. She's been through so much, and I want her to be happy, but I - "

    "What?"

    "I'm not entirely convinced she wants the same thing for herself."

    Lena closes her eyes, leaning her head against the back of her chair, because she understands that feeling more than she's willing to admit. A master of her own destruction, truly.

    She doesn't know how much work she'll be able to get done today or tomorrow. Not with the very real possibility she'll get to see Kara on Saturday.

    Or not see her; if Alex is to be believed.

    "Oh," Alex says. "Here she is." There's the sound of muffled conversation, and then more of Alex's soft laughter. "She walked right back out of the room when I told her I'm talking to you," she informs Nia. "She'll be back, don't worry. She's probably just getting her phone, so she can stare longingly at the only picture of L she has."

    There's more muffled noise, and then Alex lets out a yelp. "Ka - " she starts, and then stops. "I'm telling Mom you're throwing cushions at me."

    "She won't believe you!" Kara's voice sounds, loud enough to be heard through the receiver despite her distance from the phone.

    "Everyone now knows how violent you are, pining after your maybe-lover."

    "Shut up."

    Alex laughs. "Nia, did you hear that? My twenty-seven year old sister's best comeback is 'shut up.' I'm convinced I've failed as an older sister."

    "Don't listen to her, Nia!" Kara shouts. "She's being mean to me!"

    "You're going to wake up your kid," Alex points out, and Kara is suddenly quiet. Lena can't stop her smile, just imagining the two of them bickering in what must be Kara's living room.

    "I did hear it," Nia informs Alex, cutting into their sisterly banter. "We all did."

    "Now, she's pouting," Alex tells her. "She doesn't get that I'm just trying to help. I mean, did you know that L wrote her another letter?"

    "No, we didn't," Nia says, and her voice goes just a little high in what must be excitement at the new information. "What did it say?"

    "I think she'll actually kill me if I actually told you - and, as talented a surgeon as I am, even I wouldn't be able to save my own life - but I will say this." She pauses, whether deliberately or not. "It is a terrifying thing to put words to your feelings. I know I've struggled with it in the past. Many people do, I'm sure, because it's one thing to feel something, and something else to be brave enough to say the words out loud for someone else to hear. To be brave enough to put yourself out there, especially when you know it increases the chance of your getting hurt."

    Lena's eyes open and close a few times.

    She doesn't even know at whom these words are directed, but they sound heavy and pointed in a way that suggests they're for someone specific.

    Lena isn't entirely convinced she's that someone, and neither is Kara.

    It's for Sam.

    "I just think it's pretty neat that L would be willing to do that," Alex says. "Just proves to me that it's never too late."

    Yip.

    Definitely for Sam.

    "I hear you're in the business of granting wishes," Alex says, and Lena is convinced she's managed to charm every listener just as much as her sister has.

    "We try," Nia admits.

    "Well, here's mine," Alex says; "My wish is for everyone to have their moment of bravery. Just six seconds of courage. To go for it; to take that chance, and give it your all. You never know what good could come out of it."

    Lena shakes her head, because the optimism doesn't really fit Alex. But Lena gets it. Alex has always come across as brave, even if they've met only a few times. If Lena had to guess who would be the first one to reveal her feelings between Sam and Alex, it was always going to be Alex, and that already has little to do with Sam's hesitancy in relationships.

    Nia is silent for a long, long time, and then she says, "I think you're about to start a second Twitter frenzy, but for yourself, this time."

    Alex laughs, and it is a glorious sound. "Hah, no thank you, I'm good."

    "Not single then?"

    "Not even a little bit," Alex confesses, and Lena can only imagine what it must have felt like for Sam to hear Alex say those words, and so easily. And these: "I'm very happily in love with a woman who deserves every bit of me I have to offer."

    Nia sighs a little dreamily. "You're really not helping yourself."

    Alex laughs, kind and steady, just like her presence. "I was never really on here to help myself, you know," she reminds them. "Only to help my sister."

    God.

    Lena leans back in her chair, because this pair of sisters could have the entire world falling in love with them if left unchecked. They're diabolical, and Lena wonders if they actually know the kind of effect they have on the masses.

    Twitter must still be going crazy.

    "Do you think it's all going to work out?" Nia asks, presenting Alex with the question Lena's sure everyone has at the moment.

    Alex sighs, and it sounds like the heaviest of truths when she says, "I don't know." She hums softly, and Lena imagines she must be smiling at Kara. "For Carter's sake; I really, really hope so."

    Winn accosts Kara as soon as she gets to work, dragging her through the gallery and stopping her from attempting to greet the woman currently studying several of her pieces.

    He practically shoves her into his office, closes the door, and very pointedly says, "You are not allowed to meet that woman."

    Kara's frown deepens, confusion and irritation warring. "Why not?" she asks. "What's happening? Who is she, and why am I in here?"

    "She's Lillian Luthor," Winn says, and it feels as if all the air has been sucked out of the room. Winn shouldn't know why the name is so sobering, because Kara very clearly hasn't mentioned her feelings for Lena to him, but he's enough of a genius to figure out there must be a story there.

    "Oh."

    "Said she was looking for a very specific piece," Winn says. "A commission you agreed to. For her daughter."

    Kara thinks of the night before everything fell apart; the night she had Lena in her studio and in her line of sight. The night she put pencil to paper and began her first attempt at capturing Lena's beauty in the wonders and colours of her paints.

    The painting isn't anywhere near complete. Kara hasn't been able to work on it since the moment Lena drew a line in the sand and asked for things to remain professional.

    "Oh."

    Winn eyes her curiously. "I don't know anything about a commission."

    Kara audibly swallows. "There is no commission."

    Winn's head tilts to the side. "Why do I get the feeling I'm missing something here?"

    Kara glances over her shoulder at the door, crease in her brow. "Who did she ask for, exactly?"

    "The artist known as Supergirl."

    Kara closes her eyes for a long moment. "I'm going to have to talk to her," she says. "She's here for a reason."

    "And, you know what that reason is?"

    Kara gets to her feet, wiping her palms on her jeans. "Not a clue," she says, and then walks out of the office with Winn a few steps behind. She doesn't look at him as she moves through the gallery towards where the woman who is Lillian Luthor is studying an early painting of Kara's. It's one of her own favourites, and she's not sure how comfortable she is with having her younger self so exposed to Lena's mother.

    Kara feels like a child as she approaches the woman, the only resemblance to Lena in the regality with which she carries herself. This woman definitely had a hand in raising Lena, and Kara can't tell if that's a good or a bad thing.

    Well.

    Not until Lillian hears her approach, turns to face her, and narrows her eyes. At Kara's outfit, perhaps, because she's in paint-splattered clothing already, but maybe just at Kara herself.

    Then she says, "So, you're the one who's got my daughter all out of sorts."

    Kara doesn't even know how to respond to that unexpected comment, so she doesn't even try. Instead, she stands perfectly still, gaze steadily meeting Lillian's, and waits patiently for the reason she's here.

    Lillian sighs. "She would stop speaking to me completely if she knew I were here."

    Kara blinks. "She didn't send you?"

    "God, no," Lillian says with a huff. "I'm as surprised as I'm sure you are that she revealed your interactions at all. That is not something we would do in our family."

    From the little she's learned about the Luthor family; Kara has at least gathered that much. "Then, why are you here?"

    "I wanted to meet you," Lillian says, giving Kara another once-over. "I wanted to meet the person who has managed so effectively to unsettle my daughter by doing the thankless task of taking the time to understand her."

    Kara can't make sense of that. If anything, it's her lack of understanding of Lena that's landed her in this mess. "But you don't even know me."

    "I don't need to," Lillian says. "Not yet, at least."

    Kara just continues to stare at her, unsure what to make of this entire interaction. This is not what she expected of her day, and she doesn't know where she goes from here.

    "I have a feeling we'll be seeing more of each other," Lillian says, and it sounds only slightly ominous. "If we're both lucky, Lena will continue to want us in her life." Her head tilts slightly, eyes boring into Kara's. "And if we're smart, which I like to think you have to be, we'll do what we can to stay." She turns away then, attention drifting to Winn over Kara's shoulder. "Is the 2017 'Light of Dark' for sale?"

    Kara almost gets the feeling the woman would buy out the entire gallery just to prove a point.

    Instead, she leaves with the 2017 'Light of Dark' in her possession, and makes sure to remind Kara she'll be seeing her very soon.

    It's honestly enough to terrify her.

    Sam is the one who tells her, "Alex doesn't think Kara will go," and Lena isn't even surprised by the insider information. It was a bit of a Hail Mary; a last ditch attempt to tell Kara her truth in person and ask for another chance to get it right.

    "Are you sure?" Lena asks anyway, trying to keep the disappointment out of her voice. "Kara could change her mind."

    Sam hums, and Lena is grateful they're having this conversation over the phone, because her facial expression must be something pained. "Lena," Sam says. "I think maybe she's going to need more time."

    "As in, tomorrow is too soon?"

    "Are you even ready for what it could mean that she did show up?"

    Lena closes her eyes, leaning her head back as she attempts to relax on her couch. She's been unsuccessful since she got home, unable to lose the tension in her body. Too many things are happening, and not nearly enough.

    Tonight was supposed to be spent breaking the news to Jack that -

    Well, she wants other things, with someone else, which is about as simple as it gets.

    But Jack had to cancel, citing some kind of work emergency, and this conversation with Sam is the only reason she agrees when Jack suggests a Saturday brunch in lieu of their proposed Friday dinner.

    Regardless of what happens with Kara, she knows she can't keep doing this with Jack. Whatever it is. She owes him that much. She owes herself as well. And Kara.

    But.

    If Kara is not going to show up, then why would -

    Lena is never optimistic. It's just not in her nature - particularly when it comes to other human beings. She's learned to expect the worst of people - especially if they happen to hold the surname of Luthor.

    Lena is not an exception to her own rule.

    It's why she asks Jack the question when she sees him, the two of them meeting at a relatively popular brunch spot that Lena registers is five blocks away from the National City Zoo.

    No.

    She's not thinking about that.

    Instead, she's asking Jack, "What do you see in me?" between bites of her bagel.

    The question obviously catches him off guard because his eyes widen and his fork freezes on the way up to his mouth. Then he says, "My future," with a charming smile, and Lena knows she's making the correct decision ending this. Whatever this is.

    "You didn't think that six years ago," she finds herself saying, unable to stop herself.

    Jack's face falls, and Lena almost regrets her words. Still, she doesn't take them back, because she definitely hasn't forgotten how it felt in the aftermath of their loss. Feeling him pull away; watching as he attempted to put his life back together and continue with his plans that never truly considered a child.

    "I was a different person, then," Jack says, setting his fork on his plate. "We both were." He pauses. "We were also young, Lena. You can't tell me our lives would have turned out this way if we - uh - stayed together."

    Lena clenches her jaw, because they've never actually talked about it. Jack has always skirted around the loss of their baby, pretending as if none of it happened. As if it all happened to people who aren't the two of them.

    "You mean if I had the baby," she says, and hates how he flinches.

    "Lena."

    "You were relieved."

    Jack looks away, guilty.

    Lena has never held that against him in the way she imagines she would have if she were any other person. There are only a handful of people who even know about the miscarriage, and even fewer who even know the reasons why.

    Lena has never explicitly told anyone that the likelihood of her ever carrying a child to full term is minimal enough that Lena's scientific brain would not be willing to try.

    Her heart wouldn't be able to handle it.

    Sam has managed to deduce it, Lena thinks, and Kara must have figured it out, too. It is a scary, unmooring thing, being unable to fulfil one of the things her own body is supposed to be able to give her.

    Family.

    It is all she's ever wanted.

    And she knows she's not going to have that with Jack.

    Lena clears her throat. "We both know this won't work," she says. "You and me."

    Jack looks a little thrown by her words, and she can only wonder how that could be. Surely, he's picked up on the distance she maintains. He's not as smart as her, but he's a genius, too.

    Well.

    Emotions tend to be difficult for people like them.

    "Lena," he says. "What are you saying?"

    Lena suspects he needs her to spell it out for him; make it explicitly clear to him. So she does. She tells him exactly what he needs to hear for her to end this before it can truly begin again.

    They don't work, because she suspects they still want different things. They don't work, because he's made it apparent he actually doesn't like National City, and would move here only for her. They don't work, because Lena isn't in love with him anymore, and she hasn't been in some years now.

    Perhaps, her love for him died with their unborn child.

    She doesn't say that last part, because she doesn't actually want to hurt him, but Jack must sense it anyway.

    His shoulders slump. "I never stood a chance, did I?" he asks, almost rhetorically, and Lena doesn't respond. "I will admit I might have predicted this outcome. Sometimes, when you look at me, it's the same expression you wore that night. When everything was so wrong, and you didn't believe anything could ever be right again." He looks away, jaw clenched. "I ruined our chances back then, didn't I?"

    Lena wouldn't exactly say that, because she's convinced they could have worked if Lena hadn't met Kara. For a while, at least.

    But, then again, she was resisting his overtures long before Supergirl flew into her life.

    Perhaps it is a kindness when Lena doesn't respond.

    Jack sighs. "I think I've known for a while," he says. "Was it so wrong of me to want to give this another chance?"

    "Of course not, Jack," she says, smiling sadly. "Maybe we would have deserved it, after how things ended the first time."

    "You told me you never wanted to see me again and threw a microscope at my head."

    "It didn't actually hit you."

    "You're lucky I have such good reflexes."

    Lena shouldn't smile, but he's smiling, and she attempts to match him. "I'm sorry I couldn't tell you sooner," she confesses, because she is. They've been doing this dance for some months now, and it's taken falling for someone else - however subconsciously - for her to realise she wants something more than Jack has to offer.

    Even if that ends up not being with Kara.

    Jack must be able to read something in her expression, because he very carefully asks, "Did something prompt this?"

    It feels insensitive to tell him the truth at this point, but she wants to. Maybe he deserves to hear it from her, but she also needs to be able to tell someone else. Someone who isn't Sam or Jess or her therapist, and Lena has so few people in her life she could trust with this part of her.

    "I met someone."

    She blurts the words, which is uncharacteristic for her, but she needs to tell someone else. If anything, Jack truly is one of her closest friends.

    Jack blinks, clearly not expecting that. "You met someone?"

    "Nothing ever actually happened," she makes sure to tell him, because that's important. "But we were so close, and I - I'm afraid I messed it up, already," she reveals. "God, Jack, fear is such a terrible thing."

    Jack continues to stare at her, almost in disbelief.

    Lena can feel a blush spreading across her cheeks and down her neck. "It's just - she did this wonderful thing for me, and I repaid her by showing her what it means to be a Luthor."

    Jack, to his credit, manages to pull himself together enough to make the connection himself. There is really only one option, because he too has met this particular someone. There really isn't anyone else Lena could be referring to, when she mentions a 'she.'

    "Oh, Lena," he says, lifting a hand and calling for another round of mimosas before leaning forward with interest. "Tell me everything."

    From the moment Carter wakes on Saturday morning, he asks Kara what time they're leaving for the zoo every ten minutes. Without fail. Like clockwork.

    It is slowly driving Kara mad.

    Okay.

    Not slowly.

    She loves her son, of course, but he's entirely too persistent and dedicated, and Kara should probably be more worried that he's so determined to go to the zoo. With or without her.

    "To give L a hug," Carter argues. "We have to."

    Kara doesn't have the heart to remind him that the invitation in the letter didn't actually extend to him.

    Carter frowns adorably. "Miss Luthor likes big cats."

    Kara's heart stutters at the referral to Lena as the woman Carter has actually met. She also already knows Lena's opinions on these particular big cats, because Carter has been persistent in reminding her about that, as well.

    "But my favourite is the tiger," he informs her. "That's why she picked it."

    Kara looks to the ceiling, begging for patience. "We're not going anywhere today," she tells him for what must be the fiftieth time in the past few hours, but he just smiles innocently, and she knows he's been spending far too much time with Alex.

    No.

    Never too much time.

    Just learning all the wrong things from her.

    Kara spends minutes upon minutes just sighing, because there are only so many times she can tell Carter, in no uncertain terms, that they are not leaving this apartment today.

    It's when she realises that the expression on his face has little to do with Alex and actually more to do with Cat that she really worries. If he can channel his mother at seven, she knows she's in for an exciting few years. Rest of her life.

    It reaches its inevitable head the closer they get to twelve o'clock, and it explodes into a fight that Kara's not proud of. Carter yells things like 'but this is what Mama wanted' and 'why aren't you even trying?' and 'you said this was what you wanted, too,' and it ends with Kara sending him to his room in a huff and immediately feeling guilty about it.

    Cat would love all of this, truly. She's probably deeply amused by it all, wherever she is, and it's the only reason Kara doesn't allow the feeling of disappointing her son to overwhelm her.

    She's not as ready as she thought she was. Maybe diving into such an intense relationship as her first one post-widowhood wasn't a good idea. Maybe she put too much pressure on herself.

    Maybe a lot of things.

    With a sigh, Kara makes her way to the kitchen. If she won't take Carter to the zoo; the least she can do is make him one of his favourite foods for lunch: grilled cheese. That's another of Alex's influences, which Kara will accept isn't all bad. It's a sister to pizza, as far as she's concerned.

    She puts on some music and gathers her ingredients, choosing rather to spread mayonnaise over the back of the bread instead of butter. Eliza has had the thankless task of teaching Kara how to prepare more than just ramen and peanut butter sandwiches, now that she's Carter's sole guardian.

    The kid needs his vegetables, apparently.

    Her phone starts to ring before she can get started, and she darts into her bedroom to retrieve it from its charging station. It's Alex, and Kara has to force herself to answer it, given what she knows her sister is bound to say.

    She sighs heavily and answers with a deadpan, "Hello, Alex."

    "You better not still be at home."

    Kara rolls her eyes as she steps towards her window to peak through the curtains. She wouldn't put it past her sister coming over and literally dragging her out of here. Alex has done crazier things in the past. "I'm making lunch."

    "Kara, it's already noon," Alex points out.

    Kara leans forward, forehead pressed to the glass as she takes in her view of the closest road. "Alex, I already told you I wasn't going."

    "Kara."

    "Alex."

    "You're making a mistake."

    Kara sucks in a breath, because it's something she already knows. Either way, it's her own mistake to make, and she's come to accept it.

    Maybe.

    "I reserve the right to say 'I told you so,'" Alex says when Kara doesn't respond, but she doesn't sound amused anymore. She just sounds sad, and Kara really doesn't want to keep having this conversation.

    "Okay."

    Alex sighs. "Kara."

    "We're still on for brunch tomorrow?"

    "We are," Alex confirms.

    "Until then," Kara says, hangs up, and then goes to the bathroom. She stares at herself in the mirror for the longest time, before she rinses her face with cold water, and finally goes to make their grilled cheese sandwiches.

    It gives her something on which to focus, her mind distracted by getting the perfect crust on the bread and the gooey, melty cheese in the middle.

    She doesn't even think of Lena possibly waiting for her.

    Well.

    Not until she just does.

    She shakes her head, clearing it. She's made her decision. It'll hurt less, in the long run. Home is good. She's safe here.

    "Carter," Kara eventually calls out, carefully setting one of the grilled cheese sandwiches on a plate and finding a knife to slice it in half. "Lunch is ready." She cuts the sandwich on the diagonal, which is the way Carter prefers, and then moves towards the fridge to retrieve the salad she made the night before.

    Kara has to make sure Carter eats his greens, even though she would like nothing less for herself. Ugh. Salad.

    "Carter," she calls again, hearing nothing in response. Usually, she can hear movement, his getting himself up and ready to come eat, but there's just silence. "Carter?" she tries again. "It's time for lunch."

    Kara won't admit where her nightmares have taken her before. She's spent numerous nights caught in a horrific loop of checking in on her loved ones and just finding them not breathing in their beds. Rationally, she knows how unlikely it is, but she calls Carter's name once more, gets nothing in response, and then moves towards his bedroom.

    Heart thumping against her ribcage, Kara knocks once on his closed door, and then pushes it open. Expecting the worst, and actually finding the room empty. Which is maybe better, but also -

    "Carter," she says, already just knowing he's not here.

    He's not anywhere, because Kara immediately notices that his backpack is missing from its usual spot beside his small desk.

    Kara commends herself for remaining as calm as she does as she turns around and returns to the kitchen to locate her phone. She calls Alex, and gets her voicemail, which might actually be a good thing, because they're in a good place now and Kara wouldn't want to ruin it all over again.

    Still.

    "I swear, Alexandra Danvers, if anything happens to my son, you are going to be eating lettuce wraps for the rest of your life," Kara tells her, and then hangs up. She looks to the ceiling, groans loudly, and then goes to put on her shoes.

    Kara already knows where he is, and why.

    Well.

    This is definitely one way for Carter to get her to change her mind.

    Chapter 10

    X

    "Do you mean to tell me this woman could be waiting for you right now?" Jack asks, tone incredulous, and Lena glances at her watch. Winces at the time. It's nearly one o'clock, and Lena actually hopes that Kara hasn't actually showed up.

    What kind of disaster would it be if Kara were actually waiting this entire time? Or a saving grace, if Lena is being honest. Maybe Lena's not the right person for her, at all.

    "I - maybe," Lena finally says. "I wouldn't know."

    "Lena."

    "Jack."

    "What on earth are you still doing here?" Jack asks, and his voice is soft with kind understanding. If nothing else will come of their relationship; this man will always be a person who knows her. "She could be waiting."

    "She's not."

    "Maybe she is," Jack tells her. "And, believe me, the 'what if' of that will eat you alive." He sighs, heavy with something like regret. "Wouldn't you rather know?"

    "Truthfully," she says. "No."

    "Why not?"

    "My reality very rarely plays out well," she says. "I just - I thought I could - " she stops.

    "Lena."

    She looks away, trapping her bottom lip between her teeth. "What if she's not there?"

    "What if she is?"

    Obviously, Lena wants nothing more than for Kara to be there, but that's also so terrifying in itself. What happens after?

    "Lena," Jack says, and she finally looks at him. "You can be happy. You're allowed to be." His pause is deliberate. "Without her."

    Lena doesn't need him to clarify to whom he's referring, because she already knows. And, frankly, the words hold power only because they're coming from Jack. The one person who was closest to her at that time in her life. He says the words, and it feels -

    They are freeing.

    "Oh."

    Jack smiles, tinged with sadness. "Is that what you needed to hear?" he asks.

    Lena doesn't respond; bending to retrieve her purse. "I need to go," she says.

    "You need to go," Jack agrees.

    She goes still, eyes meeting his. "Thank you, Jack."

    He shrugs. "I'm pretty great, aren't I?"

    "You are," she agrees sadly. She wishes such great things for him, but they both know they're meant for something different.

    "It's okay," Jack assures her, probably sensing her hesitance just to leave him here. "Get out of here," he says. "Go get your Supergirl."

    Kara finds Carter sitting on a bench opposite the tiger exhibit, pouting rather aggressively. His legs are propped up on his backpack that's tucked a little under the bench and his arms are folded across his chest.

    She gets to the zoo at a speed she's convinced isn't human, and then skids to a stop when she spots him, shoulders hunched and unshed tears in his eyes. He's a heartbreaking sight, and Kara has to accept that this is also one of the reasons she was reluctant to come to the zoo.

    Better Carter be angry with her for not going than have his heart broken by a woman he's met only once.

    Kara's worry and anger disappear in a beat, and she moves to sit beside him, startling him enough that he scoots away from her before he realises it's her. Then he scrambles forward, right into her lap, and holds onto her in a way that tells her he always expected her to come after him.

    As she always will.

    "She's not here," he says, and the sound of his trembling voice is jarring. "Mommy, she didn't come."

    Kara holds him in her lap, hand smoothing over the back of his head. "Oh, honey," she murmurs. "I'm sorry."

    "Why didn't she come?"

    Kara looks to the sky for some guidance, hoping for something; anything. She wouldn't be able to say why Lena's not here, because even she couldn't explain why she didn't want to show up, either.

    Maybe they're both just so scared of what it would mean to meet each other here. She just doesn't know how to explain that to a seven-year-old.

    So she doesn't try. Just says, "I'm sorry," again. She kisses the side of his head, hugging him even tighter to her chest. They sit there for a while, and Kara can almost forget why they're even here.

    If she ever sees Lena again; Kara's going to give her a word or two about disappointing her son.

    Which.

    Okay.
