    Kara eventually shifts to look at Carter's flushed face. "Do you want to keep looking at the animals?" she asks, because they're already here, so they may as well take advantage of it.

    But Carter shakes his head. "I wanna go home," he mumbles.

    "Okay," she says, getting to her feet with him still in her arms and ignoring the eyes that glance their way at the display. It's unfortunate that she's used to it, but she just starts walking, Carter already knowing to hold onto her. "We can get ice-cream on the way."

    It's the first real smile she's got out of him all day.

    Lena regrets the stilettos before she even arrives at the zoo.

    She also deeply regrets choosing the tiger exhibit, because it's one of the furthest from the main entrance. Lena would run, but the last thing she wants to do is break a heel before she gets to her destination, even though it's been years since she's done such a thing. But she has to acknowledge the ground is uneven enough without adding speed to the equation.

    Lena also doesn't want to speed up the disappointment of arriving and not finding Kara there. Still, as much as she tries to delay the inevitable, the absence of Kara when she arrives is still heartbreaking. She spends a few minutes looking around, just in case she's missed her, but there's nobody who resembles Kara waiting.

    Lena lets out a sigh, taking out her phone to check if she's received any messages. There are two from Sam that she takes note of, but the first only wishes her luck while the second asks for an update, and then ignores the rest from everyone else.

    Kara isn't here.

    It's already past one-thirty, which means -

    Lena knows Kara would have been on time for this if she were actually coming. What if she came, waited, and then left? What if this is it? What if Lena messed up her chances even further by not being on time?

    What if -

    Lena freezes when she sees it. In the great scheme of things, it shouldn't stand out to her in any way. But it does, and she can't ignore it.

    Lena's steps carry her towards a bench, spotting a child's colourful backpack under said bench. It's familiar in the sense that she's certain she's seen it before. Somewhere. In a picture.

    Of Kara and her son, their backs to the camera as they looked out at the llamas.

    Lena's heart races in her chest the closer she gets. If that backpack belongs to Carter, then -

    Lena drops heavily onto the bench, bending to retrieve the backpack from beneath the metal. It's heavier than she expected, and she lifts it into her lap. She looks for a tag, finding only the name CARTER G. written on one of the straps in black sharpie.

    Well.

    Okay.

    If this bag is here, then that means Carter was here.

    Which means Kara was here.

    And now she's not.

    Lena's shoulders slump, which is the only way she's willing to show her disappointment. She knows she shouldn't do it, but Lena can't stop herself from unzipping the main compartment of the backpack, her breath catching at what she sees.

    It's her radio.

    Well, it's Carter's radio, now, but she recognises it as once belonging to her. With everything already going on, she managed to forget that Sam must have got her hands on it somehow, and Lena just never noticed it was missing. That's just another thing she and Sam are going to need to talk about.

    Beneath the radio is a stuffed tiger, and Lena's fingers gently touch its fur. It's so soft under her fingertips, and she can just imagine how Carter covets this plush toy.

    It is heartbreaking that she might never get to see it in person.

    Which is really the moment she hears it. Running feet and a tiny voice shouting, "I left it under the bench."

    And then Carter is standing right in front of her, eyes wide as they dart between her face and his backpack in her lap. He opens his mouth to say something - anything - but Kara runs up behind him before he can get a word out, and then the three of them are just there, frozen in front of the tiger exhibit and -

    "Lena," Kara says, surprise in her voice.

    Very slowly, as if she's worried they'll disappear if she makes too fast of a move, Lena gets to her feet. "Kara," she murmurs, and then blinks. "Carter."

    Kara takes a breath. "You're here," she says, her hands settling on Carter's shoulders and holding him still.

    Lena audibly swallows. "I'm sorry I'm late," she says, fighting the urge to shrug. Lena Luthor does not shrug. She looks down at the backpack in her hands, uncharacteristically nervous. "And, I'm afraid I've forgotten the donuts."

    It's Carter who says, "You weren't here," in the kind of voice that strikes Lena deep in her chest. Slashes at the softest parts of her. "I waited."

    Lena looks at Kara, because Carter definitely says, 'I waited,' and not we. The expression on Kara's face is unreadable, but Lena knows this isn't about the two of them right now. Not today and not in this moment.

    Lena takes steps forward, and then goes down onto her haunches to look right into Carter's eyes. "Can I tell you a secret?" she asks quietly, and waits for him to nod. "I was scared."

    "Of what?" he asks.

    "You."

    Carter looks surprised by that revelation, and he looks up at Kara for a moment before his gaze lands on Lena, once more. "Why me?"

    Lena sets the backpack on the ground between them, regretting her heels once more. And her skirt. Honestly, she's not at all dressed for the zoo. "All I wanted was to make your Christmas wish come true," she confesses. "I didn't want you to be disappointed when you learned it was me."

    Carter's expression shifts into a frown. "But why?" he asks, again. "You're perfect." He looks at Kara again. "Tell her, Mommy."

    Kara doesn't even hesitate before she says, "You're perfect," and her tone is heavy with meaning that only Lena is supposed to be able to hear.

    Carter beams at Lena, as if those two words should put an end to that one paralysing fear of hers. "We're getting ice-cream," he declares. "You should come with us."

    "Carter," Kara says, and her tone of voice is a little hard. A warning.

    Carter's eyes widen, and he immediately backtracks. "I mean, please will you get ice-cream with us? Please, please, please."

    Lena looks up at Kara, because the last thing she wants is to overstep here. She's obviously already made such a big mess of everything, and she doesn't want to agree to something when she really shouldn't.

    But Kara just says, "Please join us for ice-cream," in a voice that isn't exactly warm, even if her eyes are.

    Carter does a little jump in place, his smile getting impossibly wider.

    Lena couldn't decline the invitation even if she wanted to, so she doesn't. Which is how she finds herself being pulled through the zoo with Carter's hand in one of her own, and his backpack securely held in the other.

    Kara follows in silence, thoughtful expression on her face but amusement in her eyes.

    It is not every day Kara can find something - anything - to occupy her son's mind enough that he forgets about his ice-cream. He loves it too much, and ice-cream is usually a sure way to get him out of his own head. It's like magic, sometimes, that it can be his singular focus; everything falling to the wayside.

    Well.

    Not until Lena Luthor, apparently.

    Kara sits across from the two of them, her own ice-cream propped on the table in front of her, and watches in slight fascination as Carter talks to Lena as if he's known her his entire life. It is such a strange thing to witness, because he's usually so reserved when it comes to new people. Lena's not exactly a stranger, but Carter doesn't really know her at all.

    And yet.

    Lena's facial expression also isn't anything Kara's ever seen. She's so focused on every word Carter says - he's currently telling her about the gestational period of a tiger - and it looks as if she can't believe anything that's happening is actually happening.

    As if Carter's going to disappear any moment, and she'd have imagined the entire thing.

    Kara is familiar with the feeling.

    She says very little, herself; rather just watching and listening and wondering what's going to happen now. Obviously, she and Lena need to talk, but she's not sure she's ready for what that would mean for her and for them right now.

    Or, ever.

    So, she just eats her ice-cream and chimes in whenever Carter requests her input on something. He's very clearly in his element, basking in the attention Lena's giving him. Even Lena has to nudge his ice-cream into his eye-line to get him to eat it before it melts.

    Who knew Lena Luthor was a superior pick-me-up to ice-cream? Kara certainly didn't. Or she did. She doesn't even know, anymore.

    She knows nothing.

    Eventually, Carter does finish his ice-cream, scooping it up like a soup after the remainder has successfully melted in its cup. Then he looks hopefully at Kara, and she just knows she's going to give in to whatever he's about to request.

    Tigers.

    He wants to see the tigers.

    Lena casts a look her way, eyes soft and gentle, and Kara never quite stood a chance, did she?

    She sighs internally, and then says, "Well, I guess we're going back to see the tigers, aren't we?"

    Which is all fine and good, right until the moment Carter runs up to the edge of the tigers' enclosure to get a closer look and Kara is left standing beside Lena and struggling to think of something to say that isn't 'did you mean what you said in your letter?'

    Kara glances at her a few times, and Lena looks back every time. The green of her eyes is intense, silently asking a question Kara doesn't know the words to. She's a bit of an odd sight, standing tall in a pencil skirt and crisp white blouse, the highest heels and dark makeup. It's all compounded by the colourful backpack she has over her right shoulder, but it's obvious she wasn't dressing for coming and actually experiencing the zoo today. Despite it all, she's still the most beautiful person Kara has ever seen.

    It actually makes her smile, and Lena furrows her brow when she sees it. "What?" Lena asks, curious.

    Kara shakes her head, feeling just a little brave as she reaches for Lena's closest hand and laces their fingers together. Lena lets out a startled little breath at the action, but she gently squeezes Kara's hand in response and neither of them mentions it.

    They have so much to talk about, of course, but Kara is content to stand here with Lena's hand in her own and watch her son slowly lose his mind over the animals in the exhibits.

    Something about this moment feels monumental in a way Kara wouldn't be able to explain. She's just here with Lena and Carter, and it means something.

    Lena, who is quiet and reserved, when she's usually all confidence and purpose. It's a strange dichotomy, and Kara doesn't know how to handle the perceived power she has over the next steps they can take going forward.

    It is Lena who breaks their silence, though. Voice low enough to be heard, but not the usual lilt that Kara has come to adore. "Thank you."

    Kara turns her head, blinking. "For what?"

    "Coming."

    Kara looks away, debating whether it makes sense to reveal that she didn't actually come. Carter forced her hand, and now that same hand is holding Lena's.

    "I wasn't going to," Lena says before Kara can respond. "I had myself convinced you wouldn't come, so I thought I would save myself the disappointment." She steps a little closer to Kara, meeting her gaze steadily. "Between Sam and myself, we're really masters of our own destruction."

    Kara doesn't really want to be thinking about Sam right now; still feeling a little uncertain about all of that. "What changed your mind?" she asks.

    Lena traps her bottom lip between her teeth for a moment, and then quietly says, "Jack, actually."

    Kara says nothing, unsure how to feel about that.

    "We had brunch this morning," Lena explains. "I ended things properly, and then I told him about you. I - I think I needed him to do for me what you believe I've done for you."

    "What's that?"

    "Given me the permission I wasn't aware I needed to be happy," Lena reveals. "Without her."

    The 'her' she refers to is obviously different to Kara's, but the sentiment remains. Loss has made them cautious; made them believe they're undeserving.

    "It needed to come from him," Lena says. "Which is so silly, really, but I - it makes sense in a way."

    "It does," Kara agrees. Then she sighs. "I wasn't coming," she confesses, eyes tracking Carter's movement as he follows one of the larger tigers along the perimeter of the enclosure. "I wouldn't have come, if Carter didn't escape from his room like the little bandit I've decided he is."

    "Oh."

    Kara can feel Lena's gaze on her, but she can't bring herself to look at her. "Do you know what the last thing my wife said to me was?" she says, unexpected to them both. "The night before. Just before we went to sleep. She told me, if she allowed herself to be more selfish; she would take me with her, wherever she was going, and I remember thinking that I would go willingly." She closes her eyes, wanting to banish and treasure the memory simultaneously. "I think about that more often than I should. I think about how life would be, if things were - if just one thing was different. And I don't like that I do that."

    "Why?"

    "Because it kept me from coming to meet you."

    "Kara."

    "You told me I had time; that we had time," Kara says. "You said it was okay that I wasn't ready."

    "I meant it all," Lena rushes to say, and her body turns to face Kara properly. Head-on, full frontal. Open and bare. "Of course I did. I'm sorry I let you ever think otherwise." She breathes out. "I guess I just let my own fear get in the way of - "

    "Of what?"

    "Coming to meet you, too."

    Kara's laugh is unexpected, a sudden sound splitting the space between them. It's just a little hysterical, and Lena looks at her with worry clear in her features.

    "Kara?"

    "Lena?"

    "Are you okay?"

    "I'm great," Kara assures her, and it's not entirely a lie. "I just - I have to ask: what are you doing for the rest of your life?"

    Lena startles at the sound of the question, and Kara can't stop her smile. Because this entire thing is ridiculous. Just ridiculous.

    "Rest of your day, maybe," she offers instead. "We'll start there, and you can answer the other question whenever you're ready."

    "Um."

    They both jump when Carter's voice calls out to Kara. "Mommy, look, they're about to feed them," he says, tossing a look over his shoulder at the pair of them. "I wonder what food they give them."

    Steak, apparently.

    Carter steals Lena's attention again, and Kara doesn't even mind. It's easier to make this day about Carter, because he's always going to be Kara's priority. It really, really helps that it appears Lena understands and accepts that.

    He's a priority for her, too.

    Lena expects the day to end the moment Carter turns to his mother and says, "I'm hungry."

    Kara just looks amused when she gently rubs his shoulder and says, "Well, it's not my fault someone decided to skip out on lunch, is it?"

    "Mommy," Carter whines, and Lena can't stop her smile. She could spend forever just watching them; endlessly fascinated by this adorable, little family.

    Still, Lena is convinced this is where their day comes to an end. Kara and Carter will go somewhere - possibly home - to get their late lunch, and Lena will go to her own home and be left to fret over just where they now stand.

    Which is why it's a surprise when Kara - Kara - is the one to invite her along for pizza. She does it just a little shyly, though her eyes almost dare Lena to refuse the offer. As if she would ever do something so silly - again.

    She's done fighting herself, and she hopes Kara can tell.

    Carter begs her to say yes, which is really a no-brainer, and Carter spends the entire walk to the pizza place telling her about his new school that's actually his old school. Lena glances at Kara, who is watching them in turn, asking the silent question.

    "We're back in National City," Kara confirms, tiny crease in her brow. "It felt like time to come home."

    "We're staying in Mommy's old place," Carter tells Lena. "It doesn't smell as much like Mama."

    Lena audibly swallows, squeezing the hand she's holding. Carter's palm suddenly feels so much smaller against her own, and she's reminded that he's only seven years old, merely months on from the loss of his birth mother, and it -

    Sometimes, Lena is relieved she doesn't remember too much about her own birth mother. The memories would haunt her, probably, and it is perhaps a mercy that all that plagues her now is the lack of those memories. Can't exactly miss what you never had, and all that.

    The expression on Kara's face is enough confirmation for Lena, because there's a pain there that Lena wishes on nobody. Especially not herself. And, what's worse is that Lena knows Kara's pain isn't even her own in this moment; it's Carter's. As if the worst thing right now is that she's unable to bear the brunt of Carter's pain for him. Just to take it all away.

    Lena can't imagine being that selfless.

    Or, perhaps she can. She keeps learning more and more about her own self, just as much as she's learning about Kara. It's supposed to be the hallmark of a good relationship, isn't it? Lena wouldn't know.

    What she does know, though - and with sudden clarity - is that this is what she wants. A life with Kara and Carter; with Sam and Ruby and Alex. Even Jess. Sometimes, even her mother.

    But definitely this, with Kara and Carter now sitting across from her and indulging in more pizza than Lena considers humanly possible. It's one thing to know they're mother and son, but it's another thing to see it with her own two eyes. She's aware they're not biologically related, but there's an argument to be made about nature versus nurture when it comes to a person's love of pizza.

    Carter even manages to convince Lena to have three slices, and that's pushing it. Extra trips to the gym and some salad are definitely on the cards for her.

    It's when Carter begs Kara to go and play on one the pinball machines in the small arcade in the corner, eyes imploring and a little nervous at the mere idea of actually doing such a thing, that Lena learns about all the other cards in her future.

    Kara keeps half an eye on Carter when he scurries away, but the rest of her attention is on Lena. They're going to talk, apparently, in a crowded pizza restaurant full of nuclear families and teenagers on first dates.

    At the moment, Lena feels as if she's a bit of both.

    Kara's smile is gentle as she sips her iced tea. "On a scale of one to ten, just how tired are you of talking about cats?"

    Lena laughs unexpectedly, a little caught off guard by Kara's casualness. "I - yes, I do suppose I'm a little out of big cat facts at the moment."

    If possible, Kara's smile actually softens. "Thank you for indulging him," she says. "I'm sure this isn't what you expected out of your day."

    "It far exceeds my expectations," Lena says before she can stop herself, and she doesn't even regret it. She's just going to roll with it, from now on. "I - I should be saying thank you to you for letting me tag along." She looks down at the table, unable to look into the blue eyes that have claimed her. "After everything that's happened - "

    "What did happen?" Kara cuts in.

    Lena's gaze snaps up. "Did you - Kara, did you not get my letter?" she asks, because that's impossible. Lena definitely explained herself in the letter, which Kara definitely received. Because she's here.

    "I want to know, from you," Kara says. "I thought - " she stops. "I just thought we were on the same page." She blinks slowly. "I practically tripped into your lap."

    Lena manages a smile, blushes on both their cheeks.

    Kara grins. "I practically fell for you," she adds, delighted with herself. "That is literally a story we'll be able to tell for forever."

    It feels strange, being on such uncertain ground. Before she learned that Kara and Carter's mother were one in the same, Lena was convinced she knew where she stood. Maybe. The idea of being with Kara wasn't as terrifying as it suddenly became when she was reminded that this family already had a wife and mother who loved them with every breath in her lungs.

    How does Lena ever compare to that?

    "Will you tell me about your wife?" Lena suddenly asks, and Kara's smile slips right off her face. She goes still, eyes a little wide, and Lena immediately regrets ever asking. "You don't have to," she says. "God. That was insensitive, wasn't it?"

    "I - it was maybe just out of the blue," Kara says, which is kind of her. "What would you like to know?"

    Lena wouldn't be able to say.

    Kara leans forward, resting her arms on the table. "The way I see it, we've done everything in the wrong order," she says. "I mean, you've already met my son, and we haven't even gone on a first date yet."

    Lena can't quite get a read on Kara, but her mouth ends up saying, "You may as well just move in, then," without much thought, and they both end up laughing at the ridiculousness of it all.

    At least it eases whatever tension has seeped into their interaction.

    Then Kara says, "You actually met her."

    Lena frowns. "I did?" she asks, and can't make sense of the blooming blush on Kara's cheeks. "How could you even know that?"

    "Because you told me," Kara says. "When we were in your lab."

    "What?"

    Kara sits a moment, allowing Lena to work it out for herself. It comes slowly, the memory of their conversation on that day, because Lena just remembers her own pounding heart and the sheer monumental fact that she had Kara in her personal laboratory.

    The only thing other than her projects they talked about was -

    Well, just the magazine.

    A very specific article in said magazine.

    About Cat Grant.

    Kara tilts her head a little to the left, peeking over Lena's shoulder. "My son's name is Carter Grant," she reveals, and Lena just stares at her in disbelief. "I was the assistant Cat once asked you about."

    "Oh, my God."

    Kara just watches her, trying to gauge her reaction. "Lena?"

    Lena blinks several times in quick succession. "You were married to Cat Grant?" she asks, never ever imagining she would be saying those words to Kara. It doesn't even - what on Earth -

    "Lena?"

    And, frankly, she's sure she surprises them both when she starts laughing. It's just a little hysterical, sharp against her own ears, and Kara looks bemused by it all.

    "Lena?" Kara asks again, small smile on her face. "I mean, of all the ways I thought you would react; I didn't think you'd actually laugh."

    "I'm sorry," Lena says as she tries to control her laughter, a hand over her mouth. "I don't - it's just - "

    "What?"

    "She's why I wrote the last letter," Lena tells her, laughter tapering off. "I actually read the article when I was in my lab, and she said some words in there that - they helped me make sense of my own feelings. Allowed me to believe I could deserve you." Those words are, perhaps, heavier than either of them can handle at the moment, and Lena's relieved that Kara chooses to pick at a different point.

    "Wait," Kara says, blinking slowly. "Are you trying to tell me that my late wife, whom you actually knew, is the reason you're - that we're here?"

    Lena doesn't usually believe in fate. She's too much of a scientist for something as frivolous as that, but there's a part of her that can't quite ignore everything that's led to this moment.

    To Kara sitting across from her and looking at her with those perfect eyes, her own heart worn all over her face.

    "You could say that," Lena says.

    Kara slides her feet across the floor until they're touching Lena's under the table. "I could say that," she echoes, just a little wonder in her voice. "She really was something else, wasn't she?"

    "Reckon she'd approve of me?" Lena asks, intending for it be a joke, but it comes out sounding far too serious even to her own ears. It's now obvious to Kara it's something that's been playing on her mind. Endlessly. Practically on a loop.

    Kara's expression softens. "Is that why you've been so hesitant?" she asks. "It's not just Carter's approval you're worried about?"

    "I just - " she starts and stops. She's never been this tongue-tied over a woman before, and it's definitely starting to irritate her. Her words have always been her best weapon.

    But, then again, Kara isn't just any woman.

    "You just what?" Kara prompts.

    "I just can't seem to figure out what I could offer you," she quietly admits, crease in her brow.

    "Is this about that ridiculous list?" Kara asks, and Lena considers the question seriously. Maybe, initially, the list would have been a deciding factor, but it's more than that.

    "Partly," she relents, sighing. "We're talking about more than just a moment here, right? We're talking about something that's expected to last, and I've spent so long avoiding this."

    Kara just looks confused. "Avoiding what?"

    "Forever."

    Kara leans forward just enough that the fluorescent lights catch her eyes at a different, devastating angle. "I'll be the first to tell you my life definitely hasn't gone the way I thought it would when I was graduating from college," she offers. "Professionally and personally, I could never have predicted I'd be a widow and a mother to a seven-year-old at twenty-seven. But, you know what that tells me?"

    "Life is unpredictable and I'm wasting my time trying to find some kind of certainty in the craziness of all of this?"

    Kara blinks. "I mean, yeah," she says. "That's way better than what I was going to say."

    Lena manages a smile. "What were you going to say?"

    "No, now it'll sound silly," she says, pouting slightly. "It wasn't nearly as profound as that."

    "Were you going to make a food analogy?"

    Kara glares at her for just a moment, before her face splits into her smile. Still, she sounds deeply serious when she says, "I was actually going to tell you that I don't care."

    "What?"

    "I don't care about the thing you think I care about," Kara says, just a little cryptic.

    Lena is predictably lost at her meaning until she sees Kara's eyes drift to check on Carter. Her son, who is not quite her son by blood. Kara doesn't care, which means she doesn't and won't care about Lena's inability to have children of her own.

    "Oh."

    Kara smiles, gentle and full of understanding. "Is that what you needed to hear?" she says.

    Lena feels heat rise up the back of her neck, reminded of Jack asking the same question of her. "From you, yes."

    "Good." Kara's eyes glint with something unspoken; almost like a twinkle in anticipation of things to come. "So, I'm going to ask again, Lena: what are you doing for the rest of your life?" she asks.

    Lena feels some of the tension dissipate from her shoulders. "Exactly this," she says. "If that's all right with you."

    Kara shrugs noncommittally, but her grin belies what she's feeling. "Meh," she says; "I suppose I could make do."

    Lena taps the tip of her right stiletto against one of Kara's shoes. "We're going to be okay, right?"

    Before Kara can reply, Carter comes running back to their table, practically collapsing into Kara's lap. His smile is so wide, and his giggles sound like actual music. He's adorably breathless as he says, "Pinball is a lot harder than I remember," but he's equally delighted by it all as he turns his megawatt grin on Lena. As if she's the one he wants to have hear him. As if he's not telling just Kara.

    Lena suddenly doesn't even care for Kara's response in the light of Carter's gaze. They could crash and burn, and rise like some kind of Phoenix. None of it would matter beyond Carter, and she suspects that's something on which she and Kara always agree.

    But it definitely helps when Kara firmly says, "We are," as she pulls Carter close and drops a kiss to the top of his head.

    Lena is choosing to believe her.

    Early Sunday morning, Kara makes two significant phone calls, one more difficult than the other, and then goes to get Carter ready to meet Alex for brunch. She definitely has a lot she needs to tell her sister, and not all of it is limited to her subsequent change in relationship status.

    She and Lena really are doing things in the wrong order. A remix, as it were. She grins to herself the second the thought crosses her mind, and she uses the small gap between brushing her teeth and tying her hair to text Lena about it. Because she can do that now. She's allowed to, so she's not going to pass up any opportunity. Lena's going to hear about all her strange thoughts in real-time from now on. It's what she's signed up for.

    Lena doesn't actually reply until she and Carter are seated at a table in the new café they're planning to try and waiting for Alex to arrive. Carter is reading the menu out loud to Kara when her phone buzzes in her pocket, and she retrieves it just as Carter is moving on to the baked goods the café has on offer. All freshly baked.

    Kara's already planning on getting one of the giant muffins she's spotted on several of the other tables. She just needs to pick a flavour.

    Does this mean we now need a duo name?

    Kara grins at Lena's response, feeling just a little ridiculous as she stares at the screen of her phone and wishes wishes Lena were here.

    Well.

    Alex first.

    Alex, who arrives wearing a bit of a scowl and sporting a severe black eye. Kara's own eyes widen at the sight of her, even as Carter squeals about how cool she looks.

    "Oh my God, what on Earth happened to you?" Kara asks, mouth hanging slightly open.

    Alex just grimaces as she slides into the seat beside Carter and automatically wraps an arm around his shoulders for a loose hug. Kara isn't even mad that she doesn't get a hug of her own when she sees the way Carter leans into his aunt, utterly content and comfortable with the affection. "It was a long shift," Alex explains. "I kind of got elbowed in the face by a patient."

    Kara blinks. "You look horrible."

    Alex looks predictably offended. "Oh, thank you, Kara," she snarks. "I'm really feeling the sympathy."

    "But totally badass," Kara adds after a moment, and Carter giggles. His little finger reaches up as if to poke at the bruise, but he stops himself just in time.

    Carter's hand drops into his lap. "Does it hurt?" he asks, voice small.

    Alex turns to face him, smiling reassuringly. "Not really," she tells him. "When it first happened, sure, but it's a lot better now."

    Kara experiences these kinds of moments quite often; moments when she almost wants to melt at the idea of Alex as a mother. It just - she would be so great. She's already so great, and Kara wants it for her.

    With Sam and Ruby, preferably.

    "I'm starving," Alex eventually says. "I want carbs and coffee. I deserve carbs and coffee."

    Kara will get her carbs and coffee, and she gets to her feet to go and peruse the pastries on display before she places their collective order and goes to retake her seat.

    In the meantime, Carter fills in Alex on what happened the day before, telling her about the animals and the ice-cream and Lena and the pizza and pinball.

    His various tales make Alex give Kara a very particular look, and she just knows her sister is going to tease her until they're both on their death beds. That's okay. Kara has a lot of her own ammunition.

    "So, you and Lena?" Alex finally asks, eyes focused on Kara, even as her hands curl around her coffee mug. "Is it actually happening?"

    Kara nods, forcing herself not to do something ridiculous and actually start blushing.

    "Mommy even kissed her after we walked her home," Carter says, and Kara can't even believe this little human being is Cat Grant's son. Is nothing sacred here? The kid can't even keep a secret.

    "Is that so?" Alex says, grinning like the cat caught the canary. "And in front of your son, no less? I must say I'm surprised, Kara."

    "She covered my eyes," Carter informs her, and there's really no point in Kara being here, really. They'd be just fine if she stayed at home or went to the studio.

    Alex's features soften when she looks at Kara again. "So, you guys talked?"

    Kara nods. "We'll probably need to keep talking, but we're finally headed in the same direction."

    "Yeah?"

    Kara nods, smiling at just the thought of Lena. "Yeah, Alex."

    "I'm glad," Alex says, and Kara can tell she means it, even if her expression appears pained. Kara knows she's thinking about Sam in this moment, and -

    Well.

    Kara is on a mission, you see, and she's enlisted Lena's help.

    Which is why, just nine minutes later, three familiar people enter the café and make their way over to the table. Alex has just stuffed a lot of pancake into her mouth when Ruby suddenly appears at her side, and Alex damn near chokes to death. The next minute is just chaos, Sam slapping her back and Kara split between making sure her sister doesn't actually die all while sneaking looks at Lena.

    Lena, who comes to stand near her, hand squeezing her shoulder and mouth saying nothing behind her smile.

    When Alex has settled and no longer looks as if she's going to combust, the six of them find their seats and Sam and Alex make the introductions. This is the first time Kara's meeting Ruby, but it seems Alex talks about them both enough to the other that it doesn't feel as if they're strangers.

    The problem is that Carter grows quiet at the new arrivals, and Kara is just about to engage with him to coax him into a sense of comfort when Lena beats her to it. She asks about his morning and about how his stuffed tiger is doing, and Kara -

    She just watches, her heart expanding in her chest as they lean towards each other, whispering like they've known each other for forever. A look across at where Sam is quietly fussing over Alex's black eye makes her heart want to explode.

    This is what her life could be, and she wants it. All of it. For as long as she'll be allowed. Hopefully forever.

    Lena looks over at her after a waiter has come by to take the rest of their order, and her hand reaches for Kara's under the table. Her fingers are warm as they interlock, and Kara holds their hands in her lap. Treating them as something precious.

    "I think your little plan is working," Lena whispers to her, leaning in just enough so only Kara can hear her. "Whatever you told Sam this morning seems to have helped."

    Kara turns her head, temporarily losing her breath at the sight of Lena's green eyes. "You're dying to know what I said, aren't you?"

    Lena huffs, and it's stupidly adorable. "Sam wouldn't tell me." She smiles, all innocence. "Will you tell me?"

    "I don't need to."

    "What? Why not?"

    "You already know what I told her."

    Lena looks stumped, and Kara gets the feeling she's a woman who doesn't particularly like not knowing things. "I know we're both aware I'm a genius but, after the last few days, I honestly have no idea what you could have said to Sam to give us that."

    That.

    Kara looks across to where Sam and Alex are leaning against each other, both a little flushed and actually looking giddy. Kara gets the feeling it has nothing to do with the coffee and carbs.

    Wow.

    If only Teenage-Alex could see herself now.

    "Just tell me," Lena says, and her breath is warm against Kara's cheek. "You know you want to."

    "Persuasive."

    "I have made grown men cry, I'll have you know."

    "I have no doubt."

    Lena head tilts a little, and Kara is so lost.

    No. She's just been found.

    Kara clears her throat. "Well, if you must know, I first told her that I forgive her for the letters," she says, and definitely doesn't miss the way Lena flinches. "Which I'm guessing you haven't yet."

    "I'll get there."

    Kara allows herself to give into the urge to press a kiss to Lena's cheek, making sure her son is occupied talking to Ruby about - wait, are they talking about Star Wars? Huh. He really is her son.

    Lena hums softly. "Distraction won't work, darling."

    Kara preens at the sound of that. There's really no other way to describe it. "I told her that I understand it more than she knows."

    "What?"

    "The fear," she says. "Juggling your own feelings while trying to make sure your kid is always happy. Finding someone you could be with and struggling to figure out if it could really work when there's more than just you to consider. Trying to balance your life around the people you care about most and just wanting everyone to get along. Not knowing if this other person would really stick around if they were let into every part of your life because, dear God, not all of it is sunshine and roses, and you need that person to be willing to be there for all of it and not run at the first sight of the ugly parts. Because a lot of it is hard, Lena, and I needed her to know that I get it. I get it. Which makes me qualified to tell her that Alex can be and is already that person for her. She doesn't even have to wonder about it."

    Lena hasn't looked away from her once.

    "I'd also really like us to be that person for each other, as well," Kara admits, and Lena leans in until their foreheads are touching. It feels both big and small, minimal and monumental, and Kara can't contain her smile - not that she tries.

    This is her life, and she'd be hard-pressed to change a single thing.

    §

    On Wednesday evening, when KX3P persists in calling, Lena is the one to answer the phone. She doesn't mean to, in the fact she didn't wake in the morning and set out to get put through to Nia and actually reveal whom she is.

    "It's L," she says, her socked feet tucked under her body as she sits on Kara's couch. She can hear gasps and giggles coming from Carter's bedroom, Lena essentially in Alex's position from merely a week earlier.

    She's convinced, if Kara had her way, she would never speak to KX3P herself again. The station could just rotate through the lot of them to learn all they need to about her and how her life is progressing. Carter can go next. Eliza. Even Sam.

    Nia practically squeals at the revelation, and Lena can't hold back her own smile. "Seriously?"

    "Seriously," Lena assures her, and whoever else is listening in the country. "She's currently putting Carter to bed. They're up to the showdown in the Chamber of Secrets today, and there was a coin-toss about who would read it with him. I won, so I said no to the spiders and the giant snake." She actually shudders at the thought of the Basilisk.

    Nia's voice is tinged with wonder when she says, "I don't even know what to say."

    "I am not a fan of arachnids," Lena informs her.

    "After last week, we weren't sure what to expect," Nia says. "She came to meet you?"

    Lena glances over her shoulder when sirens pass by Kara's building. This apartment is a lot noisier than her own penthouse and she loves every second of it. "Carter did, actually," she finally says. "Our little matchmaker."

    "Does that mean you're... matched?"

    "Something like that."

    Nia puffs out a breath, still sounding a lot in disbelief. "I still don't know what to say."

    Lena clears her throat. "Nia, I'm going to tell you a secret," she says. "You can't tell anyone, though." Her request is ridiculous, obviously, but Nia still agrees, because why wouldn't she?

    Kara emerges from Carter's room before Lena can speak again, and she feels a smile tug at her lips. Kara is beautiful like this. Well, she's always beautiful, but especially like this: soft and a little sleepy, hair in a messy bun atop her head and eyes impossibly blue behind her glasses.

    Always a sight for sore eyes.

    Lena pats the space beside her and Kara pads over to take her place where Lena would love her to remain for all of eternity: right next to her. She immediately feels warmer just having her close, their arms linking and Kara's head dropping onto her shoulder.

    "L?" Nia prompts in her ear, and Lena finds herself smiling once more. "Are you still there?"

    Kara peeks at her, mouthing the words, is that Nia?

    Lena nods for Kara, and says, "I'm still here," into the phone. "Not to sound cheesy and incredibly sappy, but I was momentarily distracted by just how beautiful she is."

    "Oh, my God," Kara mutters under her breath, mouth pressed to Lena's shoulder.

    "Aw," Nia says, and it sounds genuine on her lips. "That's the cutest thing."

    "She can be, indeed," Lena says, and Kara mumbles stop into her sweater. "Carter is, too, though."

    "How is he, with everything?" Nia asks. "Is he convinced he's found a new wife for his mother?"

    Lena hums softly. "I've already received my first proposal from him," she reveals. "Last night. It was pretty cute, practically screamed over his bowl of pasta."

    "I can't even handle this right now," Nia says, and Lena would have to agree with her. "Did you say yes?"

    Lena chuckles, and then gasps softly when Kara's one hand slides under her sweater, fingers against her skin. "I - no, I couldn't do that," Lena says. "His mother and I are just starting out, so I told him to ask me again next year."

    "I'll ask you before he does," Kara murmurs, and Lena doesn't think she's actually supposed to hear her. Kara just draws small shapes on Lena's skin with the tips of her fingers, and it's a bit of a struggle to focus on the conversation she's supposed to be having with Nia.

    "But you're going to say yes, aren't you?" Nia pushes, and it might just be some light teasing, but Lena is very serious when she answers.

    "I am," she confirms, her own eyes drifting to the far wall where Kara's just-finished painting of her sits on an easel. She's stared it for longer than she'd care to admit just this evening, completely fascinated and overwhelmed by the way Kara sees her.

    Warm.

    The painting is warm, and it makes her feel warm.

    Nia sputters a little, and Lena is immensely smug about being just another person capable of catching the radio host off guard. Kara and Alex don't get the monopoly on that.

    "I'm glad to hear that, L," Nia says, and there is something in her voice that lets Lena know she's telling the truth. Always so genuine. "Though, don't think I've forgotten that you mentioned you had a secret to tell us."

    "I wouldn't expect you to," Lena says, heart rate picking up for just a few beats before it settles once more. Steadies enough to match Kara's.

    "We're all ears," Nia prompts, offering her a soft landing pad.

    "I'm happy," Lena finally reveals, and no truer words have ever been uttered. "For what feels like the first time in my life, I'm well and truly happy."

    Kara lifts her head just enough to kiss Lena's cheek, and then uses her free arm to reach for the phone Lena has pressed against her opposite ear. She brings it up to her own ear and says, "We both are, Nia."

    Lena wouldn't be able to describe how it feels to hear those words out of Kara's mouth, and know that she means them. That's the kind of person she is, and Lena is choosing to believe her.

    "You have no idea how good it is to hear that.," Nia says, and she sounds so happy for them, as well. Relieved and just a little chuffed with herself.

    Despite what anyone might say, Nia and her KX3P team achieved what they set out to do: make Carter's wish come true, even if it's a few weeks late. Because Kara is definitely smiling a lot more.

    "If we ever get invited to the wedding," Nia says, signing off on this chapter of their lives; "then I'd tell you both it's what you deserve."

    Fin
